- **Engine** (redefined 2026-10-23, [D-321](ledger/patches-and-presets.md#D-321), #189): an assembly that takes keys and makes audio: keys → voicing → voices. `web/src/engines.ts` derives it from the contents (`isEngine`, `defIsEngine`, `instIsEngine`), never from names or `category`: a `keys` input whose keys reach a Voicing module (`alloc`, labelled Voicing, was Voice assigner) or a Drum map (`VOICING_TYPES`), following keys cables through an Arpeggiator or a Steps track's keys out, and at least one `audio` output. Factory: 19 (8 Lire, Algo Four, basic poly, Briefcase 3, Canvas, drift poly, drum kit, Elvira 2, Expander S, Hera 6, Io 4, mono lead, Monolith D, Oracle V, Sallen 20, Silver Box, supersaw stack, Vernon 100, Wanderer).
- **Voice**: pitch + gate in, sound out: one note (pluck, kalimba, operator). Engines are built from voices; Poly turns one into voice cards. ("voices" alone in the web app still means polyphony.)
- **Drum**: gate in, one hit (kick, snare). The drum kit, which takes keys, is an engine.

The assembly browser and the palette put the engines on an **Engines** shelf, first among the factory shelves (`ENGINE_SHELF`, `shelvesOf` in assemblies.ts, derived, not a `category`): an engine leaves Voices, and one from another shelf stays there too (the drum kit is on Engines and Drums). Rows carry a gold ENGINE tag; the palette tags engines `engine` ("engine" / "engines" filters to them, as "assembly" does). `Editor.insert` wires an engine in the same undo step (`wireEngine`): Main's `keys` into its keys input (Main's keys may feed several allocators), and, when no cable goes into the Output, its audio into `l` / `r` (stereo: the outputs labelled l and r, else the first two) or `l` (mono). A loaded patch with nothing into the Output says "Nothing makes sound yet. Add an engine, then play a key." In user-visible text "engine" no longer means the DSP runtime ("audio" in error lines); the code keeps `engine.ts`, `engine.c`, `ENG_*`.

## Sync, glide, shaper, spread

- `sync` (a gate input) on `osc` and `lfo`: a rising edge through 0.5 sets the phase to 0 before that sample's increment; the previous sample is kept in the state (`s1`). The oscillator honours it in both the plain and the FM path.
- `glide`: `y += k (x - y)` per sample with the ADSR's rate curve (`env_rate`, 99 % in `time`); the fixed-point state is Q24 because in Q16 the step rounds to nothing while the output is still about 1 % short, which on a pitch is audible and broke the golden comparison. With `fall` above 0 (#669, [D-772](ledger/modules.md#D-772)) a falling input takes `env_rate(fall)` (lin: the step of `fall`), a rising one `time`; `fall` 0 runs the loops as before.
- `shape`: `drive` (1..32, log) into soft (`1.5 x - x^3 / 2` to the knee at 1, then flat), hard (clamp) or fold (triangle of period 4, two's complement masking does the modulo in fixed point), then `level`. The peak is 1 in every mode. `sym` (-1..1) is added to the driven sample before the curve; `stages` (1..4) cascades the curve with `y = curve(2 y)` between stages (`shapeChain` / `shape_chain`), inside the oversampler when `os` is on. No state is added. Patch format 16 (two new parameters). Golden kit `wavefold` (fold, sym 0.3, three stages, oversampled, drive swept by an LFO): 2.0 % rel rms, the fold corners move with the sweep.
- `spread`: a per-voice panel output, `(2 v - (n - 1)) / (n - 1)` for voice v of n. Now the Part module's `index` (see Polyphony).
- Binary patch format 8: the two sync inputs moved `p1` / `p2` of osc and lfo, two module types were appended, the panel grew an output. `TYPE_COLOR` in `ui.c` has entries for the new types.


## Level/rate envelope, string, wavetable, chorus

- `env`: four levels and four times, Q30 state like the ADSR. A rising segment adds 2^30 / (r sr) per sample (so 0..1 takes `r`), a falling one approaches at the ADSR's `env_rate` and ends after `r` samples (the last 1 % is stepped so the next segment starts on time). Stages 0 (idle, tracks `l4`), 1..3, 4 (holds `l3`), 5 (release).
- `env8` (Env 8, the Casio CZ's eight-step envelope): eight levels and eight times, by `env`'s segment rules. Stages 0 (resting at the end step's level), 1..8 (the steps), 9 (holding the sustain step's level). A finished step goes to the hold when it is the sustain step and the gate is up, to rest when it is the end step, else to the next step. The gate's fall jumps from the hold, or from any step up to the sustain step, to the step after it (rest when the sustain is the end). `sus` off, or past `end`, runs every step through and ignores the gate's fall (Env L/R's one-shot). With 18 parameters its matrix and input-route masks in the binary are u32 (`maskBytes`, `eng_mask_bytes`; a type with 16 or fewer keeps u16), so pins move `sus` and `end` like any parameter. That makes `MAX_PAR` 18 and the firmware's node masks u32; the parameter values themselves take only their type's count from `par_pool` ([D-369](ledger/engine.md#D-369)). The firmware caches one step's rate divisions (`env8_rates`) and works them out again when the step changes or its rate moves. Golden kits `env8` (sustain mid-way, off, at the end; rates on an LFO) and `env8-poly` (four parts, the env flaw).
- `string`: a delay line of one period with a one-pole (`cl = 1 − 0.9 damp`) in the loop; per block from the block's first pitch: the period (clamped to 2..1102 samples), the loop gain `g = 2^(−log2(1000) · period / (decay · sr))`, and the fractional delay `period − (1 − cl) / cl` (the one-pole's low-frequency delay), read with linear interpolation. A rising gate loads a burst of `round(period)` samples. The loop is clamped to ±2. Firmware: int16 Q13 lines (`str_pool`, 8 × 1104 samples, 17 KB) written with first-order error feedback, because the loop would otherwise recirculate the rounding noise with gain 1 / (1 − g); golden `string` matches to 0.04 %.
- `wavetable`: `tools/gen_wavetables.py` defines 40 frames by their first 32 harmonics (cos / sin coefficients, normalized to a peak of 1), writes them as `web/src/wavetables.json` (the reference builds its tables from them at start) and as `firmware/src/wt_tables.h` (int16 Q15, 257 samples per frame, three mip levels of 32 / 16 / 8 harmonics, 61,680 bytes of flash). The mip is chosen per block from the first pitch (32 harmonics below sr / 64, 16 below sr / 32, else 8); frames are read with linear interpolation and crossfaded by `pos`. Phase modulation is the plain oscillator's (not oversampled). The fifth bank is EML COLOR: triangle, sloped square, square, saw and double saw an octave up. Its transition positions are interpolated, not calibrated to the original knob ([D-440](ledger/modules.md#D-440)).
- `chorus`: shared like the delay (`SHARED_TYPES`, `T_CHORUS` in the firmware's voice split); a 927-sample Q13 line, two taps `12 ms ± 8 ms · depth · sin`, the right one a quarter cycle behind, linear interpolation. One instance per patch (`ch_pool`).
- Pool budget: see "Voices and the shared pools" (the numbers of this batch were superseded by the merge with the four-voice firmware).

## Sequencer, clock divider, S&H, quantizer, pulse

- All five act on rising edges through 0.5 (Q16 32768), like `sync`. `seq`: ten parameters (the first module above eight, hence the u16 masks of format 9); `out` is `s[pos]` every sample, so the step's knob is live; the random direction uses the LCG of the LFO's S&H. `clkdiv` counts edges modulo 16 · div (a common multiple of div, 2, 4, 8) and starts at −1 so the first edge is a downbeat. `quant`: semitones = 12 · in − root, octave by floor division, the nearest of the scale's degrees or the next root; ties go down (the two engines can differ at an exact float tie, which modulation never hits). `pulse`: a sample countdown of round(time · sr).
- Golden `seq-clock` (clock -> divider -> pendulum sequencer -> quantizer -> oscillator, sequencer gate -> pulse -> ADSR, d4 -> sync) and `sh-random` (clocked S&H on noise -> pentatonic quantizer, a tracking S&H on the filter, a random sequencer on pulse width; 2 voices) pass at 1.5 % and 0.2 %: the edge decisions line up sample for sample.
## Pan, crossfade, vowel, phaser, digitizer, noise colour

- `pan`: l = in·cos((p+1)π/4), r = in·sin((p+1)π/4) with p = clamp(pan + pos, −1, 1), per sample (C: sin_turn at (p+1)/8 of a turn).
- `xfade`: out = a·(1−m) + b·m, m = clamp(mix + pos, 0, 1).
- `vowel`: three SVF stages (the normalized band output k·v1, peak 1) at the formant frequencies of A (730, 1090, 2440), E (530, 1840, 2480), I (390, 1990, 2550), O (570, 840, 2410), U (440, 1020, 2240) Hz with gains 1, ½, ¼; the position v = clamp(vowel + input[0], 0, 4) interpolates the frequencies linearly between neighbours once per block; k = 1/Q, Q = 2 + 18·res. The C version reuses svf_g / svf_stage and Q20 states like the filter.
- `phaser`: four first-order allpasses y = a(x − y₁) + x₁ with one coefficient a = (g−1)/(g+1), g = tan(πf/sr), f = center·2^(2·depth·tri), tri a triangle LFO at `rate` read once per block; the input of stage 1 is in + fb·(stage 4's previous output), clamped to ±8; out = in·(1−mix) + wet·mix. In C the coefficient is Q24 from one 64-bit division per block.
- `crush`: step 2^(1−bits), y = floor(x/step + ½)·step on the taken sample, held for `rate` samples (C: shift by 17 − bits).
- `noise` `color`: y += c·(white − y), c = 2^(−5·color) (color 0 is c = 1: the old noise bit for bit); the state is Q24 in C.
- Golden kits: `pan-xfade` (3 voices), `vowel-phaser`, `crush-color`.
## Assemblies: merged ports, placement, nesting

- Merged ports: `PortRef.also` (inputs only) lists the further inner inputs a container jack feeds; `targetsOf` / `findRef` resolve them, `mergePorts` / `unmergePort` edit them, `hidePort` treats a group as one and refuses while any target has an outside cable. Cables stay per inner input: the editor creates one per target when a cable lands on the jack (`dot.dataset.also`) and removes the hidden siblings when the drawn one is clicked. Dedupe on the canvas is free: only the primary's dot exists while the assembly is collapsed.
- Rows that set several parameters (#357): `ParamRef.also` lists the further parameters a card row sets; each has the
  same range as the row's first (`sameRange`: min, max, top, step, curve, unit, choices), none is a Voice allocator's or
  another row's. `rowTargets` / `rowOf` resolve them, `mergeRows` / `splitRow` edit them (pick mode's "one row with…"
  and split), `demote` takes one parameter off (the row's first hands the row to the next), `cleanRows` (in `reconcile`)
  drops what no longer fits. The parameters are tied: `ties(p)` / `tiedTo` gives a parameter's tied set across nested
  cards (a parent's row on a child's row ties what the child's row sets). The editor writes a value to all of them
  (`Editor.mirror`, after `setParam` and a slider; the FM-1's PARAM_NOW comes through `setParam` too), a cable dropped on
  the row reaches each (`connectParameter` per parameter; removing it takes the same output's cables into the others,
  `tiedCables`), and `syncTied` (after every `change`, and on load in `fixAssemblies`) makes each follow the row's
  first: its value, p1 / p2 routing, macro range, the pin's depth where the pin routes to it alone, side-input routes.
  Card presets (`applyPreset`) set every tied parameter; the morph needs nothing (both sides keep them equal). The
  [**structure fingerprint**](ledger/vocabulary.md#structure-fingerprint) counts a row's parameters joined with `+`, so a card without such rows keeps its fingerprint.
- Named positions (#358): `ParamRef.names` (only on a Selector module's `pos` row; `ROW_NAMES_MAX` 8 names of
  `ROW_NAME_MAX` 12 characters, `fixNames`) turns the row into a row of named choices: the collapsed card, the Selector's
  own card inside the frame (`rowNames`, through a parent's row too), the patch card, the dock's dials and the FM-1 page.
  `rowRange` (modules.ts) limits a `pos` row to 1..count everywhere a row is edited (slider, typed value, the dials,
  CC); the engines already clamp. Pick mode's "names" button sets them (`setRowNames`).
- Copies shown one at a time ([D-347](ledger/patches-and-presets.md#D-347) amended: the CZ edits one envelope step at a time): `ParamRef.copies` = `{ rows,
  count, name }` on a run's first row, layout only like a heading. `copyRuns` finds the runs that hold together (no
  heading, column break or other run inside, all rows on the card; `keepParams` drops the flag when a row of the run
  goes). The collapsed card shows one copy under a picker row named `name` ("step"; `Editor.copyPicker`, the choice is
  editor state, not saved); every copy, and the row after the run, starts a group (`copyStarts` into `groupStarts` and
  Arrange's `pagesOf`), so each copy starts a new FM-1 page and ALGO steps through its pages. A copy has up to 16 rows
  ([D-639](ledger/editor.md#D-639), Decided), so one operator picker can select all fourteen Algo Four controls. Arrange mode's "⧉ one at a time"
  sets it (`setCopies`). Presets, the morph, cables and the binary see every row.
- Placement: `freeSpot(occupied, w, h)` scans `GRID` cells in reading order against the rectangles of the visible cards, containers and frames (the editor measures the DOM, or the model before the first layout).
- Nesting: `AssemblyInst.children` (ids), `allModules` / `descendants` / `parentOf` / `depthOf`, `MAX_DEPTH` 3. `boundary` and `outsideCables` use all modules; a parent's `inputs` / `outputs` / `params` may point at a descendant's module only while the direct child exposes / promotes it (`reconcile` enforces, so `reconcileAll` goes children first and loops until stable). `makeAssembly` nests when the selection is inside one assembly; `addChild` / `removeChild` move an assembly (the editor calls them after a container drag, judged by the card header). `ungroup` lifts members one level, `deleteAssembly` removes the assembly with everything inside it (nested assemblies and all their modules, #560). Saved files gain `children` and `assemblies` (NestedDef with local ids); `validateAssemblyDef` checks the tree, `fixAssemblies` repairs a loaded overlay (missing children, two parents, loops, depth).

## Unison

- `osc` parameters `unison` (1..5) and `detune` (0..100 cents, default 10): in the plain path (no pm, no feedback) the oscillator runs n phases, voice j detuned by detune·(2j/(n−1) − 1) cents (−detune .. +detune, symmetric), and outputs their sum / n (the peak stays at `level`; detuned copies beat). A sync edge restarts every phase. The FM path ignores unison (one voice). C: the extra phases are `inst_t.s.osc.ph[4]` (the union is sized by the delay's record, so nothing grew), one `exp2_scale` per voice per sample, sum × 1/n from a table. Binary patch format 11 (the oscillator record has 9 parameters). Golden kit `unison` (5 + 2 voices): 1.3 % rel rms (the detuned saw edges fall on different samples in float and Q16, as in the other saw kits).

## Frequency shifter

- `shift`: single sideband. A Hilbert pair (Olli Niemitalo's two chains of four second-order allpasses y = a²(x + y₂) − x₂, coefficients `HILBERT_A` / `HILBERT_B`; chain A's output delayed by one sample is I, chain B's is Q) and a quadrature oscillator at `shift` + cv·cvamt Hz (per block from the block mean of cv): up = I·cos + Q·sin, down = I·cos − Q·sin (the sign convention of this pair; verified by the test: a 440 Hz sine shifted by 100 comes out at 540 on `up`); each output is in·(1 − mix) + shifted·mix. Below about 20 Hz the pair's phase error lets a little of the other sideband through, as with any such design. C: a² in Q30 (`HILB_A2` / `HILB_B2`), each section in the two-state form w = x + a²w₂, y = a²w − w₂ (18 words of state), signals Q16, `sin_turn` for the oscillator, the increment from Hz as (hz << 16) / sr in u32 turns (a negative shift wraps). Golden kit `shift` (saw, LFO on cv): 0.4 % rel rms.

## MIDI performance controls

Main provides `bend` (−1..1), `mod`, `at`, `breath`, `sustain` and `foot` (0..1). Its `pitch` output already includes pitch bend at ±2 semitones. CC 1 drives `mod`, channel aftertouch drives `at`, and CC 2 / 64 / 65 drive `breath` / `sustain` / `foot`. Each control keeps its last value until another message changes it. Sustain and foot are patchable signals; the patch decides how to use them ([D-470](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-470), Proposed). CC 16..19 edit the focused device page ([D-453](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-453)).

The browser parses these messages in `input.ts`, and `Engine.bend`, `cc` and `aftertouch` store one set of values for every voice. Firmware dispatch in `midi_input.h` respects MENU > MIDI CHANNEL for these controls. `eng_ctl` stores their values and `m_fm1` writes the outputs. The new outputs are appended after the panel-button outputs, preserving existing port numbers. `MAX_OUT` is 19; module parameter records are unchanged.

## Extended oscillator waveforms

Oscillator adds sharktooth (the equal mix of its triangle and saw) and reverse saw (the negated saw). These are ideal waveforms after the Model D's triangle/saw hybrid and reverse saw, described in the [manufacturer's manual, oscillator bank](https://www.suonoelettronico.com/downloads/minimoog_manuale.pdf). They use the same edge smoothing, sync, unison and phase-modulation paths as the existing waves. The new choices use `top: 5` with the existing `max: 3`; old parameter routing and morphs retain their original range ([D-435](ledger/modules.md#D-435), Proposed).

## Audio-rate Ladder cutoff

Ladder's `fm` input adds a sample-by-sample cutoff signal to its existing `cv` input, whose value is averaged over the block. Both use `cvamt` in octaves. Coefficients are reused while the sample value stays the same. The new input follows `p1` and `p2`, so old patches keep their cable numbers, parameter records and sound ([D-434](ledger/modules.md#D-434), Proposed).

## Comb filter and modal resonator

- `comb`: the string's line (one period of pitch + coarse, 40 Hz lowest, linear interpolation, the damping one-pole's delay taken off the line) with signed feedback `fb` (−0.98..0.98) and no burst: y = clamp(in + fb·lp(y[n − period]), ±2), out = in·(1 − mix) + y·mix. C: `it->s.str` (buf, w, lp, err with the error feedback) and a string-pool line, so `ENG_NSTRING` is shared by strings and combs.
- `modal`: six two-pole resonators y = a·sinθ·x + 2r·cosθ·y₁ − r²·y₂ (impulse response rⁿ·sin(θn): a unit impulse rings at amplitude 1), partial i at ratio (i+1) + struct·(bar_i − (i+1)) with the free bar's ratios 1, 2.756, 5.404, 8.933, 13.34, 18.64; r = 2^(−log2(1000)/(decay_i·sr)) with decay_i = decay/(1 + 2·damp·i); amplitudes (0.3 + 0.7·bright)^i normalised to a sum of 1; partials at or above 0.45 sr are left out; states held to ±8. Coefficients once per block from the block's first pitch. C: g, c1, c2 in Q30, states Q20, rounded products; cos θ must be accurate near 1 (a Q16 cosine moved the pole by 1 % at middle C), so it is 1 − 2·sin²(θ/2) from a Q30 sine lookup (`sin_turn30`); r from `exp2_scale(1 << 30, e)`. Golden kits `comb` (saw, fb −0.85) 0.35 % and `modal` (gated noise burst) 2 % rel rms (the decay's table error shows up late in the ring).

## Phase distortion oscillator (`pd`, issue #351)

- After the Casio CZ ([D-431](ledger/modules.md#D-431)). A ramp u (0..1 per cycle of the note) reads a sine at the bent phase u + dcw·B(u). B is the bend at full DCW (the CZ's "digitally controlled wave"), the bent phase minus the ramp, straight between sixteenths of the cycle (`PD_SEGS` in modules.ts): saw (slope −3/7 to 7/8, then 3), square (±1, holding the sine at its peak and trough), pulse (7 to 1/8, then −1: held), double sine (the saw's bend in each half), saw-pulse (1/7 to 7/16, 7 to 1/2, then held). dcw = the parameter + the `dcw` input, clamped 0..1, per sample.
- Resonance I, II, III: a sine at 2^(dcw·`range`) times the note, restarted at each cycle start (at u times the ratio, so the restart lands on the ramp's phase), times a window of u: 1 − u, 1 − |2u − 1|, min(4u, 2 − 2u, 1). The ratio is worked out at the block's first and last sample (from dcw there) and runs in a straight line between, so a moving dcw costs no exp2 per sample.
- `wave2` (0 off) plays on the odd cycles; the cycle count runs whether it is on or not, and sync (a rising edge) restarts the cycle at the first waveform.
- Band limit, once a block from the first pitch: room = (sr / 4) / f. A bend's dcw is held to (room − 1) / steepness (steepness 3, 1, 7, 3, 7: the bent phase runs at most 1 + that times as fast as the ramp), so the bent sine stays under a quarter of the rate; the resonance's rise stops at log2(room) octaves.
- C: phase u32. The bend from 16-entry tables (`PD_T` start in Q16 cycles, `PD_S` slope in Q16): k = u >> 28, b = T[k] + S[k]·(u & 2^28 − 1) / 2^32 (one high multiply, no search), dcw·b as a u32 product (Q32, modulo a cycle), then `sin_turn`. The resonance ratio in Q16 from `exp2_q16`, its increment dt·m >> 16. A fast path (one bend wave, nothing at sync or pm) keeps the loop short. The reference reads the same bends from `PD_SEGS` in float. Golden kits `pd-bends` 0.15 %, `pd-alt` 0.4 %, `pd-reso` (an envelope on dcw, sync, pm) 2.4 % rel rms; the resonance's error is the restart landing one sample apart where the two engines' pitches differ by a hair.
- Cost on the rv32 model (`cpu-costs.json`), a block of 128 samples: about 10.6 k cycles for one bend wave (1.8 % of the FM-1's block with its parameter inputs), about 15 k with a second waveform or a resonance, 17 k with sync or pm patched, and a little more while the pitch moves (vibrato: the increment's exp2 runs once per 8 samples, as on the Oscillator). Cassia 1 XL built a line from about 60 modules, about 450 k cycles per voice; a `pd` with its dcw input does it in 12 k to 25 k, which made Cassia 1, built on it, fit the FM-1; it plays two voices there ([D-348](ledger/patches-and-presets.md#D-348)).

## Granular

- `grain` (shared like the delay: one instance per patch, `SHARED_TYPES`): records `in` into a 1 s line (`GRAIN_SECONDS`; int16 Q13 on the device, `grain_line_t` = 88,392 B with the eight grain records, in the effect memory, [D-375](ledger/engine.md#D-375): as many granulars as it holds). `density` grains per second start from a Q24 accumulator (the reference quantizes its increment to 2^-24 too, so both engines spawn on the same samples), a rising edge on `trig` starts one as well; at most `GRAIN_MAX` 8 play at once, a ninth is dropped. A grain reads `size` s (len samples) at rate 2^(p), p = the `pitch` input's first sample in octaves (appended after p1 / p2, so the Main module's pitch plays it; C4 = the recording's pitch) + the `pitch` slider / 12 (not snapped to whole semitones), clamped to ±`GRAIN_OCT` 4 octaves ([D-761](ledger/modules.md#D-761)); it reads from start = w − back, back = span + min(avail, floor(position·avail) + floor(rnd·spray·0.25 s)), span = floor(len·rate) + 1 (clamped to the line), avail = LEN − 1 − span, rnd from the LCG (seed 0x7f4a7c15; drawn even when no grain is free); so a grain never crosses the write head. Window sin²(πt/len) (C: `sin_turn` over half a turn, `winc` = 2^31/len), linear interpolation, the grains summed unnormalised; out = in·(1 − mix) + grains·mix. `freeze` stops the recording (w stays; the grains keep reading). With spray 0 and a frozen line every grain reads the same segment, so periodic content can cancel between grains: use spray. Golden kit `grain` (gated saw, LFO trig, pitch +7, spray 0.3): 0.5 % rel rms; `grain-pitch` (the keyboard on the pitch input, slider +12): 0.03 %.

## Key-press trigger

Voicing and Part append `trig`: one sample at one when a key press reaches their allocator, then zero. The allocator's channel and note range still filter incoming keys. The pulse is independent of which pitch wins the priority rule. Releases produce no pulse. Several presses arriving in one block share its pulse. Idle parts retain the existing skip rule; running parts receive the pulse ([D-438](ledger/modules.md#D-438), Proposed).

Wanderer uses legato pitch selection and subtracts this pulse from the held gate. Its envelopes restart for a middle key press, but do not restart when an outer key is released. Existing gate outputs are unchanged in other patches.

## Math and logic

- Math's appended `xor` output applies exclusive OR to signed 16-bit audio samples. It first applies the gains and offset, multiplies by 32768, rounds down and clamps to −32768..32767. The result is divided by 32768. A zero mask preserves the quantized input. Elvira 2 XL uses this for BITMANGLER; its original mask sequence remains unverified ([D-437](ledger/modules.md#D-437), Proposed).
- `math`: a' = a·ka + off, b' = b·kb; outputs add, sub, mul, min, max, abs (of a'), cmp (1 while a' > b'). `logic`: inputs high at or above `thresh`; outputs and, or, xor, not (of a). Both stateless, per sample, Q16 in C. Golden kit `math-logic` (xor of two square LFOs gates an ADSR; max / abs of the math module on a VCA and a pitch).

## Shift register and comparator (#438)

- `shreg` (Shift register, after the Turing Machine and the Rungler; [D-755](ledger/modules.md#D-755), [D-756](ledger/modules.md#D-756)): inputs `clock`, `in`, `reset`; outputs `s1`..`s8`, `dac`, `gate`; parameters `len` (1..8), `flip` (0..1), `bits` (1..8). The eight stages are one byte, `s1` in bit 7. A rising `clock` (through 0.5) shifts right and puts the new bit in bit 7: `in` ≥ 0.5 when `in` is patched, else bit 8 − `len` (stage `len`) comes round. Each shift draws the next value of an LCG flip stream (`lcg`, as the Random module's) and flips the new bit when its top 16 bits are below `flip` in Q16. The starting pattern is `hash32(instSeed ^ SHREG_PAT_SEED) >> 24` (0xA5 when that is 0), the flip stream starts at `hash32(instSeed ^ SHREG_FLIP_SEED)`; a rising `reset` restores both and wins over a clock on the same sample. `dac` = the top `bits` bits as v, (v · 65536 + (2^bits − 1) / 2) / (2^bits − 1) in Q16 (`shregDac`, `shreg_dac`), worked out at the block start and at each edge only. `gate` = clock high and `s1`. Integer in both engines, so they agree bit for bit apart from where a clock edge falls. C: between edges the stages and `dac` are filled, and only outputs something reads are written; with neither `clock` nor `reset` patched it is a fill (989 cycles; clocked, 6,546 cycles, about 1.0 % of the block, the CPU model at defaults). Golden kits `shreg-loop` (a locked 5-step loop's dac into a pitch, its gate), `shreg-flip` (flip 0.5, a 3-bit dac, reset by the keyboard's gate), `shreg-data` (`in` patched, `flip` and `bits` through p1).
- `comp` (Comparator; [D-757](ledger/modules.md#D-757)): inputs `a`, `b`; outputs `gt`, `lt`, `trig`; parameters `thresh` (−1..1), `hyst` (0..0.5). d = a − b − thresh; `gt` rises when d > hyst and falls when d < −hyst, keeping its state in between (at hyst 0, at d = 0 exactly); `lt` is its inverse, `trig` 1 on the sample `gt` rises. Q16 in C, per sample (4,415 cycles, about 0.7 % of the block). Golden kits `comp-hyst` (a sine against a slow triangle with hysteresis), `comp-thresh` (a saw against a threshold moved through p1).

## Modulation scaling (#526)

- **Depth up to ±2** ([D-293](ledger/patch-format.md#D-293), [D-955](ledger/editor.md#D-955)): `ModuleInst.att` runs over −2..2 (`DEPTH_MAX`, patch.ts `fixMatrix`); the engines were never limited (C `mul16(mean, att)`, every firmware back to 1.4.1 reads the i32 unclamped), so a depth past 1 keeps the patch's format. The editor (`web/src/depth.ts`, the routing menu in `parameter-menu.ts`) shows and reads it in the parameter's units: `depthScale` (octaves = log2(max / min) for a logarithmic parameter, max − min in its unit for a straight one, steps for a choice, % of the slider for the bent cutoff), `depthText` (three significant figures), `parseDepth` (a number or a fraction, the unit or a related one, % of the range; clamped to ±2, rounded to Q16, `storedDepth`).
- **Bipolar macro ranges** ([D-294](ledger/patch-format.md#D-294), [D-956](ledger/editor.md#D-956)): `Range` gets an optional third entry 1 (`[lo, hi, 1]`): the pin's −1..1 spans lo..hi. Binary: bit 7 of the pin byte in the macro range section (`RANGE_BIPOLAR`); `uses19` writes format 49 for one (older firmware skips an entry whose pin reads past 1). C `range_t.bi`; the live RANGE command's on byte has bit 1 for it (`rangeFrame`, proto.c `C_RANGE`, `m1_patch_range` keeps the bit in the stored copy). The editor sets it when "Play between two values" is turned on, from the source's output range (`swingsBoth`: ranges.ts `outputRange` starts below 0), and "Source swings −1..1" changes it. Golden kit `range-bipolar`; `depth-2` has depths of 1.9 and −1.75.
- `range` (Range; [D-758](ledger/modules.md#D-758), [D-759](ledger/modules.md#D-759)): input `in`, output `out`; parameters `inlo`, `inhi`, `outlo`, `outhi` (−8..8, `RANGE_LIMIT`; as made −1, 1, 0, 1), `curve` (−1..1), `clamp` (off as made). t = (in − inlo) / (inhi − inlo) (0 when they are equal), held to 0..1 with clamp, else to ±64 (`RANGE_T_MAX`); out = outlo + f(t) × (outhi − outlo), f = `rangeCurve`: t + c·t·(t − 1) inside 0..1, straight on with the end's slope outside. C `m_range`: 1 / (inhi − inlo) once a block as a Q16 reciprocal (`(1 << 32) / span`), per sample one 64-bit multiply for t, `range_curve` (two `mul16`) when curve is not 0, one for the output. Cost: 6,141 cycles a block at curve 0, 7,933 with a curve (about 0.9 % and 1.2 % of the block, CPU model). Carries `ModuleDef.since` 49 ([D-292](ledger/patch-format.md#D-292)). Golden kits `range-curve` (unclamped with a curve; clamped, inverted, logarithmic), `range-pitch` (into a pitch, its curve through p1); `mod-scaling.test.ts`.

## Matrix (#525)

- `matrix` (Matrix; [D-760](ledger/modules.md#D-760), the pin matrix of #438): inputs `in1`..`in4`, outputs `out1`..`out4`, 16 parameters `i<in>o<out>` (−1..1, 0 as made), output by output (`i1o1`..`i4o1`, then out 2's: `matrixPin`, `MATRIX_N`). out o = Σ in i × pin (i, o), the pins at block rate as a Mixer's levels; no through input, so a bypassed Matrix is silent. C `m_matrix`: only outputs off `dummy_out`, and of each only the inputs patched (`nd->conn`) with a pin off 0, gathered once a block into a loop of one, two, or three or four `mul16` a sample (an unpatched or zero pin costs nothing). Carries `ModuleDef.since` 49. Cost (CPU model, `TypeCost.matrix`, `cpu.ts` `matrixCost`, measured by `cpu-costs.ts` `matrixCost` instead of a grid of its 16 pins): 1,030 cycles with one output read and no pin in use, 388 more per further output read, and per output 1,344 / 2,511 / 4,193 / 4,972 cycles for 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 pins in use (a pin in use: its input patched and its amount off 0, or moved by a cable). A four-way router (one pin per output) costs about 1.1 % of the block, all 16 pins about 3.3 %. Golden kits `matrix-audio` (two oscillators and a noise into four outputs, pins of both signs, an output read twice, two pins moved through p1) and `matrix-cv` (Main's pitch, an LFO and an envelope routed to a pitch and a cutoff); `matrix.test.ts`.
- **The FM-1's grid page** ([D-915](ledger/device-ui.md#D-915)): a plain Matrix device's pages are its outputs, OUT 1..OUT 4, each its own section (`pages.ts` `moduleSections` puts a heading `out N` before every fourth row; `pages_ui.c` `pages_plain` names the pages and makes each part 0 of 1), so the header shows the output and a dash per output. `page_grid` finds a page whose four rows are one Matrix output's pins, in1..in4 in order (also an assembly's); its bars are then grid rows (`bar_t.grid`, `amt`, `cur`; `grid_row`): bar k is input k, `IN1`..`IN4` from defs.h, four cells of 26 px from x 44 (`MX_X0`, `MX_CW`, `MX_GAP`), each the strip's colour (the page's column: the track's, framed in ivory) with a faint middle line and a fill from the middle to the amount in the knob's colours, and the framed cell's value right. No live tick. A grid row whose cells, column or value changed is redrawn whole (`bar_txt` hashes them into `pg_drawn.txt`; `pages_ticks` checks it too, for a pin set from the editor). panel_test loads the `matrix-audio` fixture and checks the four pages, the turns, ALGO and an editor change against the screen drawn whole; `PATCHFILE=build/panel-fixtures/matrix-audio.m1p tools/ui_preview.sh OUT.png 0 5:1` draws OUT 2.

## Selector (#351)

- `select` (Selector): a stepped switch of eight positions. k = clamp(floor(pos + cv·(count − 1) + ½), 1, count), cv = the `pos` input's first sample of the block (0..1 spans 1..count, so a 0..1 row read in N steps is one cable). `out` = in_k + v_k (with nothing patched in, a table of eight values); gate g_k is 1, the other seven 0. `fade` (0..100 ms) crossfades: every input's weight (Q16) moves towards 1 (chosen) or 0 by inc = floor(65536 / samples) per sample, samples = floor(fade_Q16 · 2891317 / 2³²) (2891317 = (44118 << 16) / 1000; `selectFadeInc`), the same integers in both engines; a change during a fade keeps fading from where the weights are. Settled, it copies one input; the gates are written only where cabled (C: `dummy_out`; the browser engine: the module context's `used`, the outputs with a cable or read by a probe or watch, which marks them from the next block).
- Eleven inputs (`pos`, in1..in8, p1, p2): `MAX_IN` became 11 and `node_t.conn` 16 bits wide (an input past the eighth has its bit). That is 3.3 KB more of the pool (`tools/pool_sizes.sh`: 128 instances × five more input pointers, 64 nodes × five more side-route masks).
- CPU: 2494 cycles alone (0.4 % of the block), 640 more per gate cabled, about 30 per sample more while fading. `apply_cv` skips the side-route loop when no input has one (bit-identical: `tools/engine_exact.sh`); with eleven inputs and eleven parameters it cost the Selector 1300 cycles a block.
- Golden kit `select` (three voices): a stepped LFO on a's pos with a 4 ms fade, a value table moved by p1 that transposes an oscillator with its gates on VCAs, an instant switch on a triangle.

## Envelope follower and compressor

- `dyn`: inputs `in`, `side`; outputs `out`, `env`. The detector is a one-pole peak follower of |side| (|in| when side is not connected) with the ADSR's rate formula (99 % in `attack` up, `release` down); it rides a little under the peak of a steady tone and ripples at the signal's frequency. Gain above `thresh` (dB): 2^(makeup − slope·(log2 env − log2 thresh)), slope = 1 − 1/ratio; below it only the makeup `gain` (dB). C: env Q24, rates Q30 (`env_rate`), dB → octaves by 1/6.0206 in Q16, one `log2_q16` + `exp2_q16` per sample while above the threshold (`exp2_q16` gives a plain integer: the Q16 / Q24 offsets are added to its argument). Golden kit `dyn` (gated saw into the compressor, its env output on a second VCA): 0.3 % rel rms.
- `esp` (Follower, patch format 46), after the Korg MS-20's External Signal Processor (its owner's manual, 1978, https://archive.org/details/JL11275: the ESP's controls SIGNAL LEVEL, LOW CUT FREQ, HIGH CUT FREQ, CV ADJUST, THRESHOLD LEVEL; its outputs amplifier, band pass, F-V CV, ENV and TRIG). Input `in`; outputs `amp` (in × `level`, held to ±16), `bp`, `pitch`, `env`, `trig`. `env`: a one-pole follower of |amp| (3 ms up, 80 ms down, the ADSR's rate formula); `trig` rises above `thresh` and falls below ¾ of it. `bp`: a one-pole high pass at `lowcut`, then a one-pole low pass at `highcut`. `pitch`: bp's period between rising crossings of +h after it went below −h (h = a quarter of bp's peak hold, which falls 1/512 per sample), each crossing placed between its two samples, as log2(f / C4) octaves plus `cvadj` semitones, gliding 1/64 of the way per sample. It holds the last pitch while bp's peak is under 1/256 (no pitch from silence) and drops a measurement after 4096 samples without a crossing. Nothing on `in`: it rests and costs a fill (the FM-1 has no audio input, #274, so on the FM-1 it hears only signals made in the patch). C: every state Q24, the one-poles' coefficients from `env_rate_q8` (the corner as the time 4.6 / 2πf), one 32-bit division per crossing and one `log2_q16` per period. Sallen 20 has one: `esp signal in` is its input, summed with its SIGNAL IN row (a Selector: OFF, VCO 1, white noise, the MG's triangle), its ESP ROUTE row (a Selector whose gates open a VCA or a Logic and per route: F-V into VCO 2's pitch, ENV into the VCA's cv, TRIG into the EGs' trigger Logic; [D-370](ledger/engine.md#D-370)) works on the FM-1, the TRIG route's loop through EG 1 and VCO 1 takes its block of delay on the route's own cable (its Logic is the assembly's first module, so `processingOrder` enters the loop there; golden kits `sallen-esp-2..4`), and the jacks the manual patches its outputs into are inputs too (VCO 1+2 CV IN, TRIGGER IN through a Logic or with the keyboard's gate, INITIAL GAIN). Golden kits `esp-pitch` (a played saw: pitch and env, 0.03 % rel rms) and `esp-follow` (the manual's patch: 0.14 %).

## Mobile: zoom, touch, long press

- `Editor.zoom` scales one `#patch-world` containing the modules, external cables and drag preview (origin 0 0). `#canvas-space` tracks the scaled extent while the canvas remains a bounded scrolling viewport. Module-local SVG layers draw internal routes above the card surface and below its controls; the external SVG sits behind cards. Routing menus use top-layer popovers, positioned by `popover.ts` to stay on screen. Cable endpoints are measured relative to their layer’s origin, including when the canvas scrolls; card positions and rubber-band hits stay in layout pixels (`offsetLeft`), so only pointer deltas (`dragOn`), cable endpoints (`center`, the pending cable) and the rubber band's `at()` divide by the zoom. `setZoom` clamps to 0.3 .. 2, `zoomToFit` uses the cards' extent. Gestures (`touchGestures`): two touch pointers on the canvas pinch, Ctrl / Cmd + wheel zooms (trackpads); one touch pointer on empty canvas pans (`rubberBand` takes that path for `pointerType === 'touch'`). A touch press of 500 ms without 8 px of movement on a card header opens `contextMenu` (bypass, duplicate, select, remove) instead of a drag. CSS uses coarse-pointer targets and a full-height canvas beside a 208 px sidebar. Below 700 px, the sidebar is a dismissible drawer; its open state makes the canvas inert. A 44 px mobile strip provides Workspace and Commands buttons. Desktop has no header or patch toolbar.

## Share links

- `web/src/share.ts`: `encodeShare` = JSON → `CompressionStream('deflate-raw')` → base64url (no padding); over `LINK_MAX` (8000 characters: a Reddit comment, GitHub's issue-form prefill) the full patch is stored on the site and the link is `#s=<id>` (below); without the layout (unticked, or the site unreachable) only the positions are dropped (the assemblies are structure: poly racks, device pages), and `Editor.untangle` tidies the pile it opens as (`tidy.ts isPile`; `arrange` packs a pile freely, without reading columns). `decodeShare` inflates and runs `validatePatch`. `shareCodeOf(url)` reads `p=` from the fragment, `shareLink(code)` builds the URL on the page's own address (so a Pages deployment makes stable links). main.ts loads the fragment at start and on `hashchange` with `suppressSave` set (the remembered patch stays until the user acts) and clears the fragment with `replaceState`; the Patches page imports a pasted link into the bank list. The binary form was not used: it has no layout, and the deflated JSON of every factory patch is under 1 KB (`share.test.ts`).
- **Stored links:** `web/src/share-store.ts` (no imports; bundled by Cloudflare Pages Functions) + `web/functions/api/patch.ts` (POST a code: `{ id }`) and `functions/api/patch/[id].ts` (GET: the code, cached immutable). KV namespace bound as `PATCHES` in `web/wrangler.toml` (keys `p:<id>`); `public/_routes.json` sends only `/api/*` to functions. The id is the first 12 base64url characters of the code's SHA-256: one id per patch, never overwritten (409 on a collision). The server keeps only codes up to `STORE_MAX` (400,000 characters) that inflate (capped at 4 MB) to a JSON object with version, modules and cables; the app runs `validatePatch` on open. Client: `encodeShare` sets `stores`, `handOut` stores only at Copy / Post / Submit (`storeShare`), falling back to the `#p=` link without the layout while that fits; `openShareLink` reads both forms (main.ts `loadFromHash`). Dev: `npm run api` (wrangler pages dev on 8788, local KV) behind Vite's `/api` proxy. The deploy runs `wrangler pages deploy` from `web/` so it picks up wrangler.toml and functions/.

## Community patches (#157, 2026-10-21)

- `web/src/community.json` is a curated list bundled with the app; `web/src/community.ts` reads it. No live fetching (the app reads only the JSON; the subreddit search is a plain link), so later GitHub or Reddit sources can feed the same entry shape. Entries are third-party data under CC BY-SA 4.0 (LICENSING.md), not GPL code.
- Entry: `{ title, author, licence: "CC-BY-SA-4.0", source (https URL), code (a share link's #p= value), added (YYYY-MM-DD), notes? }`. `entryProblems` names what is wrong; `communityEntries` keeps the good ones, newest first. `loadCommunity` decodes them once (`decodeShare`, so `validatePatch` runs) and caches; `communityPatches()` is that cache, read synchronously. Decoding starts when Library > Community first shows, or at page load when entries were added.
- Where they show: opt-in. **Library > Community** (`library-community.ts`, `#community`) has one card per family (`familiesOf`: an entry "<X>" and its "<X> XL" by name, the FM-1 version first), with the author, the age, the notes' first line, the Source link, and a row per version with its chip and Add / Added ✓; filters ALL / FM-1 / SANDBOX / ADDED; links to the issue form and the subreddit. Add stores the title in `matryoshka.community-added` (`addCommunity`); `addedCommunity()` is those entries, decoded. `openableGroups()` (catalog.ts) lists them in Your patches with their authors (the Open picker, the palette, the emulated PRESET), and bank.ts lists them in Library > Patches' browser column, where Remove un-adds one with Undo. An added entry loads by decoding the bundled entry until a patch of that name is saved (main.ts calls `unlinkSaved` on every save), which makes it the user's own copy. The palette's "Browse community patches" opens the section; the share sheet's Submit to the Community shelf reaches the form.
- Submissions: `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/community-patch.yml` (label `community-patch`; fields `patch-title`, `link`, `reddit`, `author`, `notes`, a required CC BY-SA 4.0 checkbox). `submitUrl(patch, link)` builds the form's URL with those fields filled in (GitHub fills form fields from query parameters named after their ids). An accepted submission is added to the JSON in a commit; `community.test.ts` decodes every entry.

## Diff, A/B, shortcuts, palette

- `web/src/diff.ts`: `diffPatches(a, b, defs)` walks the modules by id (parameters, bypass, attenuverters, matrix, ranges, morph side) and the cables by endpoint, plus voices, arpeggiator and name; `diffLines` / `diffCount` for the panel (`#diff`, main.ts `showDiff`). The baseline is the JSON of the patch at the last `setPatch` (preset, file, palette), upload or store.
- A/B ([D-645](ledger/editor.md#D-645)): `Editor.snapshots` (JSON strings, not saved), `takeSnapshot`, `toggleAB` → `applyLive(target)` → `flipPlan` (`web/src/ab.ts`). The plan is the target with the current canvas fields (module positions, assembly positions / `open` / `frameSize` / `size` by id, `view`, `octave`, `slackFrom`, `zoomFrom`) and, when the two patches differ only in `params`, `att` (absent = `[1, 1]`), `bypass`, `range` and Steps `notes` (pattern 1), one live message per difference, which `applyLive` sends as `onParam` / `onAtt` / `onBypass` / `onRange` / `onTrack` (the browser engine, the emulator and the FM-1 with Live on update in place, no upload), then `onLayout`. Any other difference (structure, ids, `map`, `inputMap`, `paramsB`, Steps name and patterns, Drum map names, Main's `panel` and `seq`, the assembly overlay apart from its geometry, `bypass`, `kept` and `preset`, or a field the plan does not know) is a full load through `onStructure`. A gesture and a REC pass end first; the side being left is re-snapshotted, then the flip is one undo step tagged with that side. `flipAB` (main.ts) puts the target's name in the name box. UI: three palette rows, Alt+B (matched by `e.code`, since Option+B types ∫ on a Mac), the help card row, Snapshot B among the morph picker's candidates.
- Shortcuts (main.ts, a capturing keydown listener before the note keyboard's; off while a field has focus): palette, `?`, Esc (cancel an armed jack, close the diff), Delete, B, G (only with a selection, else it is a note), Ctrl/Cmd+D, arrows, Alt+B, Space. Tab retains normal focus navigation; sidebar tabs use Left/Right/Home/End. Editor actions: `removeSelected`, `bypassSelected`, `nudgeSelected`, `duplicateSelected` (copies params, att, map, range, bypass and the cables among the copies), `cancelArm`. Click-to-connect: a pointer released on the jack it started on arms it (`.armed`); the next `beginDrag` on a jack of the other kind connects (merged jacks included).
- Palette (`<dialog id="palette">`): modules, factory/saved assemblies, patches, file actions, history, selection actions, snapshots, hardware settings, Flash/About and help. Empty search shows actions; filter buttons search Modules, Assemblies and Patches. `commands.ts` searches every query word across names, tags, shelves and descriptions, without truncating results. The input is a combobox with an active-descendant listbox; arrows select and Enter runs. Opening/cancelling preserves the current page; editing actions bring the patch into view. FM-1/MIDI configuration uses its own modal dialog.

## Scope

- The floating scope window and shift-click probes are gone (2026-10-18); the Scope module is the only scope. `Engine.probe(id, port)` / `probeVoices` stay: the worklet's Scope views (`view` / `unview`, scope-stream.ts) and the sidebar slider watches read outputs through them.
- `web/src/scope.ts` holds only the FFT (`fft`, `spectrum`) behind the Scope module's spectrum view (scope-view.ts); `scope.test.ts` checks its calibration. `worklet.test.ts` checks views across every factory patch and a polyphonic load, levels without cancellation, patch resets and per-voice rings.

## Arpeggiator module, tempo and transport (#58, 2026-10-18; replaces the firmware arpeggiator)

- **Arpeggiator module** (`arp`, appended to `ALL`; `web/src/arpeggiator.ts` is the note logic, `engine.c` `arp_*` the same in C): keys in, keys out, between the Main module and a Voice allocator (`Main keys -> Arpeggiator keys -> allocator keys`). Params: mode (up, down, up-down without repeating the ends, random (LCG `seed * 1664525 + 1013904223`, index `(seed >> 16) % len`), as played), rate (1/4, 1/4T, 1/8, 1/8T, 1/16, 1/16T, 1/32 = 24, 16, 12, 8, 6, 4, 3 ticks of 24 ppqn), octaves 1..4, gate 0.1..1 (a share of the last step's length; the first step's length from the tempo), latch (a new chord after all keys were let go replaces the held one; turning latch off drops the keys already let go). Input `clk`: with a cable, one step per block with a rising edge there (the input as the last block left it); without, its own rate on the engine's clock. No p1 / p2 (`PIN_EXEMPT`), runs once (`SHARED_TYPES`), at most 2 per patch on the FM-1 (`ENG_MAX_ARPS`, `DEVICE.arps`).
- It runs at the start of every block, before the modules: block-quantized (a step lands on a block boundary, up to 2.9 ms late). Its notes go to the groups it plays (`group_on` / `group_off`); a part it lands on gets `retrig` (envelopes restart, also at gate 100 % where the next note follows in the same block). The keys reach the groups whose source is the keys and every arpeggiator.
- **Bypassed, fed by a track, into Drum maps (2026-10-23, [D-425](ledger/modules.md#D-425)):** a bypassed arpeggiator plays nothing of its own (`arp_block` / `arpBlock` skipped) and hands the keys it hears straight on: its groups and Drum maps take them as its own source's (`engine.c grp_src` / `dm_src`, `engine.ts effSrc` / `passesMain`: Main's keys, or the track's notes). It keeps the held keys, so switching it on arpeggiates what is held; a switch (bypass or its Toggle: `arp_switched` / `arpSwitched`) lets go of its groups' parts and its Drum maps' rows. An arpeggiator whose `keys` in is cabled from a Steps track's `keys` out plays that track's notes, not Main's keys (`node_t.ksrc` = 2 + the track's index; `ArpRun.feed`; `deviceView` keeps that cable in the device form). Its notes also go into the Drum maps it feeds (their ksrc = 2 + the arpeggiator's index; `deviceView` keeps the arpeggiator → Drum map cable so the compile pass keeps the arpeggiator). The factory patches use all three: Main → PATTERN → a bypassed arpeggiator on ARP → the voice ([D-336](ledger/patches-and-presets.md#D-336)). In the [grouped order](ledger/vocabulary.md#grouped-order) a device a track plays through an arpeggiator counts as driven by it, and a patch never opens on an arpeggiator (`homeDevice`, `pages_ui.c pages_frame`). The CPU estimate charges a Steps track Main's keys pass through `types.steps.thru` cycles per key (as many keys as the largest poly group has parts; `cpu-costs.ts thruCost`). ARP as the Toggle of Arpeggiators lights while they play ([D-231](ledger/device-ui.md#D-231): `engine.c eng_buttons_lit` for the LEDs, the bar's ARP ON / OFF and the EMU view, `buttons.ts arpLitInverted` for the dock); the engine's button bit stays "engaged = bypassed". `eng_keys_drummap` (the black keys on a Drum map) skips an arpeggiator a track feeds: the track, through it, decides.
- **Device form:** `poly.ts keySource` / `arpOf`; `deviceView` drops every keys cable, keeps the arpeggiator in the module list, gives an arpeggiated allocator a group even with one voice, and sets `DeviceGroup.src`. Binary format **23** (always written; `ENG_PATCH_COMPAT_VERSION` 23): the group record ends with its note source (`GROUP_REC` / `ENG_GROUP_REC` = 45 bytes: 0 the keys, else 1 + the arpeggiator's module index).
- **Continuous Arpeggiator rate:** the appended `rate` input is in hertz. Its previous block's average overrides the tempo-based note length. Positive values clamp to 0.02..80 Hz; zero or negative values pause and release the sounding note. A cable in `clk` takes precedence. Both engines accumulate unsigned 32-bit turns with the same quantized increment, so timing stays within one block without accumulating block-rounding error. Gate length follows the current period. The CPU probe counts note scheduling as well as the module's signal output ([D-439](ledger/modules.md#D-439), Proposed).
- **Tempo and clock:** Main parameter `tempo` (20..300 bpm, whole bpm, default 120). The engine's clock (`Engine.ticks`, `E.ticks`) always runs: per block `acc += n x bpm x 24`, a tick per `60 x sr`; while MIDI clock comes in (`clockExternal` / `eng_clock_external`; the worklet and `midi_drain` turn it off after 0.5 s without a tick) its ticks replace the tempo. **Transport:** `Engine.transport` / `eng_transport` (PLAY on the FM-1, the deck's Play and Space in the browser, MIDI Start / Continue / Stop, protocol TRANSPORT 0x25 with INFO feature bit 1); a new Main output `run` is high while it runs; the Main `clock` output pulses (half a block per block that saw a tick) only while it runs. Arpeggiators step whether or not it runs.
- **The global tempo** ([D-907](ledger/device-ui.md#D-907)): GLO > PANEL > TEMPO is GLOBAL (as made) or PATCH. While GLOBAL every patch plays at one tempo that a load never changes; Main's `tempo` stays in each patch, unused. Engines: `Engine.tempoGlobal` (worklet message `tempo`; `parameterValues` reports it as Main's tempo) and `eng_tempo_global` (engine.c `tempo_glo`: `eng_load` puts it into Main's tempo parameter and keeps the patch's own in `tempo_own`, which comes back with `eng_tempo_global(0)`; `eng_set_param` / `eng_knob_param` on Main's tempo move it, `eng_tempo_moved` says so; `eng_tempo_row` tells `m1_patch_param` and a device preset load to leave the patch's own). Firmware glue in `tempo_glo.h`: the setting, the settings record's bytes 5 and 7 (the tempo's low byte; bit 7 the mark, bit 1 PATCH, bit 0 the tempo's bit 8; a record without the mark, from older firmware, reads as GLOBAL at the boot patch's tempo, `ENG_TEMPO_ADOPT`), and `gt_poll`, which writes the settings record 5 s after the last change once the transport is stopped (main.c `tempo_poll`, no SAVE). Protocol: TEMPO 0x38 (mode, bpm u16) and TEMPO_NOW 0x55 (after INFO, and unasked on a change), INFO byte 12 (after the firmware version) feature bit 16. Editor: `web/src/tempo.ts` (the setting; `localStorage['matryoshka.tempo']`), FM-1 & MIDI > Tempo, Main's tempo row labelled `global tempo` (`Editor.globalTempoRow`), the knobs (`setParam`, `shownParam`), the FM-1 tab (`fm1w_tempo`, import `tempo`). Older firmware gets the global tempo as Main's PARAM under Live.
- **MIDI clock out** (`web/src/clock-out.ts`, FM-1 & MIDI > MIDI clock out, [D-466](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-466)): while on, the ticked Web MIDI outputs get clock (0xF8, 24 a quarter note at the tempo that plays: the global tempo, or Main's `tempo` while TEMPO is PATCH; read at every clock) all the time, and Start / Stop (0xFA / 0xFC) when the transport switches. Each clock is sent `LOOKAHEAD` (300 ms) ahead with its own timestamp; the timer is a worker's (`clock-tick.ts`, 25 ms) so a background tab keeps its pace, and a timer over 1 s late restarts the clock from now. The browser engine keeps its own tempo clock: it does not follow the clock it sends.
- **Old patches:** `Patch.arp` (the firmware arpeggiator's settings) is dropped by `validatePatch`; its bpm becomes the Main module's tempo when the patch had none. `arp.ts`, `arp.c`, `arp_ui.c`, the arp pages (ARP / SEQ / LFO buttons now unused, for #60), the ARP command 0x24, `tools/arp_test.sh` and the dock's arpeggiator panel are gone; the dock's clock column has the clock and run jacks and the tempo; Play (`#arp-btn`, the tutorial target) is PLAY in the dock's button block since #113. The saved-state record keeps its arp bytes as zeros.
- Golden kits `arp-updown-poly`, `arp-random-latch`, `arp-clk-played`, `main-clock-run` (events `run` / `stop` in `.ev`, `host_test` calls `eng_transport`).

## Macro ranges

- Model: `ModuleInst.range` = `{ p1?: { [param]: [lo, hi] }, p2?: ... }`, ends 0..1 in `paramUnit` space (octaves for a log parameter, no slider bend; `paramValue` is the inverse: the same pair the morph uses). `fixMatrix` keeps a range only on parameters the pin controls (`map`) and clamps the ends; `rangeOf(m, pin, name)`.
- Engines: in applyCv / apply_cv a pin with a range on the parameter sets the base to `paramValue(lo + clamp(v, 0, 1) × (hi − lo))` with v = block mean × attenuverter (a bipolar range, format 49: v = (block mean × attenuverter + 1) / 2, `rangeShare`; C `(mean + ENG_ONE) >> 1`; [D-294](ledger/patch-format.md#D-294)) (replacing the slider's value and the morph), the other pin adds as before (`modulate`, which for a log parameter adds in the bent unit space exactly as it always did, so patches without ranges are bit-identical). C: `range_t E.ranges[ENG_MAX_RANGES]` (48 per voice: module, pin, parameter, lo, hi as u8) and `node_t.ranged[2]` bit masks so the table is only scanned for a ranged destination; `morph_value` maps the u8 ends (× 257 → Q16).
- Binary format 15: header flag bit 1 = a macro range section after the morph B set: u8 count, then 5 bytes per range (module index, pin, parameter index, lo, hi as 0..255). The section is last, so a live edit on the device (`m1_patch_range`: update, append, or remove by moving the last entry into the hole) never shifts the records. Protocol RANGE 0x23 (module, pin, param, on, lo, hi); `eng_set_range` / `eng_range`.
- LCD: `module_row_b` draws a bracket (ticks at both ends, a line between them) over the bar of a knob row whose parameter has a range on p1 or p2.
- Golden kit `macro` (a triangle LFO into the filter's and the ADSR's p1 with ranges in octaves, the ends of the decay swapped; K2 into the oscillator's pulse width): 1.6 % rel rms.

## Patch morphing

- Model: `ModuleInst.paramsB` (the B side's parameters per module, never on the Main module), `Patch.morphName` (display). `sameStructure` (same module types in order, same cables by module index), `setMorphTarget` / `clearMorph` / `hasMorph`, `morphValue(pd, a, b, t)` (choices and toggles snap at 0.5; log parameters interpolate in octaves without the slider's bend; the rest linearly). The Main module gained the parameter `morph` (0..1) and the control input `morph`.
- Engines: the morph position of a block is the Main's `morph` parameter plus the block mean of its morph input, which holds what the input summed in the previous block (one block late, so the LFO can be anywhere in the processing order), clamped to 0..1 (`Engine.morphPosition`, `morph_position` in engine.c). applyCv / apply_cv start from the morphed base instead of the parameter when the patch has a B side and the position is above 0; the modulation then adds as before. The firmware keeps the B side as unit positions (`node_t.parb`, u16 per parameter, octaves for a log parameter: `morph_unit` / `morph_value`), 24 B per node. A cable into the Main's input never makes the Main per voice (`voiceSplit`, and in C the panel already has nv instances); T_GRAIN was missing from the C shared list and is there now.
- Binary format 14: header word 6 (was reserved) bit 0 = a B set follows the cables, one i32 Q16 per parameter of every module in record order (the Main's values are written but ignored). `m1_valid` accounts for it; records keep their offsets, so PARAM / ATT / BYPASS edits are unchanged. Protocol: the morph position is the Main's PARAM (no new command); CC 22 in on the device.
- Device: see [D-453](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-453).
- Golden kit `morph`: a subtractive voice with a brighter square B side, an LFO into the Main's morph input, morph 0.3: 2.4 % rel rms (the unit-space rounding of the B side shows in the resonant filter).

## Reverb (FDN)

- `reverb` is an eight-line feedback delay network: line lengths 331 439 547 661 773 881 991 1117 samples (primes, 7.5..25 ms at 44118 Hz; the TS engine scales them with the sample rate), Householder feedback f_k = t_k − Σt/4 (lossless mixing, so the decay is set by the per-line gains alone), in every line a one-pole damping filter (coefficient damp × 0.8) and a gain g_k = 10^(−3 L_k / (T·sr)) so that every line loses 60 dB in T = 0.2 × 2^(5·size) s (0.2 .. 6.4 s). Input: pre-delay (0..40 ms, `pre`), two diffusion allpasses (223, 347 samples, g 0.5), × 0.125 into every line; wet = Σ raw line outputs × 0.5. Lines 0 and 1 are read with a fractional, linearly interpolated delay that swings by `mod` × 6 samples (sine at 0.5 and 0.83 Hz): the chorusing that keeps fixed lines from ringing. Measured in the test: a sustained noise of 0.5 rms gives a wet level of 0.13 (size 0) .. 0.29 (size 1); the tail of size 0.5 falls 53 dB/s. Memory: 8074 int16 samples = 16 KB per instance (`RV_LEN`; was 12 KB), in the effect memory ([D-375](ledger/engine.md#D-375)). C: Q13 lines as before, T and the gains through `exp2_q16`, the modulation through `sin_turn`; golden kit `reverb` 0.1 % rel rms (the C and TS modulators agree to a fraction of a sample over the whole tail).

## Oversampling toggle (shaper, digitizer) and sequencer glide / gate

- `shape` and `crush` have an `os` toggle (patch format 13: new parameters). With it the module runs at 2x: the FM oscillator's polyphase half-band (the same four allpass sections, Q16 coefficients `HB` / `HB_A`) is used as the interpolator (even sub-sample = path 0, odd = path 1, no gain change: the zero-stuffed signal's half-band has gain 2) and again as the decimator, path 1 fed the odd sub-sample of the previous frame as in the oscillator (`os2` in modules.ts; `os_up0` / `os_up1` / `os_down` in engine.c, 17 words of state). Measured in the test: a 5 kHz sine hard-clipped at drive 4: the 5th and 7th harmonics, which fold to 23 and 13 kHz at 48 kHz, drop by 11..12 dB with `os` (what is left is the folding of still higher harmonics at the doubled rate); the fundamental and the 3rd harmonic are unchanged, the rms is 9 % lower (the energy above 22 kHz is gone rather than folded). The digitizer's `rate` keeps counting input samples (the counter runs at 2 x rate). Golden kit `os-shape-crush` (saw into a hard clip into 5 bits / rate 3, both oversampled): 1.2 % rel rms.
- `seq` parameters `glide` and `gate` (fractions, 0..1): the clock period is measured between the last two rising edges (`since` / `period`, nothing until two edges were seen), `glide` is a one-pole on `out` reaching 99 % in glide × period (Q24 state, `env_rate_q8`; 0 = the step itself, bit-exact as before), `gate` holds the gate output for round(gate × period) samples after an edge that lands on a non-rest step (1 = legato; 0 = the clock's own width as before). MAX_PAR is 12. Golden kit `seq-glide`: 0.8 % rel rms.

## Filter: notch, peak, allpass outputs

- `filter` outputs `notch`, `peak`, `ap` appended after `mix` (positions are append-only, so the binary patch format is unchanged). From stage 1's values (v0 = in, v1 = bp, v2 = lp12, k = 2 − 1.98·res): notch = v0 − k·v1 (= lp12 + hp), peak = v2 − hp (= lp12 − hp: unity far from the cutoff, 2/k at it), allpass = v0 − 2k·v1 (= notch − k·bp, |H| = 1). The C version computes them only when one of the three outputs has a cable (`dummy_out` check once per block). Golden kit `filter-notch` (saw, sine LFO on cv, the three outputs mixed): 0.2 % rel rms.
- `filter` output `hp6` (patch format 45), appended after `ap`: hp + 2·bp = (s² + 2s) / (s² + k·s + 1), the high pass of the Korg MS-20's filter as T. E. Stinchcombe derives it ("A Study of the Korg MS10 & MS20 Filters", section 7): 6 dB per octave below the cutoff, √5 / k at it, unity above. The C version computes it in the same branch as notch / peak / allpass. Sallen 20 takes its high pass from it. Golden kits `filter-hp6-0.2` and `filter-hp6-0.95`.
- `filter` parameter `osc` (patch format 47, appended, off by default): self-oscillation, as the Korg MS-20's filters at full PEAK. On, from `res` 0.85 (`FILTER_OSC_FROM`) up, stage 1's damping is k = 2 − 1.98·res − 1.19·d + 5.6·d·b² (d = res − 0.85, b = the band-pass state ic1 held to ±2): below 0 from about res 0.95, it grows with the level, so the filter rings a bounded, nearly sine tone at the cutoff (a Van der Pol oscillator: about 0.5 peak on lp12 at res 1, at any cutoff; the pitch within 1 % of the cutoff up to a few kHz). A filter at rest starts from ic1 = 1/1024. The C version (`filter_run` with osc a constant at each call, as `ladder_run`) solves stage 1 per sample there (`svf_stage`, one division); off, or below 0.85, the filter is the same code as before. Sallen 20 turns it on in both filters ([D-371](ledger/engine.md#D-371)). Golden kits `filter-osc-self` (no input, res 1) and `filter-osc-driven` (a saw, res 0.97, the cutoff swept).

- `filter` parameter `drive` (#670, patch format 51, appended; 0, its default, is off): a soft clip inside the resonance loop, as the Korg MS-20's diodes clip. The resonance feedback r·bp (r = 1.98·res) passes the Ladder's sat at drive times its level; read from the band-pass state ic1 as the self-oscillation reads it, what the clip takes off comes back as damping: k grows by r·(1 − sat(a)/a), a = drive·r·|ic1| held to 64 (`filterDriveLoss`: a²/6.75 below the knee 1.5, 1 − 1/a above it). It is the prototype the owner heard (input × drive, the loop clipped, every output ÷ drive) with the scaling folded into the clip's level, so a quiet signal plays as with drive off and nothing is multiplied per output. With `osc` on, the self-oscillation's term still reads the unscaled state, so the drive also lowers the self-oscillation's level (the clip limits it first). The C version (`filter_run` with drv a constant at each call, from `m_filter`: drive above 0 and res above 0) solves stage 1 per sample, as with osc (`svf_stage`, one division, and one more above the knee); off, or without resonance, it is the same code as before. Golden kits `filter-drive`, `-quiet`, `-max`, `-osc`, `-nores`. CPU (`npm run cpu-costs`, cycles per block, one instance): off 25,748 → 25,753; on 41,573 (+15,820, about 68 µs or 2.3 % of the block), with `cv` patched 53,619 → 61,824 (the coefficients follow the cv per sample there anyway).

## Ladder filter

- `ladder`: four TPT one-poles (y = G(x − s) + s, s' = y + v with v = G(x − s); G = g/(1+g), g = tan(πfc/sr)) in a loop with k = 4·res. Zero-delay feedback solved linearly per sample: y4 = (G⁴x + S)·F with S = (G³s₁ + G²s₂ + Gs₃ + s₄)(1 − G) and F = 1/(1 + kG⁴); the loop input is then u = sat(drive·x − k·y4) and runs through the stages (the saturator is inside the loop, so at res 1 the self-oscillation limits itself). sat(v) = v − v³/6.75 for |v| ≤ 1.5, ±1 beyond (slope 1 at 0, peak 1). Outputs lp24 (stage 4) and lp12 (stage 2). Parameters cutoff (log, 20..4000 Hz), res, drive (1..8, log), cvamt, track. The coefficients are computed once per block from the block mean of `cv` (× cvamt, + key tracking), unlike the SVF's per-sample update: one `recip24` per block instead of one per sample. No resonance compensation (the passband drops with res, as on the original). C: G, G²..G⁴, F in Q24, states Q20, products rounded; the saturator bounds the states so they need no clamps. Golden kit `ladder` (saw, LFO on cv, res 0.85, drive 3): 0.12 % rel rms.
- `ladder` parameter `stsat` (#672, patch format 51, appended, off by default): the same sat at the input of stages 2, 3 and 4 too, as each transistor pair of a Moog ladder saturates (the linear loop solve is unchanged). C: `ladder_run` with stsat a constant at each call, so off is the same code as before. Golden kits `ladder-stsat`, `-max`, `-clean`, `-fm`. The stage saturators work in 32-bit products (`lad_sat_st`, within 2⁻¹⁴ of `lad_sat`): half the cycles of three more `lad_sat`. CPU (cycles per block, one instance): off 22,719 → 22,733; on 28,110 (+5,377, about 23 µs or 0.8 % of the block).
- `ladder` parameter `diode` (#671, patch format 51, appended, off by default): the diode ladder of the Roland TB-303 and the EMS VCS3 (`diodeLadder`, C `diode_run`). Four coupled stages, y' = ω(−M·y + e₁·u) with M tri-diagonal [2 −1; −1 2 −1; −1 2 −1; −1 1] (characteristic polynomial s⁴ + 7s³ + 15s² + 10s + 1): about 14 dB per octave around the cutoff, 18 an octave higher, 24 far above. The phase reaches −180° at ω·1.1953, where |H| = 1/18.39, so k = 18.39·res (res 1 is the edge, as the Moog mode's 4·res) and g = tan(π·fc/sr)/1.1953 (fc held to 0.3·sr): `cutoff` is the resonance frequency, as in the Moog mode. Trapezoidal integration: each sample solves (I + g·M)·y = z + g·e₁·u by the tri-diagonal (Thomas) elimination, its pivots' reciprocals iu₀..iu₃ and multipliers m₁..m₃ = g·iu worked out once per block (five `recip24`) or at each change of `fm`; the loop is solved linearly (y₄ = p₃ + b₃·u), then u = sat((drive·x − k·p₃)/(1 + k·b₃)). No high pass in the feedback: inside the loop the passband falls by 1/(1 + k) with res (−25.8 dB at res 1), the diode ladder's bass loss; the outputs are multiplied by (1 + k)/(1 + 4·res) after the loop (`diodeMakeup`, C `mk` in Q24 once per block), so the passband sits where the Moog mode's does and the switch keeps a patch's level ([D-388](ledger/engine.md#D-388)). `stsat` does not apply in this mode. The four states are the Moog mode's. Golden kits `ladder-diode-0`, `-0.5`, `-0.9`, `-drive`, `-fm`, `-self`. CPU (cycles per block, one instance): 32,721 against the Moog mode's 22,733 (+9,988, about 43 µs or 1.5 % of the block; the makeup takes 3,636 of them).

## Voices and the shared pools (merge of the four-voice firmware 1.0.2 with polyphony and the module batches)

**Superseded (#59, 2026-10-18):** the four-voice build was removed. The firmware runs one patch (`eng_one`, `#define E eng_one`); `ENG_VOICES`, `ENG_VOICE_INIT`, `eng_voice_select` and the `_v` calls, SEL, the per-voice mixer rows, mute keys, MIDI channels and colours are gone; the mixer level is `patch_gain` in audio.c; one MIDI channel (`cfg.ch`). The pools keep their owner arrays (a load frees every slot). The saved-state record still has four patch slots (`BLOB_NV`) and the firmware reads the first of a record the four-voice firmware wrote. The text below is the history.

Upstream's 1.0 line made the FM-1 four-timbral: four independent patches, which the panel, the manual and the saved state call **voices** (SEL cycles them, each has its own MIDI channel, arpeggiator, mixer level and accent colour; `eng_t EV[ENG_VOICES]`, `eng_voice_select`). This branch had meanwhile given a patch **polyphony** (then `Patch.voices`; since format 20 poly assemblies, see Polyphony). Both are kept: the firmware runs four patches, each of which may be polyphonic. In the firmware the two are told apart as voices (patches) and parts (`pv_t`, `grp_t`, `ENG_MAX_PARTS`, `eng_parts`); the web app has one patch and says *parts* for polyphony.

See [D-202](ledger/device-ui.md#D-202) and [D-256](ledger/patch-format.md#D-256).

**Ports.** The Main module's outputs were then `pitch gate vel k1..k4 clock spread` (`clock`: upstream, 24 ppqn from the arpeggiator or MIDI clock; since format 20 `spread` is the Part module's `index`). The LFO's inputs are `clk` and `reset` (upstream's names; this branch's restart input was called `sync`, which is now the LFO's tempo-sync *parameter*; `validatePatch` renames cables into an LFO `sync` input to `reset`, the factory assemblies were regenerated). The oscillator and the wavetable keep their `sync` input. Binary patch format 10.

See [D-220](ledger/device-ui.md#D-220).

See [D-202](ledger/device-ui.md#D-202).

## Bypass (module on / off)

Every module except Main and Output has a bypass switch, the way a device in a DAW rack can be turned off. A bypassed module does not run (its state is kept, so it resumes where it stopped when turned on again) and its outputs carry its **through input**: `ModuleDef.thru`, by default the input called `in` (filter, ladder, delay, reverb, chorus, shaper, digitizer, phaser, vowel, glide, comb, modal, shifter, compressor, granular, S&H, quantizer); the mixer passes `in1`, the crossfade `a`, the pan copies `in` to both `l` and `r`. A module without a through input (oscillators, LFOs, envelopes, noise, sequencer, string, math, logic, pulse, clock divider) is muted: silence on every output. Input sums still run, so a bypassed module inside a feedback loop keeps the graph consistent.

- Model: `ModuleInst.bypass` (patch.ts, dropped on Main / Output by `validatePatch`), `thruInput(def)`. Binary format 12: bit 7 of a record's type byte (the record size is unchanged); `ENG_PATCH_VERSION` 12.
- Engines: `Engine.run` copies or clears the outputs (`Engine.setBypass` for the live switch, worklet message `bypass`); engine.c `m_bypass` instead of `process()` (`node_t.bypass`, `eng_set_bypass` / `eng_bypass`, `def_t.thru` from export-defs).
- Protocol: `22 BYPASS module u8, on u8` (protocol.ts, proto.c `m1_patch_bypass` keeps the stored copy in step so SAVE / STORE keep it).
- Editor: the ⏻ button in the card header (the body dims, undo works); `EditorEvents.onBypass`. An assembly's ⏻ bypasses its members (see **Assemblies** below).
- FM-1: a patch button of the Toggle kind bypasses its module or assembly ([D-213](ledger/device-ui.md#D-213)); FX is the SEQ page's accent key and SEL held the roll's pen ([D-223](ledger/device-ui.md#D-223)).

## Parameter ranges and steps (audit)

- The editor's logarithmic sliders rounded their value to a whole number (`Math.round` after `fromUnit`), so a glide time, envelope rate, chorus or phaser rate, drive, grain size or decay could only take 0, 1, 2 ... They now move by the parameter's `step` from the value they hold, a tenth of one with Shift, and a typed value is kept as typed (`stepToward` and `parseValue` in value-input.ts, [D-953](ledger/editor.md#D-953), [D-954](ledger/editor.md#D-954)); the LCD knobs were not affected (`eng_step_param` works in octave units).
- Filter and Ladder `cutoff` open to 16 kHz (was 4 kHz; the tan table allows 0.45 of the sample rate). The modulation range is 9.6 octaves now instead of 7.6, so a parameter input on the cutoff moves it a bit further per unit; the Pluck preset's K1 start was recomputed.
- LFO `rate` (0.02..40 Hz) and the ADSR's `a`, `d`, `r` are logarithmic like the Env L/R rates and Glide: the slider and the knobs give the short times room, modulation moves them in ratios.
- The Delay's `time` plays up to its `length` (10..3800 ms, `DELAY_LENGTH_MIN/MAX`, 700 for a Delay from before the row, times the rate's k, half with ping-pong: `delayReach`), in both engines; the knob's range for modulation, macro ranges and the morph stays 1..700 ms (`DELAY_TIME_MIN/MAX`), its base reaches 15,200 ms (`DELAY_REACH_MAX`, `DELAY_TIME_TOP` in engine.c: the longest length at 11k). The synced note lengths are spread over 1..520 ms (`DELAY_SYNC_TOP`, `defs.h` too), so a stored synced Delay keeps its note length; a time past 520 ms picks the longest (1/1). The FM-1's knob steps 7 ms per click (`DELAY_INC`).
- **The Delay's glide** (#484, [D-750](ledger/modules.md#D-750)): a new time (the knob, a cable or pin, the morph, a synced tempo) is reached by a glide of the read position, never a jump, which cut the echo (a click at every edge of 8 Lire's square Hyper LFO). The distance behind the write head (`cur`, samples Q16, in integers in both engines) moves at a `rate` that heads for 1/1024 of the remaining distance a sample, at most half a sample a sample (`DELAY_GLIDE_MAX`), and changes by at most 128 (Q16) a sample (`DELAY_GLIDE_ACC`, full rate in 6 ms); it lands within 1/64 sample. While it moves, one loop (`dly_glide` / `delayGlide`) serves plain, ping-pong and swell and reads the line linearly between two samples; a time that holds still runs the old loops, bit for bit as before (`tools/engine_exact.sh`). The CPU model charges `glide` (cpu-costs.json, about 11,000 cycles a block, 1.6 % of the FM-1) to a Delay whose `time` a cable, pin or the morph moves. Golden kits `lire-hyper-delay-1` and `-2`.
- **The Delay's rate** (#659, [D-762](ledger/modules.md#D-762), [D-763](ledger/modules.md#D-763), [D-764](ledger/modules.md#D-764), [D-765](ledger/modules.md#D-765)): a `rate` row appended in format 51 (`since: 51`), 44k / 22k / 11k (`DELAY_RATES`, k = 1, 2, 4). 44k runs the loops above, bit for bit as before (`tools/engine_exact.sh`, `tools/old_format.sh`). 22k and 11k run one loop (`delaySlow` / `dly_slow`): the input through a 4-pole Butterworth low pass at 0.4 of the line's rate (8.8 / 4.4 kHz; `delayAaCoefs`, two direct-form-I sections, coefficients Q30 in `defs.h` `DELAY_AA`, states Q26, each product one multiply-high, the input clamped to ±4: engine.c `dly_aa`, a pass of its own over the block that leaves the filtered samples the line takes in a small array), every k-th filtered sample plus the feedback written to the line; every sample reads the line between two line samples (linear) at the time's distance behind the write head, so the time keeps its sample resolution and the glide works as above. The filter's state lives in the line's last `DELAY_TAIL` (48) samples (words 0..7; Tape's in 8..14), so `inst_t` does not grow. The line's bytes do not depend on the rate: it holds the length times k, the time reaches that (`delayReach`, `row_top`; half with ping-pong), the length row shows that time (`altText`, pages_ui.c `stretched`) and the FM-1's time knob clicks k × 7 ms. The longest time is `DELAY_REACH_MAX`, 15.2 s (3.8 s at 11k × 4). A new rate is a tape changing speed (`delayChange` / `dly_change`, [D-764](ledger/modules.md#D-764)): the line keeps its samples and the read point stays on the same line sample (`cur` becomes k_new / k_old times as many samples, `ph` 0), so the echoes in it play at k_old / k_new of their pitch, with no jump. The read point then stands (`hold`: in C the glide's `rate` past `DLY_HOLD`, the samples left) until the old rate's samples have played, k_new − 1 + `cur` samples counted in whole blocks, and then glides to the time. A lower rate's low pass coming from 44k starts settled on the block's first input. When the line gives its tail to the filters' state or takes it back (a lower rate or Tape, against 44k without Tape), its ring (each ping-pong side its half) drops its 48 oldest samples or gets 48 silent ones older than all (`delayRing` / `dly_ring`), so every echo keeps its distance from the write head. CPU model (cpu-costs.json): about 14,700 cycles a block at 22k and 13,800 at 11k against 6,600 at 44k. Golden kits `delay-rate-*` (the rate changing: `-switch`, `-switch-pingpong`, `-switch-11k`, `-switch-tape`; a loud saw and noise: `-loud-22k`, `-loud-11k`).
- **Tape on the Delay** (#660, [D-766](ledger/modules.md#D-766), [D-767](ledger/modules.md#D-767), [D-768](ledger/modules.md#D-768), [D-769](ledger/modules.md#D-769), [D-770](ledger/modules.md#D-770)): rows `tape` (switch), `wow` (0..1, 0.3 as made) and `heads` (`DELAY_HEADS`: 1, 2, 3, 1+2, 1+3, 2+3, 1+2+3; `DELAY_HEAD_MASK`) appended in format 51. Tape off runs the loops above, bit for bit. Tape on runs one loop at any rate (`delayTape` / `dly_tape`): each head in the mask reads the line between two samples (linear) at its multiple of the time (1x, 2x, 3x `cur`, clamped to the line's end) plus the wow and flutter offset, and the heads sound at 1 / n each (`DELAY_HEAD_GAIN`) into the output and the feedback. Wow and flutter: two sines in the read position, 0.6 Hz at ±1 ms and 6.7 Hz at ±0.1 ms times `wow` (`DELAY_WOW_*`, `DELAY_FLUTTER_*`; u32 phase accumulators with quantized increments, the offset worked out at each block's end and ramped across the block in whole steps of 1/256 sample, so both engines move alike). The feedback runs through a one-pole low pass at 4 kHz and a one-pole high pass at 80 Hz (`DELAY_TAPE_LP` / `HP`; Q24 states, the coefficients per rate step because the loop runs at the line's rate), and the write (input, through the rate's low pass at 22k / 11k, plus feedback) through `delayClip` / `dly_soft`. Distances are 1/256 samples in 32 bits. The state sits in the line's tail (words 8..14: the two sides' loop filters, the phases, the last offset); Tape on or off keeps the line, as a new rate does (`dly_change`: at 44k the ring drops or gains the tail's 48 samples at its oldest end, so no echo moves; Tape's state starts from silence). While the time stands and every head stays inside the line for the block, one of six loops specialised for ping-pong and the number of heads runs (`tape_fast`): each head's read point is a distance in 1/256 samples modulo the line that steps by the same amount every sample (by a whole sample for a head held at the line's end the whole block), the same sample and fraction as the general loop works out, without its multiply and clamp per head; a moving time, or a head that reaches the line's end within the block, runs the general loop. The Delay's products of a line read (|e| ≤ 2^18) and a level are multiply-highs (`MUL_E`), as are the soft clip's (`dly_soft`) and Tape's loop filters' (the poles × 256, unsigned): the same results bit for bit. CPU model (cpu-costs.json, heads measured with Tape on: `CHOICE_CONTEXTS` in `scripts/cpu-costs.ts`): one head at 44k about 15,900 (2.4 %) cycles a block, 1+2 20,400 (3.0 %), 1+2+3 25,300 (3.7 %); ping-pong 27,000 to 40,000. Golden kits `delay-tape-*`.
