- **The Delay's length** ([D-375](ledger/engine.md#D-375), [D-376](ledger/engine.md#D-376), [D-426](ledger/modules.md#D-426)): the `length` row (format 50, `since: 50`) sizes the Delay's line when a patch loads, in both engines (modules.ts `delayLine`, engine.c `dly_samples`; the browser makes a new empty line when the length changes, as the FM-1's load does). It is `ParamDef.fixed` (`DEF_FIXED` in defs.h): both engines read its base, the FM-1's `eng_set_param` and `m1_patch_param` ignore it, its page row says SET IN THE EDITOR, presets skip it, and the editor sends the whole patch when it changes (main.ts `onParam`). The editor's row stops at what the effect memory has left (`delayRoom`, editor.ts `lengthCap`) with a note from `linesAdvice`; a new Delay that does not fit at 700 ms takes what is left. The time row spans 1 ms to what the line plays (`rowRange`, pages_ui.c `row_top`). A Delay saved before the row gets the length it played with (`validatePatch`: 700 ms, or for a time past 700 ms the browser's old line, half each side with ping-pong). A time past 700 ms on a morph B side stays browser only (`browserOnly`: the FM-1 keeps a B side past the range in 16 bits). Golden kits `delay-long*`, `delay-1600*`, `delay-length-*`, `delays-three`, `reverbs-two`, `choruses-two`, `lines-full`.
- **Range extensions as new rows** (#351, [D-429](ledger/modules.md#D-429)): an existing parameter's `min` / `max` never widens, because pins, macro ranges and the knobs map onto that range, so a wider one would change old patches. A new row, whose default plays as before, scales or replaces the value inside the module (both engines, the FM-1 too; no new memory):
  - ADSR `long`: `d` and `r` times `ADSR_LONG` (2.4; C `ADSR_LONG_Q16`), up to 12 s and 19.2 s. The card shows the stretched time (`altText`); the LCD shows the row's own value. The C release now steps at least one Q30 unit per sample: past about 11 s `v · ar` rounds to 0 above the end level and the envelope would never end (old times never get there, so old patches stay bit-identical).
  - LFO `phase` (0..360 degrees): added to the phase where the wave is read, so a reset or a clock restart starts there. `dir` back: the wave read at `off − phase`, done as −w(phase − off) for sine, saw and square (odd waves) and w(phase − off) for the triangle, so the poly-BLEP keeps its sign. `range`: free rate × 1, / 25 or × 2 (`LFO_RANGE_K`; C: `(rate << 16) / (25 · sr)` for slow, `hz_turns << 1` for fast). The `rate` row shows the rate the LFO runs at, the value times its range's factor (`lfoRateText` through `altText`; the FM-1's `stretched` in pages_ui.c), while its knob keeps the row's own 0.02..40 ([D-372](ledger/engine.md#D-372)). Io 4's LFO RATE is the fast range: 0.04..80 Hz, the Jupiter-4's top. `mult` (/8 .. x8, `LFO_MULTS`): the synced length × num / den. Ticks per cycle are then fractional (a 1/16 triplet x8 is half a tick), so `lfoSyncCycle` works in per8 = 8 × ticks per cycle (a whole number for every length) and snaps every `snap` = per8 / gcd(per8, 8) ticks, where `cycles` = 8 / gcd(per8, 8) cycles end; the clocked phase wraps freely `cycles − 1` times and waits at the end of the last. A whole-tick length is snap = ticks, cycles = 1: the old rule, bit-identical.
  - Delay `note` (synced): one of `DELAY_NOTES` (1/32, 1/2T, 1/2., 1/1T, 1/1.) in place of the time knob's length; the line still caps it (its length, half with ping-pong). `swell`: fb × 198 / 95 (0.95 reads 1.98) and every write into the line through `delayClip` (x − 4/27 x³, flat at ±1 from 1.5; C `dly_soft`), so a loop gain past 1 settles near full level. C runs the swell in a loop of its own, so the plain loops are unchanged.
  - Reverb `freeze`: every line gain 1, damping 0, nothing into the lines; the Householder matrix is lossless, so the tail holds (C: the lines keep their Q13 rounding; the modulated taps' interpolation slowly softens the highs).
  - Pulse `inf`: the countdown stops while it is on (a new trigger reloads it); switched off, the window ends `time` after the last trigger.

**Assemblies.** The ⏻ on an assembly card or frame (`bypassAssembly` in assembly.ts) bypasses every member, nested ones too, and remembers in `AssemblyInst.kept` which members were already off on their own; switching the group back on restores exactly that. A nested group's own switch is superseded while the parent is off. `toDef` writes the members' own switches into a saved definition, not the group's. Editor only, like the rest of the overlay.

## Fixed input links and additional routes

A parameter whose definition names a dedicated input (`ParamDef.input`: VCA gain from `cv`, filter and ladder cutoff from `cv`, filter and Main `morph`, shift `cv`, pan and crossfade `pos`, vowel) is **permanently** fed by it: that wiring is what makes the module the instrument it is, so the editor draws the route inside the card greyed out (`.cable.alias.fixed`) and offers no way to cut it. Dropping a cable on such a parameter's slider thumb plugs it into the dedicated input (`connectParameter`). An unplugged dedicated input reads silence in both engines; for the VCA that means **a VCA with nothing at its cv is closed** (as on a modular; before 2026-10-14 it passed at full gain), so a level trim needs a Constant into cv, or the Output's / a mixer's level. `ModuleInst.disabledInputs` (format 17, flag bit 2) was the switch for these links; `validatePatch` drops it, so an older patch gets its built-in routing back, and the encoder never writes the flag. The firmware still reads the flag for binaries stored by older editors (untested: no kit exercises it any more).

`ModuleInst.inputMap` adds destinations to any non-p1/p2 side input: these are the user's own internal routes (coloured, click to disconnect, drag a side jack to a thumb to add one). Each source's block mean adds a normalized parameter-range offset (octaves for a logarithmic parameter), alongside the p1/p2 modulation. A dedicated input is excluded from the map for its own parameter (no double modulation). Patch and assembly validation remove invalid and duplicate destinations.

Binary format 18 adds header flag bit 3: a count byte followed by module index, input index and a u16 parameter mask per routed input, after the (now never written) disabled-input masks and before macro ranges. The firmware accepts 16–18. The encoder uses 18 only for additional routes and 16 otherwise, preserving unchanged patches for older firmware. Golden kits `fixed-links` (every dedicated link at once) and `side-input-fanout` compare the browser and C engines.

## Voicing (issue #22; Main's voicing moved to poly assemblies in format 20)

Serum 2's Voicing panel in this modular's terms, plus the parts of an analog polysynth that drifted out of spec.

- **Legato, priority, flaws** are settings of a poly assembly (see Polyphony); a mono voice with them is a poly assembly of one part. `auto` is legato with one part and a restart for a taken-over part; `off` restarts the envelopes on every change of key while the gate is up (one part: also when a release falls back to a held key); `on` never does.
- **Part outputs** `legato` and `jump`. Recipes: Serum's ALWAYS = a plain `glide`; legato-only portamento = Part `legato` into the glide's `time` thumb with a depth (or a macro range); SCALED = `jump` → math `abs` → glide time, or simply the glide's `lin` curve (constant rate, so a bigger step takes longer). Per-part detune / cutoff / envelope spread: a fixed **Random** into the destination with a small depth; slow pitch wander: a walking Random into an oscillator's `fine`.
- **Oscillator** `analog` (#673, [D-774](ledger/modules.md#D-774), [D-775](ledger/modules.md#D-775); format 51, `since: 51`, 0 = off): `analogShape` / `waveAnalog` in modules.ts, `ana_init` / `ana_saw` / `ana_tri` / `ana_wave` in engine.c. Saw: r = (1 − e^(−c p)) / (1 − e^(−c)), c = 0.35 × analog × (1 ± 0.3), as 2r − 1 minus its mean; C works it out as p · T(c p) / T(c) with T(x) = (1 − e^(−x)) / x from `ANALOG_SAWC_T` (129 entries over 0..0.5, Q30, linear interpolation: about 6e-7 off), 1 / T(c) and the mean from their series once a block. At the reset: polyBLEP on the step and on the dip's first half, polyBLAMP (2 c dt × x³ / 6) on the ramp's corner, the dip (0.12 × analog of the swing 2, ±30 %) for 25 µs and a polyBLEP on its end. Triangle: the peak at 0.5 + 0.03 × analog × t (naive, as the ideal one); square: pw + 0.015 × analog × t. Tolerances t: four per instance from its seed (`analogTol`, engine.ts `instSeed`, `inst_t.s.osc.tol`, Q15; the union did not grow). Analog 0 never reaches this code: the module switch (`process`) reads the row once a block and runs `m_osc_ana` when it is above 0, else `m_osc` as before (its code unchanged, `tools/engine_exact.sh`; 7 cycles a block for the check). `m_osc_ana` sends the sine back to `m_osc`, runs `osc_ana` on the plain path (a loop per wave, one copy or unison, with and without sync; the square only moves its pulse width) or the FM path with `ana_wave` (the dip twice as many sub-samples). `ana_saw` takes `ana_t` by value so its fields stay in registers. rv32 model, one Oscillator at C5 (cycles a block): saw 5,070 → 7,725 with analog 1, triangle 3,227 → 4,153, square 8,507 → 7,707, sharktooth 5,848 → 8,980. Aliasing of an E7 saw (energy off the harmonics, Hann window): ideal −27.3 dB, analog 1 −26.6 dB in modules.ts, the same in C. Golden kits `osc-analog-*`.
- **Oscillator** `blend` (0..1, default 0.5): copy weights centre 1 − b, others b, normalised (`blendWeights`; the centre is the middle copy, or the two middle ones; with one or two copies all are equal), so 0.5 is the plain average, 0 the centre copy alone, 1 the detuned copies alone. `stack` (off / +oct / +5th / +oct+5th / −oct): the copies cycle through the intervals (copy j at `STACKS[k][j % n]`), and the copy count is at least the number of intervals (unison 1 with +oct plays two copies; the cap of 5 copies stays: each copy is a full oscillator). The level stays at `level` because the weights sum to 1. Blend, stack and unison are plain-path only. (The oscillator's own `drift` is gone: a Random drifts a whole part, not each unison copy.)
- **Glide** `curve` (exp / lin): exp is the one-pole (99 % of any step in `time`); lin moves at one unit per `time` (C: Q24 step `2^40 / (time_q16 · sr)`).
- **Factory assemblies**: `mono lead` (one part, the Part's legato into the glide), `supersaw stack` (four parts panned by index), `drift poly` (was `analog voice`, briefly `basic mono`: four parts, walking Randoms on the oscillators' fine tune, a fixed one on cutoff and decay), `basic poly` (was `poly voice`: the plainest four-part voice). Tutorial chapter 12 "Voicing" (unpublished) walks through all of it. Tests: `web/src/voicing.test.ts`, `web/src/poly.test.ts`.

## The bank: 64 slots, power-safe, SAVE writes the slot (#61 #62 #74, 2026-10-18; replaces the saved-state blob and patchstore.c)

- `firmware/src/slotstore.c`: 64 patch slots (`M1_SLOTS`) plus one settings record (id `SL_SETTINGS` = 64), log-structured over 66 sectors (0x97000, 0x98000 = the old patch store, and 0xA0000..0xDFFFF; all inside Felucca's data window, never erased by the loader). One record per sector: header (magic "M1S2", id, sequence number, length, CRC-16, name; written last = the commit) + data from +256 (up to `M1_PATCH_MAX` 3840 B). A write takes a sector without a live record (round robin), erases it, programs data then header, verifies, and only then erases the replaced record's sector; at boot (`sl_scan`) the newest valid record of each id wins and older copies are erased. 65 live records in 66 sectors: one is always free. 32-slot bank records ("M1SL" at 0xA0000 + n x 4 KiB) are read as slot n with sequence 0.
- **Writes keep the sound (#656, [D-926](ledger/device-ui.md#D-926)).** The store's erases and page programs (`storage_hw.c` `st_op`, for the slots, the settings record and the presets) run in steps: `fl_step_ram` (`hal/fm1_flash_step.h`, in RAM, interrupts off, the flash's read path off) starts or resumes the write and polls the chip; once the audio interrupt is pending, or 450 us have passed, it suspends the chip (75h), waits until the chip reports not busy, turns the read path back on and returns. `st_op` then lets the interrupts run (the audio renders its half from the flash, core 1 included; the key scan; USB), turns them off and resumes the chip (7Ah). Each run lasts at least 150 us, as the chip's datasheet asks for an erase to make progress. The read path comes back only after the chip said it is not busy, so a chip that does not take a suspend within 100 us only ends that write's steps (DIAG 128 counts it); that write then runs to its end with the interrupts off and the audio buffer zeroed (a gap of up to about 20 ms). A write still running after 250 ms finishes without steps. Only on the P25Q80H (JEDEC 85 60 14, `ST_STEP_JEDEC`): the UC25HQ80 of newer FM-1s (#739, `FL_JEDEC_UCUN`) writes as before, interrupts off and the audio zeroed: it has the same suspend, resume and SUS bits, but an erase needs runs of 200 us to make progress (over `ST_RUN_MIN`), so it waits for a check on a unit with that chip. The overload guard does not count the halves rendered during a write (`st_busy`). The update path (`ota_hw.c`, the loader) keeps `hal/fm1_flash.h`'s plain driver, and FACTORY RESET still stops the engine for its 66 erases. `tools/build.py` refuses `.ram_text` with a call or an address in the flash's window (a constant or jump table the compiler put in `.rodata`). DIAG page 128: erases, programs, the longest stretch with the interrupts off (us, counted from the end of the step's command, so a page program's data bytes are not in it), the longest erase and program (us), the steps, the writes without steps, the suspends not taken; page 129 first writes the settings record (a test; it does not wait for silence). The store reads and programs at most 64 bytes with the interrupts off at a time (`ST_CHUNK`, #729): each byte is a polled transfer on the flash's SPI bus, and a whole 256-byte page in one stretch may hold back the USB service long enough to lose a USB audio packet (not yet measured). DIAG page 130 (#729, zeroed by each read): TIMER5's longest wait between two entries (us; it nests in the audio interrupt and runs the 2 kHz USB service that queues each USB audio packet) and the `fm1_ms` it ended at, the store's longest read window and longest whole write step with the interrupts off (us, timed around the RAM call) and when each ended, the `fm1_ms` the last write ended, and `fm1_ms` now. `checkFlashWrite` logs it, with DIAG 126's underruns and missed packets, around each write. The device check's `checkFlashWrite` holds C4 on Basic Poly through each kind of write and looks for a dropout in the recording.
- The bank is the device's only patch list: HOME held + PRESET steps the occupied slots (#395; PRESET alone steps the focused device's presets), program change n = slot n (empty: ignored), the editor's SLOT commands address 0..63 (`SLOTS` 64 in protocol.ts). People see slots numbered 01..64 (`slotLabel`), so program change 0 loads slot 01. The first boot (an empty store) and MENU > SYSTEM > FACTORY RESET (`sl_clear_all`, ~3 s) write the factory patches (presets.h) into slots 0.. (`factory_bank`). A slot that does not load, or none at all, plays `FALLBACK_PATCH`.
- SAVE (and the editor's STORE) writes the running patch, edits included, into the slot it came from (`cur_preset`) under that slot's name, and the settings record (16 bytes: version, slot, octave, LED mode, knob accel (bit 0; bit 1 set: the quick start off, [D-247](ledger/device-ui.md#D-247), so a record from before reads as on and an older firmware reads the accel as before), the global tempo's low byte (was CPU in the bar), clock in, TEMPO and the tempo's bit 8 (was program change; `tempo_glo.h`), MIDI channel, MIDI out, the bank's format, selected module, REC's count-in, then the bank's factory set in bytes 13..15; a 12-byte record from before reads the count-in as OFF, a record under 16 bytes reads the factory set as unknown). The record stays at most 16 bytes: firmware up to 1.6 reads it into a 16-byte buffer without a bound, and reads only the bytes it knows. The next boot opens the slot the settings record names. Choosing a patch does not write the flash, so the boot opens the slot last saved. Edits since the load show as ` *` after the name (`m1_edited` in proto.c, set by the live edit writers, cleared by `m1_set_current` and SAVE).
- After an update (#441, [D-283](ledger/patch-format.md#D-283)): when the settings record's factory set differs from `FACTORY_SET_ID` (presets.h: 24 bits of FNV-1a over every factory patch's name and bytes, from `export-defs`) or its format is below `ENG_PATCH_VERSION`, the boot runs `bank_upgrade` once: every slot whose bytes, in any format, match an earlier factory patch (`LEGACY_FACTORY`: FNV-1a and length, from `web/scripts/legacy-factory.ts` over every committed presets.h) gets today's patch of that name, keeping the slot's name, and the bar says FACTORY PATCHES UPDATED. `export-defs` leaves out of the table any bytes that are one of today's patches, so a slot already holding today's patch is never rewritten; an edited slot matches nothing. Then `bank_add` (#630, `firmware/src/bank_new.h`) places every factory patch that is new for the bank: the bank's released set (`RELEASED_SETS`: the names each release tag's presets.h had, from `legacy-factory.ts` into `web/scripts/released-factory.json`; set 0 is 1.6.0 and older, a set from between releases counts as every release's names) had no patch of that name and no slot holds one. Each goes into its own slot if empty, else the first empty slot, never over a used one; with no room it is skipped. The bar says NEW PATCH: 2. INIT (NEW: 30. VERONA DRUM 1 past 26 characters), 2 NEW PATCHES ADDED, or NO FREE SLOT FOR NEW PATCH instead of FACTORY PATCHES UPDATED. Then the settings record takes today's factory set and format. Run `legacy-factory.ts` before `export-defs` after any change to a factory patch, and after a release tag (`legacy-factory.test.ts` checks every tag's set is listed).
- Gone: `patchstore.c`, the state blob and its versions, `state_pack`, `ps_*`; old stored states are not read.

## Panel controls as patch outputs (#60, 2026-10-18; the knobs K1..K6 superseded by "Device pages" below)

- The patch owns the FM-1's spare controls (#57 decision 4): Main parameters `k5` / `k6` are the SELECT / ALGO encoders (outputs after k4), and the gate outputs `fx scl env lfo edit glo arp` (`PANEL_BUTTONS`; the matrix ids 2 3 4 5 6 7 10, M-VAVE's order) are the buttons (SEQ and REC were patch buttons too until #83 gave them to the step page; format 26: 7 buttons, 13 labels, CC 80..86). The device keeps PRESET, PLAY, SAVE, HOME and OCT- / OCT+.
- `ModuleInst.panel` on the Main module: `toggle` (buttons that switch on / off instead of acting while held), `on` (switched ones that are on, saved), `labels` for K1..K6 and the buttons (`panelLabel`: the LCD's capitals and signs, at most 10 characters). Binary format 24: a fixed panel section (`PANEL_BYTES` = 154: u16 toggle mask, u16 on mask, 15 labels x 10 bytes) after the poly group bytes; `eng_panel_at` / `eng_records_at` in engine.h; proto.c `m1_panel` / `m1_panel_on`.
- Engines: the button states are runtime (`Engine.setButton`, `eng_buttons`), started from the saved switches at load; the Main module fills the outputs. CC in: 16..19 = the focused page's rows, 22 = morph, 80..86 = the patch buttons (64 and up: on).
- Firmware: K1..K6 always turn the Main module's knobs; a held button is on while held, a switching one flips on the press (the patch's `on` mask follows, SAVE keeps it); the buttons' LEDs are lit while on and glow dimly when a cable leaves the output. Gone: the mixer page (level = MASTER, morph / legato / priority = the patch), the ENV page, FX bypass, SELECT's module stepping.
- Editor dock: the buttons with their jacks (press = hold / switch; right-click: switch or hold, label); the layout is "Main dock: button block" under Device pages (#113).

## Player screen and legend (#63, 2026-10-18; superseded by "Device pages" below)

- Page 0 is the player screen (`ui.c player_draw`): the top bar is the slot number (1..64) and the patch name, ` *` while edited; K1..K4 as four big rows (the panel label, else where the knob's cable goes, the position in percent, a bar); K5 / K6 small, side by side; a footer with PLAY / STOP and the tempo (MIDI while MIDI clock drives the clock), the parts and the octave, and a square that lights on each beat while the transport runs. Turning a knob or pressing a patch button puts its label and value / state in the bar for a second.
- HOME tapped shows the legend (`legend_draw`): every knob and patch button with its label; buttons no cable uses are dim, switched-on ones say (ON). HOME again goes back.
- Gone: the module page (SELECT stepping, P1 / P2 lines), the patch diagram (its 30 KB picture buffer), the ENV performance page.

## Steps module and the SEQ page (#83, 2026-10-18; XOX steps after charlesvestal/fm1-x0x; the grid and the roll since #239 / #241)

- Module `steps` (#83; a track of notes since #133; `seq` stays for the eight-step CV sequencer): inputs `clk`, `reset`, `transpose`; outputs `trig`, `pitch`, `vel`, `keys`; params `len` (1..64), `rate` (`STEPS_RATES` in track.ts: the Arpeggiator's seven, then 1/2 and 1/64 appended, #351; `STEPS_RATE_HT`: half ticks of the 24 ppqn clock per step, so 1/64 is a tick and a half and every second step starts `tickLen >> 1` samples after a tick, [D-536](ledger/steps.md#D-536)), `swing` (50..80 %: every second step late by (swing − 50) / 50 of a step), `gate` (a share of a note's last step). The rates 1/2 and 1/64 and swing past 75 % are a **stretch** past the parameter's `max` (`ParamDef.top`, [D-535](ledger/steps.md#D-535)): `max` stays 6 and 75, so the parameter inputs (base + mean × (max − min)), macro ranges and the morph's unit space are what they were and a patch made before plays bit for bit the same; the card's slider (`topOf`), a typed value and the FM-1's knobs reach the top (`defs.h` `ptop` / `DEF_TOP`; a choice knob already steps every name). A base past `max` is clamped to the top under modulation (patch.ts `modulate`, engine.c `apply_cv`); a morph with a side past `max` moves linearly (choices snap at 0.5), and engine.c keeps such a B side as B − min in 1/2048 (`node_t.over`, so a stretch is linear and under 32 wide: `export-defs` checks). Golden kit `steps-rates`. `ModuleInst.name` is the track's name (8 characters of the LCD's font), its binary record's data (`ModuleDef.data` = 8). It runs once (`SHARED_TYPES`); 16 per patch on the FM-1 (`ENG_MAX_TRACKS`, `DEVICE.tracks`).
- **Notes (#133, format 31, `web/src/track.ts`):** `ModuleInst.notes` = `StepNote[]` (`at` start step 0..63, `len` steps, `note` MIDI, `vel` 1..127, `chance` 5..100 % in steps of 5, absent = 100), sorted by step then note, one per step and key (`fixNotes`), at most `TRACK_NOTES_MAX` 128 per track (per pattern) and `DEVICE.notes` 1536 per patch, every pattern counted (the FM-1's pool, `ENG_MAX_NOTES`). Binary: flag bit 4 = the notes section (u8 count, per track with notes in module order u8 module, u8 n, n u32 LE `noteWord`: bits 0..5 start, 6..11 length − 1, 12..18 note, 19..25 velocity, 26..30 chance code (100 − chance) / 5, bit 31 since format 50: tied across the loop point, #384). A note with bit 31 (`StepNote.wrap`, C `ENG_NOTE_WRAP`) counts its length on past the end of the pass into the next one (`left` = length − 1, `snd_t.dr` bit 7 keeps the pass's end from cutting it); with length 64 it is a drone (`isDrone`, `eng_note_drone`): it starts once (`dr` = 0x80 | step + 1) and, while a drone of the same step and key is in the pattern playing, its step coming round starts nothing (`steps_drone_lives`), so it holds until a stop, a mute or an edit takes it away ([D-819](ledger/steps.md#D-819)); golden kits `steps-drone`, `-pp`, `-rnd`. Patches from before carry `steps` (a byte per step: bit 0 on, bit 1 accent, bits 2..6 chance code, bit 7 tie) and the `notes` / `vels` lanes: `fixTrack` → `migrateSteps` makes an on step a note at its note and velocity (an accent at full velocity, 127), a run of tied steps one longer note; a tie after an off step or on step 1 is dropped. Cables from the gone `acc` output are dropped (patches and assembly definitions).
- Playing (`modules.ts steps`, `engine.c m_steps`, the same): on step k every note sounding with steps left goes on (two steps more at most, so a stopped clock lets it end), then the notes starting on k sound unless muted or their chance says no (`notePlays` / `steps_plays`: `hash32(seed ^ loop·0x9e3779b9 ^ (k+1)·0x85ebca6b ^ j·0xc2b2ae35) % 100 < chance`, j = the note's place among the step's notes, so note 0 hashes as the old step did). A note lasts min(len, track len − k) steps, `gate` of its last step; at most `TRACK_POLY` 8 sound at once (the oldest ends), a note on a key that sounds ends it first. `trig` is up while any note sounds (one low sample when a note starts over a sounding one), `pitch` (octaves from C4, plus `transpose`) and `vel` (v / 127) hold the last note that started (the highest of a chord). **keys out:** every note on / off, transposed by whole semitones (`floor((q16(transpose)·12 + 32768) / 65536)`), like Main's keys: a Voice allocator fed by it gets a group whose note source is the Steps module (`DeviceGroup.src`, the group record's source byte; poly.ts `keySource`). The notes of one block reach the groups at the start of the next (block-quantized like the Arpeggiator; `Engine.trackKeys` / `steps_keys`), in order; a part that one of them let go and took again in the same delivery restarts its envelopes; overlapping notes are legato as the allocator says.
- Clock: with nothing in `clk` the first step comes when the transport starts, then one step per `rate` ticks of the engine's clock (tempo or MIDI clock), each on the sample its tick falls on (the engines report the block's tick offset, count and samples per tick: `Voice.tick` / `ticks` / `tickLen`, `E.tick_off` / `tick_n` / `tick_len`; MIDI clock ticks fall on the block's first sample, their length is measured). A cable in `clk` steps on its rising edges instead (the step length is the edge distance). `reset`: the next step is step 1. Stop and PLAY again restart at step 1.
- Mute is runtime state (`Engine.setMute`, `eng_steps_mute`), never saved; a muted track starts no notes. Protocol (#133): TRACK 0x2A (module, n, n u32 notes: all of a track's notes) and unasked TRACK_NOW 0x49 after an edit on the FM-1, MUTE 0x27 / unasked 0x45; INFO feature bits 2 (MUTE) and 5 (TRACK). STEP 0x26 / 0x44 and STEPVAL 0x28 / 0x46 are retired. The firmware keeps the running binary in step (`m1_patch_track`: the track's entry replaced, added or removed, the section created or dropped, what follows moves).
- **Step view** (track.ts `stepView` / `setStep` / `setStepValue` = engine.c `eng_steps_get` / `set` / `value` / `value_set`): what the card's lanes and the SEQ page edit. A step is on when notes start there (its accent, chance, note, velocity are its lowest note's), tied when a note started before goes on through it. On adds C4 at the default velocity if the step has no note; off removes its notes; accent = full velocity (taken away: 100); a tie lengthens the notes sounding on the step before (the step's own notes give way); untying shortens them; a note / velocity edit moves a chord as one and sets every velocity, an empty step gets a note.
- SEQ page (firmware `seq_ui.c`, page 8): SEQ opens / closes it; the top bar is the track's name (STEPS n without one) and ` REC` / ` ARM`. Every track is shown on an 8 × 16 grid (`seq_grid_draw`): a drum track's Drum map rows (see Drum tracks on the SEQ page) or a melodic track's piano roll (see Piano roll on the FM-1). ALGO steps through the tracks; the 16 white keys are the page's steps of the selected row (`seq_row`); held + SELECT (the roll: K3) that note's chance in 5 % steps, + K2 its velocity; OCT- / OCT+ page through tracks longer than 16; SEQ held + a black key mutes (any page); SEQ held + REC on this page clears the selected track (#231, [D-534](ledger/steps.md#D-534)): `seq_clear_press` asks first (`CLEAR name? REC AGAIN` in the bar for 3 s, `SEQ_CLEAR_MS`), and REC again within them, SEQ still held, sets its notes to none (`eng_steps_set_notes`, then `seq_sync`: the running binary and TRACK_NOW, which the editor takes as one undo step) and the grid or roll clears its cells in place; another button, key or knob, SEQ's release or the 3 s running out leave it as it was (`seq_clear_cancel`, `NOT CLEARED`), and neither SEQ's nor REC's release then does its own thing (`seq_held_used`, `rec_held_used`). The state is `seq_clear_at` / `_gen` / `_trk`; the messages go out in the loop's pass (`seq_msg_due`, `seq_msg_ms`), not from the handler ([D-228](ledger/device-ui.md#D-228)). The track line adds `SEQ+REC clear` where it fits. REC is global (below, "Arming and recording on the FM-1"); the page shows ` REC` in a red bar while REC records the track it shows. Key LEDs: the selected row's notes lit, the step playing at the mid level (dark on a step that is on), the selected row's black key lit (blinking while the track is muted), rows with notes dim; REC's LED while recording. Edits go into the running binary (SAVE keeps them) and to the editor (TRACK_NOW). Leaving the page leaves REC on. A plain white key switches its step when it is let go ([D-501](ledger/steps.md#D-501)): the press only holds the step (`seq_held`, `seq_held_tap`, the selected row's note in `seq_held_note`), and the release (`seq_held_tap_done`) switches it unless the step was used meanwhile. Any encoder turned with a step held (panel.c `panel_encoders`), `seq_grid_held` and `seq_held_undo` clear `seq_held_tap`; a second white key pressed while one is held switches the first one's step first. FX held + a white key still acts on the press, and a turn then undoes it once (`seq_held_turned`, the track's notes as before the press in `seq_held_notes`). Preview: `tools/ui_preview.sh out.png PRESET TRACK 0 8 1 [REC]`.
- Editor: the card's grid (`step-grid.ts`, the `grid` display under the rows): every step up to `len` in rows of 16, three lanes over the step view: steps (click on / off, Shift- or Alt-click accent, Ctrl / Cmd-click tie, vertical drag chance), note (vertical drag, a semitone per 6 px, Shift octaves, a chord moves as one and shows `+`), vel (press and sweep; off steps are skipped); double click resets. The name field, a mute switch, `rec` (cursor entry, #127: a played note replaces the cursor's step with that note; Backspace removes the step's notes, Enter ties). Edits are undo steps (`Editor.setNotes`, `onTrack`) and go to the worklet (`Engine.setTrack`) and to the FM-1 with Live on. The playhead from the worklet (`stepsView`).
- **Arming (#134, editor only):** Steps has a `keys` input, appended after its pins (port indices stay; `export-defs` takes `<T>_CVBASE` from p1's index, so ports after the pins keep it right). ARM on its card arms the track (`Editor.armedTrack`, one at a time; superseded by the armed set, [D-514](ledger/steps.md#D-514); not saved, no undo step; another patch or the module going disarms it): the keys (`Engine.noteOn` / `noteOff`: computer keys, on-screen keys, MIDI) go into the track's keys in (`Engine.arm`, worklet `{t:'arm'}`) instead of the shared keyboard, the groups that play the keys and the Arpeggiators, and through it as its own notes (`StepsState.thru`, the start of its next block: transposed, into keys out and the mono outputs, held until let go, muted too; `StepsSound.thru` marks them). The keys held when the routing changes go quiet. The dock draws Main's KEYS to the track's keys in dashed in the armed colour (`armRoute`, in the knobs' binding layer: not a cable); the card is edged in it; the status line says what the track plays (`poly.ts trackTargets`; `pinStatus(…, 'arm')`). A cable into keys in does nothing yet. With Live on an armed track's keys are not sent to the FM-1 (the FM-1 arms on its own panel, #139 below).
- **REC (#135, editor only):** on the armed track (`Editor.recOn`; a card's `rec` arms its track; Shift+R toggles it; disarming or another patch stops it). Transport stopped: cursor entry (`step-grid.ts`, `recordNote` / `recordOff`): every key held when the first is let go becomes the cursor's step (`track.ts setChord`: the step's notes replaced, one step long, its chance kept, a tie through it cut), the cursor moves on; Backspace a rest, Enter a tie, arrows, Esc. Running: the engine records (`Engine.setRec`, `StepsState.rec`): a note played through lands on the nearest step (samples since the step began, `ago`, against the step's length `sl`; past half: the next step), lasts its held time in whole steps (at least one, cut at step 64), and goes to the editor at its release (`takeRecorded`, worklet `{recorded}`), which lays it over the track (`overdub`: a note on the same step and key is replaced). Each time round the pattern is one undo step (`Editor.recorded`, `pass`). Untransposed (the track's transpose plays it back as it was heard). No count-in.
- **Piano roll (#136, `web/src/roll.ts`, design A):** ROLL on a Steps card opens a drawer over the canvas above the dock (absolute in `#tab-patch`, full width, 45 % high by default, its grip drags the height, kept in `localStorage['matryoshka.roll-h']`, moved from `modular-1.roll-h` by `storage-key.ts`). One roll at a time (`Editor.openRoll` / `closeRoll`; another patch or the track going closes it). All 64 steps across, 12 px per pitch, the keyboard down the side (the wheel scrolls; a key press plays into the track's keys in, armed or not: `Engine.thru`, worklet `{t:'thru'}`), the velocity lane (a sweep sets the velocity of the notes starting on each step, the selected ones if any are there), the playhead (`showSteps`), steps past `len` dimmed. Draw / Select / Erase as the header's tooltips say; every press-to-release is one undo step (`ops.begin`), arrow / Delete keys one each (`Editor.rollKey`, asked first by main.ts's key handler while the focus is in the roll). The header has the name, what it plays, ARM, REC, length and rate (read-only), the tools, what the pointer is over, close.
- **Piano roll, Ableton-style (the #136 follow-up, 2026-10-20, the owner's list):** one pointer mode plus Draw mode (B, the pencil; a press paints a note at the last length, a drag one per step, a press on a note removes it, a drag removes along). Pointer: a click on a note selects it (Shift adds / takes away), a drag moves the selection (Option at the press: a copy), its right edge the length, its left edge the start (ends kept), Cmd-drag the velocity (1 per px); a press on the empty grid draws a band (Shift adds) and sets the insert marker; a double click adds a note (selected, the last length and velocity) or removes the one under it; right-click removes. Keys: Delete / Backspace, arrows (Shift + ↑↓ an octave, Shift + ←→ length), Cmd-A / C / X / V (at the insert marker) / D (the selection again after itself), B, Esc. Fold shows only the pitches used (and the keys held). A ruler over the grid (bar.beat numbers) sets `len` by a drag (`RollOps.len` → `Editor.setParam`). The side keys light while Main holds them (`Editor.rollHeld` from main.ts's note sink: keyboard, on-screen keys, MIDI; the roll's own keys too) and show their name under the pointer. **While the roll is open the canvas is the space above it** (`#tab-patch.roll-open .canvas` height minus `--roll-h`, kept by a ResizeObserver), so every card can still be scrolled to; the knob binding lines are clipped at the roll (`clip-path` on the bindings layer). The roll keeps its keys while the focus is in it or the last press was (`Editor.rollActive`): Delete never reaches a card.
- **Drum tracks on the SEQ page (the #139 follow-up, format 33):** when the selected track's keys out goes into a Drum map (`eng_steps_drummap`), the SEQ page edits it by rows: black keys 1..8 pick the map's row (`seq_row`; stopped, the row sounds: `eng_steps_hit_note` without recording; SELECT with no step held moves it, clamped, REC on or off: `seq_row_step`, [D-526](ledger/steps.md#D-526)), white keys switch that row's note on the page's steps (`eng_steps_toggle`, velocity 100; FX held: 127, on a hit already there the accent switches), a held white key + SELECT its chance, + K2 its velocity (K1 nothing: the note is the row's), ALGO picks the track; a black key sounds its row while held and picks it, REC on or off, and while REC records the track (armed: `seq_rec_sync`) it is also recorded into the nearest step (a hit recorded ahead skips only its own note when the step comes, `stp.skipn`; [D-523](ledger/steps.md#D-523)). LCD (the owner's grid, 2026-10-23, [D-521](ledger/steps.md#D-521)): `seq_grid_draw`, an 8 × 16 grid of the map's rows on the page's steps, the same for editing and recording: the bar the track's name (`DRUMS`, ` REC` / ` ARM`), the info line at y 26, rows of 19 px from y 44 (the row's name in a 46 px field, the selected one ivory on coral; 16 cells of 10 × 15 from x 52, 11 px apart and 2 px more between beats: empty the panel colour, a beat's first a shade lighter `C_BEAT`, the step playing grey, a hit ivory, an accent coral, a chance fills part way up), the playhead a coral tick under its column (y 196), `TRACK n/m` / ARMED / MUTED at y 202, the hint at y 222. It draws in place against a shadow of what is on the LCD (`seq_grid_cell`, `seq_grid_row`, `seq_grid_tick`, `seq_grid_text`): a hit or a step is one cell (one LCD transfer, 150 px), a row pick two names (28 transfers), the playhead its two columns (17 measured; a full hit under it is not repainted), measured by `tools/panel_test.sh`. While REC records, the grid stays as it is (`seq_kbd` is false for a drum track); the hint line says `BLACK rec`. **The keys on a Drum map** ([D-520](ledger/steps.md#D-520)): whenever Main's keys reach a Drum map and nothing that plays them as notes (`eng_keys_drummap`: of the armed set, else of what is cabled, a Drum map on the keys or a track into one, and no group taking the keys, shared keyboard with Main's pitch / gate / velocity cabled, Arpeggiator or track into no map), `panel_key` plays black key n (n = 1..8, from the lowest black key, any octave) as row n's note and the white keys and black keys 9..11 as nothing (`key_eng`, `KEY_SILENT`), or as their own note when the map repeats its rows ([D-427](ledger/modules.md#D-427)); MIDI out sends what plays: a black key the row's note, a white key nothing ([D-525](ledger/steps.md#D-525)); MIDI in still plays a row by its note. Key LEDs: the row's hits, the selected row bright, rows with hits dim. Names: binary **format 33** adds the Drum maps' row names section (header flag bit 7, after the button kinds, before the macro ranges: u8 count, per map u8 module index and 8 names of 8 bytes; `patchbin.ts drumMaps`, `drumRowName`: the editor's names, else KICK SNARE CLAP CL HAT OP HAT LOW TOM RIM COWBELL); `eng_names_end`, `eng_drum_name`, proto.c `m1_drum_name` (without the section: `ROW n`). Compat stays 31 (older patches load, rows named ROW n). The editor's own step grid still shows a drum track as steps (the roll shows the rows).
- **Piano roll on the FM-1 (#241, the owner's decisions of 2026-10-23, [D-527](ledger/steps.md#D-527)):** a track that drives no Drum map is shown as a piano roll on the drum grid's geometry and look: 8 chromatic pitch rows (top the highest; `seq_roll_lo` the lowest, 0..120, so the window covers MIDI 0..127, C-1..G9, the track's whole range) × the page's 16 steps. The row names are the pitches (`seq_note_name`: C4, C#4 ...); a C row's name and empty cells are tinted (`C_CROW`, a beat's first `C_CBEAT`; cell code `GRID_TINT`). A chord is several cells in one column; a note longer than a step is ivory on its first step and dim (`C_DIM`, code `GRID_TIE`) on the steps it goes on through, each joined to the cell before by filling the gap. It opens (`seq_roll_aim`, once per track and patch: `seq_roll_trk`, `pages_gen`; `seq_select` opens it again) on the fixed page of 8 pitches (`seq_roll_lo` a multiple of 8, [D-809](ledger/steps.md#D-809)) that holds the track's median pitch, an empty track's C of the keys' octave. SELECT (with or without a step held) moves the view 8 pitches a click, SEL held + SELECT one pitch (`seq_row_step`; panel.c marks `seq_pen_ms` so SEL's release opens no SONG page); `seq_row` stays, so the selected row keeps its screen row and black key. The bar names the pitches shown (`seq_bar`, `seq_bar_lo`, redrawn by `seq_poll` on `seq_shown_lo`; `bar_text` takes 38 characters). Keys (`seq_key`): white keys switch the selected pitch's note on a step (`seq_roll_toggle`: `eng_steps_toggle` at velocity 100; on a step a note of that pitch goes on through, the note is cut before it and one starts there); LFO (`BTN_LFO` 5) held + a white key ties it (`seq_roll_tie`, [D-531](ledger/steps.md#D-531)): the row's last note starting before the step ends on it, a step inside a note ends it there, its last step makes it one step again, and with no earlier note the tie wraps round the loop (#384: the row's last note goes on across the loop's end to that step; on its own step it becomes a drone); a drum track's grid takes the same toggle and tie and draws a note's length the same way (#368), and its black keys sound their row through the track while held (`eng_steps_thru`, engine.ts `thru`; `seq_thru_note` / `seq_thru_up`, let go on any page): while REC records the track (armed, `seq_rec_sync`) it records live (`eng_steps_rec`), so a key held while it plays lands on the nearest step lasting the whole steps held ([D-523](ledger/steps.md#D-523), golden kit `drum-thru`); FX held + a white key the accent, as on drums ([D-529](ledger/steps.md#D-529)); black keys 1..8 pick the 8 rows on screen, black key 1 the bottom row (`seq_row` = 7 - n; they follow the window as it scrolls; stopped, the row sounds: `eng_steps_hit_note` without recording; black keys 9..11 nothing; the key LEDs as on drums: the selected row's bright, rows with notes dim; ALGO picks the track; #387, [D-528](ledger/steps.md#D-528)); **the pen** ([D-528](ledger/steps.md#D-528)): SEL (`BTN_PEN` 3) held + any key sets the selected row to that key's pitch (the keys' octave; off screen, `seq_roll_show` moves the view to the fixed page that holds it) and sounds it (`seq_pen`, `eng_steps_hit_note` without recording); FX held + OCT- / OCT+ change the keys' octave instead of the page. SEL's release does nothing on the SEQ page (`sel_open`), and its jack still goes high while held. **Markers** ([D-533](ledger/steps.md#D-533)): a step column with a note sounding above the window gets a dim triangle (2 / 4 / 6 px rows, `seq_grid_mark_paint`) at y 40..42, under the info line, and one with a note below at y 192..194, over the playhead's tick; `seq_grid_mark` (4 bytes, bit c up, bit 16 + c down) is what is drawn, so a scroll, page or edit repaints only the markers that change (one 6 × 3 fill to clear, three fills to draw). **Tie** ([D-531](ledger/steps.md#D-531)): LFO held + a white key (`seq_roll_tie`): the selected pitch's last note starting before that step is extended to end on it (a note of its own there joins it, `TIED`); on a step inside a note the note ends there (`TIED`), on its last step it is one step again (`TIE OFF`); with no note of the row before the step the tie wraps round the loop (#384, [D-819](ledger/steps.md#D-819)): the row's last note is tied across the loop point to end on it (`eng_note_end_on`, `TIED`), and on that note's own step it becomes a drone (bit 31 + length 64, `DRONE`; a drone there: one step, `TIE OFF`); a drone's cell carries `GRID_DRONE` (a dim band at its left edge); `NOTHING TO TIE` only for a row with no notes; LFO + FX with no step held, outside step record (`seq_row_drone`, panel.c): the selected row one drone on its first note, the row's other notes gone (`DRONE`; again `DRONE OFF`, one step; an empty row `NOTHING TO DRONE`; track.ts `droneRow`, the roll's Shift+L); in a live take a note held round the whole pass is recorded as a drone (modules.ts `took`, engine.c `steps_thru_off`); LFO's press is the page's there (no patch button). The message waits for the loop's pass (`seq_msg_due`): a key never draws ([D-228](ledger/device-ui.md#D-228)). **A held step** ([D-530](ledger/steps.md#D-530)): its note in the selected row (`seq_grid_held`: put there when the step has none) takes K1 (`seq_value`: a semitone a click, stepping over pitches the step already holds, the selected row following it), K2 (velocity), K3 (chance; SELECT moves the view even then); the rest of the chord stays. **REC** ([D-532](ledger/steps.md#D-532), [D-548](ledger/steps.md#D-548)): while REC records the roll shown (the page armed it), the 27 keys are its keyboard (`seq_kbd`: REC on, this page, the shown melodic track armed, running; keys held lit, every C dim, OCT the octave, SELECT pages the view) and the engine records them live (`eng_steps_rec`); REC off or a stop gives the roll back (`seq_poll`: the hint line). Hint lines (#450: in `FONT_S` at most 228 px, SELECT the knob and SEL the button): the roll `BLACK row  SEL+KEY pen  WHITE step` then `OCT 17+` on a track of more than 16 steps (#490), else `SEL song`; recording `KEYS record  SELECT page  OCT octave  FX acc`; a drum grid `BLACK row  WHITE step  FX acc  SEL song`, on a long track `BLACK/SELECT row  WHITE step  FX acc  OCT 17+`, while REC records it `BLACK rec  SELECT row  WHITE step` then `OCT 17+` or `FX acc`; a step held, its knobs (`HELD: K2 vel  SELECT chance  ALGO lock`, the roll `HELD: K1 note  K2 vel  K3 chance  ALGO lock`, on a lock page `HELD: K1-K4 lock  back to value: off  ALGO`; the key's release redraws it). The track line `TRACK n/m  ARMED  ALGO track  SEQ+REC clear`, each hint dropped when the line passes 224 px. The SONG page: `SELECT entry  K1 pat  K2 shift  FX+K1 insert` and `ALGO song  PRESET pat  SEQ+REC del  SEL back`. Drawn in place like the drum grid (the same shadows `seq_grid_cell` / `seq_grid_row` / `seq_grid_tick` / `seq_grid_text`, plus `seq_grid_lo`, the window as drawn; no new buffer), measured by `tools/panel_test.sh`: a step one cell (1 LCD transfer, 150 px), a row move or a pen in the window two names (28 transfers, 1288 px), a page or a pitch move the bar, the 8 names, the cells and markers that changed (204 transfers, 20,107 px for a page on <7PH's PATTERN; the text lines untouched), the playhead its two columns (17 transfers). The old cursor entry (FX + OCT rest, LFO + OCT tie, `WRITE n`) is gone; `eng_steps_chord` stays in engine.c, unused by the panel.
- **Step record on the FM-1 (#532, [D-804](ledger/steps.md#D-804)..[D-808](ledger/steps.md#D-808)):** on the SEQ page (a drum grid and the roll), REC let go with the transport stopped starts it on the first step of the page shown (`panel_edges` → `sr_rec_tap` → `sr_start(seq_pg * 16, -1)`; running, `sr_rec_tap` is `seq_rec_press`), and REC held + a white key starts it on that step, stopped or running (`panel_key` → `sr_rec_white`, which sets `rec_held_used` so REC's release does nothing, and ends a live take first with `REC OFF: STEP RECORD`; REC held + a black key still arms); while it runs every key edge goes to `sr_key`, except on the roll a key pressed with SEL held, which is the pen (`seq_pen`: the view to its pitch's page, nothing written; it sets `seq_pen_ms`, so SEL's release opens no SONG page). A key's note (`sr_pitch`: the roll its own at the keys' octave; a drum grid as `panel_key` maps keys to a Drum map, black keys 1..8 the rows, else silent unless the map repeats) sounds through the track while held (`eng_steps_thru`, `seq_thru_note`, as a drum grid's black key) and joins the chord on the cursor's step (`sr_note` / `sr_at` / `sr_end` / `sr_hold`, `sr_of` per key); the chord's first key takes the track's notes as they are (`seq_held_keep`, so `seq_held_notes` doubles as the snapshot: no step is held meanwhile). Each write (`sr_put`) lays the chord over that snapshot: the notes starting on the steps from the chord's first to the cursor go, a note sounding into the first ends before it, and a held note ends on the cursor's step; then `seq_sync` (TRACK_NOW). The last release writes and moves the cursor (`sr_move`: wraps, and the view's page follows it). LFO (`sr_lfo`, from `panel_edges`): no key held a rest (`sr_clear`), keys held the tie (cursor + 1, written); at the last step the held notes end there and their releases are dropped (`sr_skip`, which also holds the keys that started it). K4 `sr_knob`, black key 10 (note key 22, `SR_BACK`) back one and cleared; OCT- / OCT+ the octave. REC (`sr_rec_tap`), HOME or SEQ (`panel_edges`), ALGO (`seq_select`), another page (`seq_leave`) or patch (`seq_poll`) end it (`sr_stop`). PLAY starting the transport in step record makes it a live take (`play_toggle` → `seq_rec_press`, which ends step record). REC's LED is lit during it (`seq_leds`). The bar `STEP n  name ARM P01` (`seq_bar`, redrawn by `seq_poll` on `seq_shown_sr`); the cursor's column: empty cells `C_CUR` (cell code bit `GRID_CUR`: `seq_grid_cell` is 16-bit) and a 2 px sky tick at y 199 (`seq_grid_cur`); the hint `KEYS note  LFO rest/tie  K4 move  REC done`, the track line `TRACK n/m  ARMED  FX acc  BLACK 10 del`; key LEDs: the keys held bright, the roll's Cs dim (a drum grid: its rows' black keys). The FM-1 tab's wasm passes what the page sounds through a track to the browser (`HOST_THRU_HOOK`, `host_steps_thru` → the `note` import), so step record's keys and a drum grid's black keys sound there too. Tests: `panel_test` (`firmware/test/steprec_test.h`; `PANEL_SNAP_STEPREC`, `PANEL_SNAP_STEPREC_DRUM`), `fm1-ui.test.ts`, the emulator scenarios `stepRecord`, `recStepRecord`, `rollPaging` and `rollPen`; `STEPREC=n tools/ui_preview.sh` renders it.
- **Drum map (#137, format 32, `drummap`, appended to `ALL`):** keys in; eight rows, each a note parameter `n1..n8` (General MIDI drums by default: 36 kick, 38 snare, 39 clap, 42 closed hat, 46 open hat, 45 low tom, 37 rim, 56 cowbell; `NOTE_NAMES` calls 36 C2, the issue's C1 is the other octave naming), a gate output `g<r>` (up while its note is held; a row hit again while up, or let go and hit again within one block, gets one low first sample) and a velocity output `v<r>` (velocity / 127, held). Outputs interleaved g1 v1 g2 v2…; no pins (`PIN_EXEMPT`), runs once. Its notes come as an allocator's do, not as a signal: from Main's keys or a Steps track's keys out (`poly.ts keysInto`; `Engine.mapKeys` / `engine.c dm_keys`, delivered at the start of the next block, Steps events in `trackKeys` / `steps_keys`, keyboard in `noteOn` / `eng_note_on`; an armed track diverts Main's keys from it). Its record's one data byte says from where: 0 none, 1 Main's keys, 2 + the Steps module's index. Row names are editor-only (`ModuleInst.names`, 8 characters, `drumRowName`): the card labels the note rows and gate jacks with them (a click renames), the roll shows them as its rows when the track's keys out goes into a map. `cpu-costs.ts` measures its outputs additively (`ADDITIVE_OUTPUTS`); `export-defs` writes a choice list once and shares it (`NOTE_NAMES` for the eight rows and the allocator's range). **Repeat** (#184, [D-427](ledger/modules.md#D-427)): the ninth parameter `repeat` (off / on, default off) tiles the rows' keys over the keybed (`modules.ts drumRowsOf`, `engine.c dm_rows`): when every row is on a white key a key's place is its white-key count (a black key the white key's below it), else its note; the block runs from the lowest row's place to the highest (period = span + 1), and a key plays every row whose place matches its own modulo the period (F3..F4 rows: every eight white keys, G4 starts the next block). With repeat on the map keeps the keys held (`DrumMapState.held`, `dm.held`, 128 bits): a row another held key holds up is neither restarted nor let fall (gate down with the last of its keys), and a release of all rows (`dropRows` / `dm_drop`) clears them. With repeat off the matching is the plain note test, bit for bit as before. On the FM-1, `panel_key` gives a key that would be `KEY_SILENT` on a repeating map (`eng_drummap_repeat`) its own note. Golden kit `drummap-repeat`. Factory `drum kit` (rebuilt): keys in → a Drum map → boom kick, snare, clap, hats (closed gate and open, one assembly so the open hat chokes), tom, rim, cowbell, each through a VCA on its row's velocity (gain 127 / 100; the hats follow the closed hat's), two mixers. Golden kit `drummap`.
- **Presets (#192, editor; [preset](ledger/vocabulary.md#preset), [structure](ledger/vocabulary.md#structure)):** `web/src/structure.ts`: `defStructure` / `structureOf` (an instance goes through `toDef`), an 8-hex FNV-1a fingerprint that never reads module ids: each module's label (type, nesting depth, its card-row parameters) is refined `ROUNDS` = 4 times by its cables' neighbours' labels and port names, then the sorted module labels, cables, nested assemblies' member labels and card rows are hashed. Every factory definition hashes the same as its inserted copies (tested). `rowOrder` / `defRowOrder`: the card rows in canonical order (label + parameter, ties in definition order), the order of a preset's values. `web/src/device-presets.ts`: `Preset {structure, name, values, factory?}`; the user's in localStorage `matryoshka.presets`, factory ones set by `setFactoryPresets` (a list, or a function giving a structure's; read-only: `storePreset` over one names a new one "<name> 2"); `applyPreset` (clamped to each range, sets `AssemblyInst.preset`), `matchesPreset`, `stepPreset`, `renamePreset`, `deletePreset`, `exportPresets` / `importPresets` (file format `matryoshka-presets`, Load .json takes it). `AssemblyInst.preset` (≤ 24 characters, kept by `fixAssemblies`): display only. Editor: `presetChip` on top-level collapsed assembly cards that have presets (‹ name ›, the star from `matchesPreset`, redrawn by `chipsSoon` after value changes), `presetItems` in the assembly menu; a load is `change()` (one undo step).
- **Factory presets (#193, #413):** `web/src/factory-presets.ts` (imported by main.ts for its side effect): `presetsOfDef` gives Init (the definition's values) and then the engine's card file when it has one, else one preset per `RULES` entry that moves at least one card row. A card file (`web/presets/cards/<slug>.txt`, `preset-cards.ts`) holds presets written by hand: `engine:` once, then `preset: NAME` blocks of `section / label = value` lines (a number in the row's unit, a choice by name, on / off; the label alone when no other row has it; a section repeating a label numbers them `a (1)`, `a (2)`); rows not listed keep Init's value; a mistake is reported with its line and the whole card is left unused. `npm run presets` lists every engine's presets; `-- card NAME [--new]` prints (or writes) a new card with Init and every range as comments; `-- show NAME` prints what an engine ships as blocks; `-- check` parses every card (CI's `npm test` does too); `-- from-library FILE` turns a library exported from the browser into blocks to paste. The rules read module types and parameter names only (filter / ladder cutoff, res, adsr / env times, env r2 / r3 decays, delay / reverb / chorus mix and delay fb; clamped to each range). An XL's engine keeps the rule-made presets: the XLs built as their originals' whole panels do not share their FM-1 version's rows, so a card cannot be carried onto them (`projectPresets` does that only when every row a preset sets is there under the same name). `factoryPresets()` for every factory engine (the FM-1's ROM list, export-defs), `xlEnginePresets()` for the XLs' engines; both are worked out when first asked, not as the page loads. `setFactoryPresets` gets a function that gives one structure's factory presets: a factory engine's structure needs no XL built, and the XLs' presets are made only when a structure no factory engine has is asked for.
- **The environment's order (#196):** `devicesOf` (pages.ts) lists every top-level module or assembly with rows except Parts and editor-only modules: Main (`kind: 'main'`) first, then each Steps track (`kind: 'steps'`, named after the track) followed by the devices it cables into (`Device.track`), then the rest in reading order, the Output (`kind: 'output'`) last. `homeDevice` (the first with no `kind`) is where `fixFocus` and the firmware (`pages_home`, from `pages_frame`) put a fresh patch's focus. In the binary these are plain-module devices (kind 0), so the FM-1 makes their pages from defs.h (Steps: len rate swing gate; Main: morph tempo; Output: level); `pages_plain` names a track's device after it (`m1_track_name`, `pg_tname`). SEL (`BTN_SEL` 3) or SEQ on a track's device page opens the SEQ page on that track (`pages_focus_track`, `seq_select`).
- **The menu's settings tabs (#395; GLO's own page of #199 became them):** `menu_tab_page` (pages_ui.c) builds the page of the MIDI, PANEL or SYSTEM tab: up to four setting rows, the module byte `SETTING_ROW` (0xFF), the parameter byte a menu row (`MR_CH`, `MR_CLOCK`, `MR_MOUT`; `MR_LEDS`, `MR_ACCEL`; `MR_UPDATE`, `MR_DEFAULTS`; the `MR_*` and `MT_*` enums and `menu_tab` live in ui.c, `menu_page()` says the menu shows such a tab). `row_show` / `pages_cc` read them (`menu_value`, `setting_bar`), `pages_turn` steps them with `setting_step` (menu.c) and returns 2, which panel.c neither sends to the editor nor to the patch; MIDI CC 16..19 leave them (`pages_set_unit`). The INFO and GUIDE tabs are texts (menu.c `menu_draw`; the GUIDE, `manual.c`, explains ideas, not controls: [D-248](ledger/device-ui.md#D-248)). A turn right on UPDATE MODE or FACTORY RESET arms the row (`menu_armed`, `MENU_ARM_MS` 5 s, the bar says HOLD GLO 2 S) and GLO held 2 s in the menu does it (`menu_fire`, from panel.c `panel_edges`; `glo_t0`); a GLO tap in the menu goes back. The editor's GLO has no rows. Button names on screen: `buttonLabel` (modules.ts) and `PANEL_BTN_NAME` say SEL for `scl`.
- **Format 34, the device extras (#194, #197):** a section right after the device pages (header flag bit 8, `FLAG_EXTRAS` = 256: bit 0 of the flags' high byte, which was always 0; engine.c / proto.c accept it from format 34 on): per device u8 track (the device index of the Steps track that drives it, `ENG_NO_TRACK` 0xFF none), u32 LE structure (structure.ts; 0 a plain module), the preset name it was set to (a text). The editor's budget counts 12 bytes for an engine device's name whatever its length, so a preset load never takes a patch over the FM-1's bytes; another device's name is written only while it fits, else left out, as the FM-1 skips one (`presetNames`, [D-284](ledger/patch-format.md#D-284)). `extrasRecord` (patchbin.ts) writes it whenever there are device pages; `eng_extras_end` (engine.h) walks it in `eng_load`, `names_at`, `m1_valid`, `m1_range_section`, `pages_list`.
- **Format 36, morph membership (#355):** header bit 9 adds a mask after the morph B set, one bit per module, rounded up to whole bytes. A set bit means that module has a B side in the JSON. Only those modules morph. Old records without the mask retain their playback when loaded and upgraded. Reading this format is advertised by INFO bit 12. New editor writes use format 39 below. JSON already records B sides per module and needs no migration. See [D-268](ledger/patch-format.md#D-268), Proposed.
- **Format 37, allocation:** group priority bit 7 disables voice stealing. Assignment value 3 selects reset rotation. The group record stays the same size, and older formats reject these new values. A missing no-steal flag preserves stealing. `steal` is device page field 5, following unison at field 4. Reading format 37 is advertised by INFO bit 13. JSON stays at format 2. See [D-269](ledger/patch-format.md#D-269), Proposed.
- **Format 38, unison count:** the group parts byte keeps polyphony in its low four bits and stores the unison count in its high four bits. Zero means all; explicit device counts are 1 through 8. Old formats reject a nonzero high half. Device page field 6 edits the count live. Reading format 38 is advertised by INFO bit 14. JSON stays at format 2. See [D-270](ledger/patch-format.md#D-270), Proposed.
- **Sync compatibility:** INFO bytes 7 and 8 report the lowest and highest readable binary patch formats. The editor checks its writer version against this inclusive range. Firmware without the range needs legacy bit 15 to sync with a format 39 editor. Existing feature bits remain for older editors. See [D-272](ledger/patch-format.md#D-272), Proposed.
- **Format 47, written only when used ([D-281](ledger/patch-format.md#D-281)):** the filter's `osc`. The editor writes a patch that uses no parameter appended after format 46 (a value off its default on either side, a pin, macro range, input route or card row on one) as format 46 (`WRITE_FLOOR`, `formatOf`, `paramsAt` in patchbin.ts), with format 46's parameter counts: an appended parameter costs bytes only where it is used. The FM-1 reads it as any older binary (`eng_npar`) and `m1_set_current` upgrades it when the result fits. A format that changes anything but appended parameters raises `WRITE_FLOOR` to itself (patchbin.test.ts checks every format past it adds a parameter).
- **Format 49, the 1.9 format (#518; `FORMAT_1_9` in patchbin.ts):** release 1.9's one format change; later 1.9 work adds to it (its parameters carry `since: FORMAT_1_9`, its other uses go into `uses19`), never a new number. Everything in it is written only when used, so a patch without it stays format 46 or 48. It holds the oscillator's appended `pm2` / `pm3` (`since`) and its ports `pm2`, `pm3`, `index` after the pins; values past a top that grew (`ParamDef.grew`: `formatOf` writes 49 for a value past `grew.from`): a phase modulation index past 8 rad (osc, wavetable, pd: up to 16, `PM_TOP`), the LFO's `random` wave (5, after S&H: a new random target each cycle, reached from where the wave is along 3x² − 2x³ over the cycle, `lfoSmooth` / `lfo_smooth`; like S&H it ignores `phase`), the `wide` rate past 55 Hz (to 80, `LFO_WIDE_MAX`; its `max` itself grew, so a modulated wide rate moves over 3 % more octaves than before); and a device of kind 2 in the device pages section (`NAMED_MODULE`, `ENG_DEV_NAMED`): a plain module with its own name (`ModuleInst.name`, #518), u8 its index and a text, whose pages `pages_plain` names after it. A module inside an assembly keeps its name in the editor only; a Steps module's name stays its track name in its record. Golden kits `fm-3pm-index`, `fm-3pm-index-fb`, `fm-pm2-only`, `lfo-random`, `lfo-random-clk`, `lfo-random-reset`, `lfo-wide-80-0`; panel_test checks the named device. The module types `shreg` (Shift register) and `comp` (Comparator, #438) and `range` (Range, #526) and `matrix` (Matrix, #525) come after `esp` and carry `ModuleDef.since` 49: `uses19` writes 49 when one reaches the FM-1's patch ([D-292](ledger/patch-format.md#D-292)). A bipolar macro range (bit 7 of its pin byte, #526) also makes it 49 ([D-294](ledger/patch-format.md#D-294)).
- **Format 51, the 1.11 format (#667; `FORMAT_1_11` = `COUNTED` in patchbin.ts, `ENG_COUNTED` in engine.h; [D-299](ledger/patch-format.md#D-299)):** release 1.11's one format change; later 1.11 work adds to it (`since: FORMAT_1_11`), never a new number. A module record carries its parameter count n after its type byte, then only its first n parameters, and the morph B set has n values for it; the parameters after n are at their defaults on both morph sides, and both readers give them their defaults (`storedParams`; engine.c `eng_rec_npar`, `eng_rec_size`). The encoder keeps at least every parameter the FM-1 can change in its copy of the patch (`fm1Rows`: a device page row and what it also sets, a card row, all of a plain module device's, Main's tempo), so a knob or a preset load on the FM-1 never makes the record grow. Format 51 leaves out more of what is at its usual value: a module with parameter inputs whose attenuverters are 1 and whose p1 / p2 masks are its type's `defaultMap` sets bit 7 of its count byte (`REC_TEMPLATE`) and writes nothing after its parameters, else a presence byte and only the items off that template ([D-1000](ledger/patch-format.md#D-1000)); there are no knob masks ([D-1001](ledger/patch-format.md#D-1001)); with every port index under 16 a cable is 3 bytes (header flag bit 12, `FLAG_CABLES3`, [D-1002](ledger/patch-format.md#D-1002)); a row label that is its parameter's own name and a page name that is its device's are written empty, and three or more member indices in one run are written as the first and the count (`MEMBER_RUN`, [D-1003](ledger/patch-format.md#D-1003)); the morph B set starts with the module mask and then, per module with a B side, a bitmap of its parameters and only the B values that differ from A (`morphDiff`, [D-1004](ledger/patch-format.md#D-1004)). When the FM-1 sets the A value of a parameter whose B value its copy leaves out, `eng_b_keep` first writes the old A value in as the B value, so the B side stays where it was, and the budget keeps 4 bytes for every such parameter the FM-1 can change (`morphRoom`, [D-1005](ledger/patch-format.md#D-1005), Proposed); an edit of a depth on a record at its template writes the block out whole first (`eng_rec_matrix`). It writes format 51 only for a patch whose lowest older format is over the FM-1's 3840 bytes and whose counted form is smaller (`layout`), or that uses a `since: FORMAT_1_11` parameter; the budget's patch bytes are the smaller form's (`need`). On the FM-1, `m1_set_current` rewrites an older binary as counted records (`eng_upgrade`: the trailing defaults leave as the encoder drops them, without the rows the encoder keeps) in the same compact layout, cables kept at 4 bytes and the device sections as they were, when it would fit with every byte it gains; an edit of a parameter a counted record leaves out grows the record and its B bitmap (`eng_rec_grow`). `npx vite-node scripts/patch-bytes.ts` lists every factory, XL and community patch in both forms. The golden kits have a counted twin of every FM-1 factory patch, of the morph kits and of `oracle-morph`, `matrix-presence` and `cables-wide`; `proto_test --grow` sets every parameter and depth of every counted twin through the FM-1's in-place edits and checks the grown binary renders as the running engine does; `tools/old_format.sh` checks that every older binary upgraded, then grown back to all its parameters, renders bit-identically.
- **Patterns and songs (format 48, #375; [D-539](ledger/steps.md#D-539) to [D-544](ledger/steps.md#D-544), [D-282](ledger/patch-format.md#D-282)):** a Steps track holds up to 64 patterns (`PATTERNS_MAX`, track.ts): pattern 1 is `ModuleInst.notes`, patterns 2..64 `ModuleInst.patterns` (`Pattern`: notes, an own `len` 1..64 and `rate` or the track's; `null`: the track has none of that number and plays its pattern 1). Main's `seq` (`SeqState`): `pattern` (the selected one, 0-based, saved), `song` (0: none, 1..8: that song plays), `songs` (up to 8 lists of up to 64 `{pat, shift}`, shift -12..12). `patternOf(m, p)` is a pattern as it plays. Binary: flag bit 10 (`FLAG_PATTERNS`, p[7] bit 2), right after the notes section (which stays pattern 1), written only when used (`patternRecord`; [D-281](ledger/patch-format.md#D-281)): u8 selected, u8 song, u8 tracks, per track u8 module, u8 P, per pattern u8 n (0xFF none), u8 len (bit 7: short words), u8 rate (0xFF the track's), n words: u16 short (`shortWord`: bits 0..3 start, 4..7 length − 1, 8..14 note, 15 accent; only when every note of the pattern has start < 16, length ≤ 16, velocity 100 / 127 and chance 100 %, `isShortNote`) or u32 `noteWord`; then u8 songs, per song u8 n and n (u8 pattern, i8 shift). `engine.h` `eng_patterns_end` / `eng_steps_end` skip it; every section walker (m1_valid, names_at, pages_ui.c) uses `eng_steps_end`. Playing (modules.ts `stepsTurn` = engine.c `steps_turn`, the same): every track plays the selected pattern; `setPattern` / `eng_pattern_set` queue it on each track (`queued` / `E.trk_q`), which takes it at the end of its own pass (where `nextStep` gives visit 0; the length, rate and the next step are worked out again for the new pattern), or at the start of its next block while the transport is stopped. In a song (`setSong` / `eng_song_set`) each pass end moves the track to its next entry (after the last, the first), its pattern and its shift (added to the notes before `transpose`, pitch output too; never on a track that plays a Drum map, `trk_drum` / `StepsState.drum`); PLAY (the run starting) begins at entry 1. FM-1 RAM: the note pool `E.trk_note[1536]` (6 KB, in `ENG_POOL`), slices `pat_first` / `pat_n` per track and pattern laid out by `pat_layout` (every track's pattern 1, then track by track its patterns 2..), `pat_has` (a bit per pattern), `pat_len` / `pat_rate`; `TRK_FIRST` / `TRK_N` are the slice of the pattern a track plays, so the step view, the SEQ page and recording edit it; `trk_put` writes into the pattern playing (one the track had not got is made then, from its pattern 1, which `trk_copy` read). `eng_patterns_record` writes the section from the engine byte for byte as `patternRecord` (proto_test `patterns_test` checks it on the steps-patterns and steps-song kits). Golden kits `steps-patterns`, `steps-patterns-dir`, `steps-song`; `host_test` events `pat N` / `song N`. Protocol ([D-473](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-473)): TRACK / TRACK_NOW end with the pattern byte (`trackFrame(module, notes, pat)`, `parseTrack` reads pattern 0 without it), PATTERN 0x36 (pattern, song: `eng_pattern_set` / `eng_song_set`), SONG 0x37 (a song's entries), unasked PATTERN_NOW 0x52 / SONG_NOW 0x53; the editor sends them while the firmware's INFO reads format 48 (`DeviceLink.hasPatterns`). After any of them, and after any track edit, proto.c rewrites the section from the engine (`m1_patch_patterns`). FM-1 panel ([D-541](ledger/steps.md#D-541), [D-544](ledger/steps.md#D-544)): on the SEQ page PRESET steps the patch's pattern (`seq_pattern_step`; the bar shows ` P03`, ` P03>P04` while waiting, ` S1` while a song plays: `seq_pat_text`, redrawn by `seq_poll` when it changes), SEL tapped (no key as the pen since its press: `sel_down_ms`, `seq_pen_ms`) opens the SONG page (`PAGE_SONG` 11, seq_ui.c `song_draw` / `song_knobs` / `song_del_press`; rows redrawn only when their text changed, `song_line`). The wasm UI's `track` import carries the pattern, `pattern` and `song` imports tell the editor's FM-1 tab. Editor ([D-640](ledger/editor.md#D-640)): `Editor.pattern` / `song` / `songs()` read Main's `seq`; `selectPattern` / `selectSong` (no undo step) and `setSong`, `setPatternShape`, `copyPattern` (undo steps) write it; the grid and the roll get `trackView(m)`, a Proxy whose `notes` / `params.len` / `params.rate` are the selected pattern's (`patternOf`), and `setNotes(m, notes, p)` writes that pattern (making a missing one). `pattern-strip.ts` (on the card under its head, in the roll's header) and `song.ts` (the drawer); `showSteps(view, pats)` takes `Engine.patternView` from the worklet for the strips' waiting pattern and the drawer's live entry; `SeqState.names: '303'` names patterns I-A1..IV-B8 (`patternName`, editor only).
- **P-locks (format 49, #524; [D-817](ledger/steps.md#D-817), [D-810](ledger/steps.md#D-810) to [D-814](ledger/steps.md#D-814), [D-291](ledger/patch-format.md#D-291)):** a pattern's locks are `StepLock`s (track.ts: `at` step, `module` id, `param` name, `value` in the parameter's units), pattern 1's in `ModuleInst.locks`, the others' in `Pattern.locks`; `fixLocks` keeps one per step and row, sorted, at most 64 (`LOCKS_MAX`); `setLock`, `locksAt`, `patternLocks`. `locks.ts` `lockPlan(source, dev)` is what both engines play, in the device form's indices: per track the locked rows, each the parameters it sets (its own, then its device page row's `also`, `findRow`; at most `LOCK_PAIRS_MAX` 6), and per pattern its locks with values clamped to the row's range (`lockValue`). A lock on a module the device form lacks, or on a Part (a Voice allocator's settings), does not play; limits `LOCK_ROWS_MAX` 32 rows a track, `LOCK_TARGETS_MAX` 32 rows and `PATCH_LOCKS_MAX` 256 locks a patch (`DEVICE.locks`, a budget line). Binary (`patchbin.ts` `lockRecord`): flag bit 11 (`FLAG_LOCKS`, p[7] bit 3), right after the patterns section and inside `eng_steps_end`, so every section walker skips it; u8 tracks, per track u8 module, u8 T rows, per row u8 n and n (u8 module, u8 parameter), u8 P, per pattern u8 its number, u8 L, L × (u8 step, u8 row, i32 LE value Q16.16); written only when a lock plays, which makes the patch format 49 (`uses19`). Playing (engine.ts `locksStep` = engine.c `locks_step`, the same rule): after a Steps track's block, when its pattern (the one it plays: `TRK_P`) or step changed, its rows let go and the step's locks set theirs; nothing locks while the track is stopped (`E.running` / `s.run`, or `clk` patched) or bypassed. A locked value replaces the parameter's base before modulation, the morph and the flaws: engine.ts `applyCv` reads `node.lock`, engine.c `apply_cv` reads `lock_base` (a copy of the base values with the rows that are on, each clamped to its parameter's range) for a node flagged `locked` at load, so other modules pay nothing. FM-1 RAM: `E.lt[32]` rows, `E.lk[256]` locks of 8 bytes, about 2.8 KB in `ENG_POOL`. Golden kits `steps-locks` (patterns, a choice, values past the range, a clock in clk, stop) and `steps-locks-poly` (three voices, a row with a further parameter). Editor: the grid (`step-grid.ts`) marks a step with locks (`.locked`, a dot, `u.lock-dot`); right-click a step or click its dot for `.lock-pop`, the step's locks with their values and a row picker (`StepGridOps.lockRows` / `locks`; `Editor.lockRows`: the rows of the devices with `Device.track` = the track); `Editor.setLocks` writes the selected pattern's (making a missing pattern from pattern 1's notes and locks), `copyPattern` and Clear carry them. A lock edit is a structure change (`onStructure`). CPU: `web/scripts/lock-bench.ts` on the rv32im model (`tools/cpu/prof.c` event 4: the transport). FM-1 entry ([D-815](ledger/steps.md#D-815), [D-816](ledger/steps.md#D-816)): on the SEQ page with a step held (`seq_held`), panel.c sends ALGO to `seq_lock_step` and K1..K4 to `seq_lock_turn` while `seq_lpg` (ALGO's stop, 0 the note) names a page; `seq_lock_pages` lists the `pg_page` indices of the devices whose `pg_dev[].track` is the track's device. `seq_lock_turn` marks the step used (`seq_held_undo`: its release switches nothing), takes the pattern (`eng_locks_pattern`: the one playing, made from pattern 1 with its notes and locks when missing), steps the value from the lock's or the parameter's (`pages_lock_turn`, as `pages_turn`; `eng_step_value` for a log row) and sets or removes the lock (`eng_lock_set`, with the row's extras from `row_more`; removing the last lock of a row drops the row), then `m1_patch_locks` rewrites the section from the engine (`eng_locks_record`, byte for byte as `lockRecord`; refused and undone when the patch would outgrow `M1_PATCH_MAX`) and `m1_locks_now` sends LOCKS_NOW 0x56 ([D-480](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-480)). Grid: cell code bit `GRID_LOCK` 0x200 paints a gold corner (4 + 3 + 2 + 1 px); the track line becomes `seq_lock_line`. Editor: `parseLocksNow`, `DeviceEvent` `locks`, `fm1Event` maps the device-form indices back to ids and names and calls `Editor.locksFromDevice` (an undo step; `onStructure` neither uploads nor reloads the FM-1 tab while the event comes from there). The wasm import `locks` does the same for the FM-1 tab. Tests: proto_test `locks_test` (steps-locks kit), panel_test (the roll of <7PH), fm1-ui.test.ts.
- **Format 40, wide LFO rate:** the appended `wide` parameter covers 0.0008–55 Hz when range is WIDE (0.0008–80 Hz since format 49). Existing rate settings and modulation scales stay unchanged. Sync uses its clock or tempo as before. Algo Four XL uses the wide parameter for LFO SPEED. See [D-445](ledger/modules.md#D-445) and [D-273](ledger/patch-format.md#D-273), Proposed.
- **Format 39, shared unison:** group legato bit 6 selects shared unison and requires bit 7. The lower six bits retain legato. Older formats reject the new flag. Unison device field 4 now has values OFF, ON and SHARE. For firmware without an explicit readable format range, the editor requires INFO bit 15 (`FEATURE_SYNC` 32768). JSON stays at format 2. See [D-271](ledger/patch-format.md#D-271), Proposed.
- **Format 35, Tier 1 of #351:** the types `pd`, `env8` and `select`; u32 masks for a type with more than 16 parameters (`maskBytes`, `eng_mask_bytes`); parameters appended to existing types (Drum map `repeat`, ADSR `long`, LFO `phase` / `range` / `dir` / `mult`, Delay `note` / `swell`, Reverb `freeze`, Pulse `inf`); the Steps rates 1/2 and 1/64 and swing past 75 % (`ParamDef.top`). `ENG_PATCH_COMPAT_VERSION` stays 31, so a bank saved by an older firmware still loads. An appended parameter carries `ParamDef.since` (the format that added it; `defs.h` `psince`), and every walk over a binary's records goes through `eng_npar(d, p[1])` / `eng_record_size(d, ver)`: a record of an older binary holds its format's parameters, and `eng_load` gives the rest their defaults (the morph B set too). `m1_set_current` then rewrites the running copy into today's format (`eng_upgrade`, in place, when it fits `M1_PATCH_MAX`), so an edit of a new parameter is saved. A `since` parameter never joins `defaultMap`, so a module without a stored matrix routes as before. `tools/old_format.sh [BASE]` (default: the newest release tag in the checkout's history; at a format change also run it with `v1.4.1`, the last release with format 34, [D-250](ledger/patch-format.md#D-250)) checks this: BASE's golden kits, every factory patch among them, render bit-identically with BASE's engine and today's, and each upgraded binary renders the same, passes `m1_valid` and reports whether its bytes equal today’s encoding of the same kit. INFO feature bit 11 (`FEATURE_SYNC` 2048) marks firmware that reads format 35.
- **The environment page (#197):** `PAGE_ENV` 9 (ui.c), drawn by `env_draw` (pages_ui.c): eight rows of 26 px under the lilac ENVIRONMENT bar (`pages_strip`), one per device in `pg_dev` order (the grouped order); a Steps track's row has a square in its hue (`TRACK_HUE`, by `env_track_no`), a device a track drives (`env_extras`: format 34's track byte) is indented with a 3 px stripe in that hue; the preset name (`env_extras`) in coral after the name, cut first (`fit`); `env_status` on the right (Main's BPM, a track's length, ARMED). The row in focus is `pg_cur_dev` (framed in ivory): SELECT moves it (`pages_select`), K1..K4 turn its page 0 (`env_open` sets it; `env_knob_msg` puts the value in the top bar), SEL (`sel_open`, `dev_arm(1)`) adds the row's device to the armed devices or takes it out ([D-515](ledger/steps.md#D-515)), ENV (on release, `env_held_used` when SAVE came meanwhile) or HOME goes back along the trail. Fixed buttons: `FIXED_BTNS` (panel.c: GLO, SEL, ENV; no Page or Toggle role, never switching), `eng_load` makes an old patch's SEL / ENV / GLO kinds Gates; the editor's `FIXED_BUTTONS` (patch.ts) / `FIXED_DOES`. FX (`BTN_ACC`, seq_ui.c) is the SEQ page's accent key, SEL held the roll's pen; LFO (`BTN_LFO`) the roll's tie key. ENV held + SAVE calls `preset_store_focused` (#194). PRESET or ALGO on a row steps its presets (`env_preset_step`, #198); REC there is global REC, as everywhere ([D-548](ledger/steps.md#D-548)).
