These redraw only the rows whose look changed (#229, [D-216](ledger/device-ui.md#D-216)): `env_row` keeps each screen row's look as an FNV-1a signature of what it shows (`row_sig`; `list_shown`, 8 words, cleared by a whole redraw) and `list_row` composes a changed row a scanline at a time in lcd.c's `lcd_line` (the frame, square or stripe, then the type blended on with `text_onto`), so every pixel goes out once and nothing blinks. A SELECT click is the old and the new row (52 LCD transfers, 12,480 px; the whole page was 224 transfers, 115,510 px), a scroll past the window the eight rows (208 transfers, 49,920 px), never the bar (measured in `tools/panel_test.sh`).
- **Presets on the FM-1 (#194):** `firmware/src/presetstore.c` (after slotstore.c; `ps_scan` at boot after `sl_scan`): 7 sectors 0x99000..0x9FFFF, 10 slots of 384 bytes each (a 32-byte header: magic `PRS1`, sequence, structure, value count, CRC-16, a 16-byte name; up to 88 Q16 values, `PS_SLOTV`); a preset of 89 to 176 values (`PS_MAXV`; Cassia 1 has 118, #485) takes two slots of one sector: the first holds the header (count and CRC of all values) and 88 values, the second (magic `PRS2`, the same sequence number, structure and name, its own count and CRC) the rest, found at scan by sequence number (`ps_link`). A write takes clean slots from the sector with the fewest that still fit (`ps_alloc`), programs the second slot whole first, then the first slot's values, header and magic (the commit of both); a second slot no live record claims is killed at the next scan; a replaced or deleted record gets its magic programmed to 0, then its second slot's. When fewer than 12 clean slots (`PS_KEEP`) would be left beyond the new record's, the sector with the most dead slots whose live records fit elsewhere (`ps_fits`) is compacted (records copied whole, pairs together, then erased), so at most 58 slots are live (`PS_MAX`; a two-slot preset counts two); a scan keeps the newest of a (structure, name). Records of one slot are written as before, so a store from older firmware reads as it is; older firmware sees a two-slot record as dead. The 2 slots kept beyond one sector's worth absorb a clean slot a power cut during a compaction can cost: with 10 kept, such cuts in a full store left storing refused as full until up to 9 presets were deleted; with 12, none in the cut tests of `preset_test.c` (10,756 cuts). Host test `tools/preset_test.sh` (a simulated NOR flash with cuts and foreign bytes; in the firmware workflow). Factory presets in ROM: `FPRESET_LIST` in presets.h (export-defs, Q16). Format 34's extras give each assembly device its structure and its rows in canonical order (`pg_dev[].structure`, `.rows`, `.nrows`; `pages_extras`), and its preset name (`pg_pname`, kept across a re-read of the same patch). `preset_list` (factory, then the store's by name), `preset_load` (`eng_set_param` + `m1_patch_param` + `m1_param_now` per row, `poly_row_set` for a Voicing's rows), `preset_store` (ENV held + SAVE: over the user preset of the device's name, else "<name> n"), `m1_preset_name` (proto.c: the name into the running binary's extras, so SAVE keeps it). `PAGE_PRESETS` 10 (`presets_draw`): nothing opens it since SEL arms ([D-327](ledger/patches-and-presets.md#D-327); panel_test opens it with `presets_open` + `page_set`); SELECT / ALGO / PRESET load as they turn; SEL or HOME back. Its rows are drawn as the environment's (`list_row`, `list_shown`: a click redraws the old and the new row, 52 LCD transfers, 12,480 px; the whole page was 204 transfers, 105,068 px, #229); its gold bar is `presets_bar`, drawn by `header_draw`, so a message's end puts it back. The host UI (`host_ui.h`) runs the store over RAM (`host_ps_init`).
- **Arming as a set on the FM-1 (#191):** `eng_arm_mask(mask)` (engine.c; `E.armm` over module indices, an `eng_mset_t` of four words since #721 with `E.armany` its any, `E.armg` the groups with an armed module, `E.arm` the first armed track for REC): while not 0 only armed listeners hear Main's keys (`hears_grp`, `hears_trk`, Arpeggiators by `arp.node`, Drum maps in `dm_keys`), never the shared keyboard; `eng_arm(module)` is the one-track form; `eng_listens` says what can hear. Golden kits `arm-set` (event `arms <mask>`, `Engine.arm` with the devices of those module indices) and `arm-many` (70 modules, event `armx <a> <b>`: two indices, any of 0..127, #721). Format 34's extras list each device's member modules (`pg_dev[].mem`); `env_listeners` / `env_armed`; `dev_arm` (pages_ui.c, #395): SEL on a device page arms the focused device's listeners alone ([D-235](ledger/device-ui.md#D-235)), or takes them out when armed ([D-239](ledger/device-ui.md#D-239)), SEL on an environment row toggles them in the set, a device with none says `NOTHING HERE TAKES KEYS` and changes nothing; `seq_arm_key` toggles a track in the set. Every arm from the panel but REC held + a black key goes through `seq_arm_set` (seq_ui.c): it sets the mask, tells the FM-1 tab and, while REC is on, moves the recording to the armed Steps tracks (`seq_rec_sync`, [D-800](ledger/steps.md#D-800)). The SEQ page arms the track it shows (`seq_arm_shown`: `page_show` of the SEQ page, `seq_select` there, and `seq_poll` when `pages_gen` moved; [D-545](ledger/steps.md#D-545), [D-546](ledger/steps.md#D-546)); when it opens while REC records, it shows the first armed track that records instead ([D-547](ledger/steps.md#D-547)). Nothing armed is [wired](ledger/vocabulary.md#wired): the cables decide, and every patch loads so (`eng_load` clears `E.armm`; the patch format holds no armed set). Mirrored with the FM-1 tab as a mask in four words (#721): `m1_arms_now` → wasm import `arms` → `armedFromMask` (arm.ts) → `Editor.setArmed`; the editor's `armMask` → `fm1w_arm_mask`. Over the USB link the FM-1 reports its set (`m1_arms_now` in proto.c → 54 ARMS_NOW, the mask in four u32 LE (an editor before #721 reads the first two), after every change on the panel and as 0 after a slot loaded from the panel, #191): `DeviceLink` → `fm1Event` `arms` → `armedFromMask` → `Editor.setArmed`, never echoed back; `armFromDevice` (main.ts) keeps the editor's notes going to the FM-1 while the set is the FM-1's own. The mirror replays the FM-1's buttons into the FM-1 tab's wasm (`mirrorInput`), whose own `dev_arm` would report its result through the `arms` import too: once an FM-1 has sent an ARMS_NOW (`armsReported`), that import no longer sets the editor's set but pulls the wasm to the editor's (`emuArm`), and an ARMS_NOW being applied (`armsApplying`) is not pushed into the wasm, which gets it from the replayed press. The editor's arming reaches the FM-1 with feature bit 20 ([D-821](ledger/steps.md#D-821), proposed): `Editor.onArm` (not from the FM-1, under Live) → `DeviceLink.arms` → 39 ARMS, the mask in four u32 LE with feature bit 21, else the first two ([D-822](ledger/steps.md#D-822), Proposed) → proto.c `C_ARMS` → main.c `m1_arms` (`eng_arm_mask`, `seq_rec_sync`, the footer; no ARMS_NOW back), and again after every upload (a load disarms); the editor then sends its notes to the FM-1 whatever is armed (`device.takesArms`). With older firmware it stays in the editor ([D-802](ledger/steps.md#D-802), [D-516](ledger/steps.md#D-516)). Tests: `proto_test` (ARMS into the engine's mask, no reply), `protocol.test.ts`, `panel_test` (a key with two armed tracks plays through both), the emulator scenarios `arming` and `armWide` (Silver Box behind 64 VCAs: SEL and the editor's arming past module 63), `panel_test`'s `arm-many` fixture (SEL on a Drum map at index 69). An engine with no keys cable has no group in the binary (poly.ts deviceView drops an unkeyed Voicing), so the FM-1 cannot arm it; the device extras mark it (`ENG_KEYS_UNREACHED`), and SEL there says `NO KEYS REACH THIS DEVICE` ([D-905](ledger/device-ui.md#D-905)).
- **Presets move with their engine (#627, [D-865](ledger/patches-and-presets.md#D-865)):** `web/src/preset-lineage.json` holds every structure each factory engine has shipped (seeded from the release tags 1.2.0..1.9.1 by `npx vite-node scripts/preset-lineage.ts --seed <tags>`, which checks each tag's fingerprints and Init values against its presets.h), each card row as `[type#id.param, label, place on the card]` in canonical order. `gen-assemblies` (and `scripts/preset-lineage.ts`) adds today's structure where it changed (`scripts/lineage-lib.ts` `recordToday`: an entry no release tag shipped is replaced, not kept) and writes `web/src/preset-migrations.json`: for each earlier structure, today's structure and one op per today's row (an earlier row's index, -1 Init, a table over one or two earlier rows read as whole numbers, or a line: an earlier row times a factor plus an offset, in Q16, held inside a range, the line picked by an earlier row read as a whole number). Rows match by module (its type and its rows' parameters and labels, alike modules paired in card order; else the same type and id) and parameter; a step is automatic only when every earlier row matches, else `web/src/preset-maps.ts` holds a written map or a `none`, and `preset-migrate.test.ts` fails until it does. Steps compose down the lineage. Editor: `device-presets.ts` `migrateUserPresets` at start (main.ts), `addUserPreset` and `importPresets` move what comes in (`movedPreset`: not onto a factory preset's name). FM-1: export-defs writes `PMIG_LIST` / `PMIG_OPS` / `PMIG_TAB` / `PMIG_TV` / `PMIG_LINE` / `PMIG_LV` into presets.h (3029 bytes) and folds the table into `FACTORY_SET_ID`; `firmware/src/presetmig.c` `pm_run` (main.c `presets_upgrade`, after `bank_upgrade`, before the settings record) puts each moved preset (`ps_put`, today's Init values from `FPRESET_LIST`) and then deletes the old one, oldest structures first. Checked against the editor's values in `preset_test.c` (with a power cut at every byte) and the emulator smoke's upgrade (`firmware/test/pmig_cases.h`, from export-defs).
- **Preset transfer (#195):** protocol (INFO feature bit 7): host → device 2D PRESETS (answered by one 4C PRESET per user preset: index, count, structure, name, value count; count 0: none), 2E PRESET_GET (→ 4D PRESET_DATA: the Q16 values, 0xFF none), 2F PRESET_PUT (key, n, n Q16 values → ACK, 7 = full; n up to 176 with INFO feature bit 17, else 88: `DeviceLink.putPreset` refuses more to older firmware, `PRESET_NEEDS_UPDATE`; the USB receive buffer `sx_frame` is 1024 bytes for it), 30 PRESET_DEL (→ ACK, 8 = none); device → host 4E PRESET_STORED, unasked, after ENV held + SAVE. A key is the structure u32 LE and 16 name bytes (`devicePresetName`). proto.c through hooks (`m1_presets`, `m1_preset_get / put / del` in main.c over presetstore.c; proto_test's RAM store), `m1_reply_long` for PRESET_DATA. Editor: `DeviceLink.listPresets` (collects the PRESET frames), `getPreset`, `putPreset`, `deletePreset`; a preset of fewer values than its card's rows (stored before 1.8.2) loads the rows it has on both sides (`applyPreset`, `dp_load` / `dp_short`), the editor's status line says why (`shortPresetText`), the FM-1's bar `STORED SHORT: SEE EDITOR`; bank.ts "Presets on the FM-1" (Read, Get, Delete, Send my presets); main.ts takes PRESET_STORED under Live into the library (`addUserPreset`).
- **Arming as a set (#190, editor; [arm](ledger/vocabulary.md#arm)):** `web/src/arm.ts`. Any device (top-level module or assembly; `m<id>` / `a<id>` as pages.ts keys them) with a keys input can be armed (`armable`, `keysTargets`: an assembly's exposed keys inputs' targets, a module's own keys inputs). `Editor.armedSet` (independent toggles: `toggleArm`, `setArmed`; ARM on module and assembly card heads, the Steps grid and roll as before; `armedTrack` is now the one armed Steps track, if exactly one is, for REC / the emulator / Live). A device whose keys input is cabled from another key source arms that source with it, back to Main (`armedWith`, #395: the track and the Arpeggiator in front of a factory instrument), so `armedTrack`, the virtual cables and `armMask` count them; `armedFromMask` leaves out a device armed only as another's source, and takes an Arpeggiator in the mask as the device its keys play (the FM-1 arms an instrument as its key sources, `eng_arms`; #418). An Arpeggiator alone is not armable. The FM-1 does the same with `eng_arms` (a group's or a Drum map's source, and the track an Arpeggiator plays). The engine plays it as a patch transform (`armView`, applied in `Engine.load`; `Engine.arm(devices)` reloads): Main's keys cables into unarmed devices go (`mutedCables`, drawn `.cable.muted`), Main's keys into each armed device's keys targets come (`virtualCables`; a keys input another key source already feeds keeps it), so the existing paths (groups from `keySource`, Drum maps from `keysInto`, the fed tracks of #138) do the routing; the shared keyboard and the Arpeggiators not fed by Main in the view are skipped while anything is armed. Worklet `{t:'arm', devices}`. The editor draws exactly what the arm routes (`armDrawing`, #418, [D-641](ledger/editor.md#D-641), [D-642](ledger/editor.md#D-642)): `virtualRoutes` (editor.ts) draws each virtual cable as a dashed keys ribbon into the jack it goes into, along the curve a cable there would take, on the bind layer; `markArm` gives the cables Main's keys travel (`live`: Main's keys cables into armed devices, and on from each key source reached through its keys outputs) the `live` class, drawn in the arm colour, and the overridden ones `muted`; `showArmed` marks the devices armed with the user's choice (`armedWith` less `armedSet`) `armed-with`, with a half-filled ARM dot whose title says which device they are armed with, and `onArm` tells main.ts their names for the status line. No line is drawn from Main straight to an instrument whose keys come through a track. The FM-1 arms the same set ([D-515](ledger/steps.md#D-515)). While anything is armed the editor sends no notes to the FM-1.
- **Main's keys into a track by cable (#138, editor only):** a cable from Main's keys into a Steps track's keys in passes the keys through it while no track is armed (`Engine.fed`), as arming does: Main's keys play the track's instrument and the track adds its notes (the Demo's OPER and the adsr operator). Other cables into keys in do nothing. The FM-1 does the same since #139 (`deviceView` keeps this one keys cable in the binary, engine.c reads it as the track's `ksrc` = 1).
- **Arming and recording on the FM-1 (#139, the owner's picks 1B / 2C / 3B from the canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/SXuedHA3UiaWXFLP41MXu3, after X0X):**
  - Engine (`engine.c`, the same as engine.ts, golden kit `steps-arm`): `eng_arm(module)` / `eng_armed()`; `eng_note_on` / `off` put the keys of an armed track (or of the tracks Main's keys feed, while none is) into a ring (`thru_put`; the main loop fills it, the audio side empties it at the start of a block into `blk_thru`), and `steps_thru` (in `m_steps`, before its samples) plays them through as engine.ts does (`snd_t.thru` = note + 1, held until let go, never counted down). Arming lets go of the keys' listeners (`keys_off`) and of the old track's thru notes (`thru_kill`). Live recording: `eng_steps_rec(module, on)`; a note lands on the nearest step (`sst` against `slen`) in `E.rin` and goes, at its release, into the `took` ring as a note word; the panel takes them (`eng_steps_take`) and lays them over the track (`eng_steps_overdub`: a note on the same step and key is replaced). `eng_steps_chord` (track.ts setChord, the editor's cursor entry) is unused by the panel since the roll (#241).
  - Panel (`panel.c`, `seq_ui.c`): REC acts on its release; **REC held + a black key** (any page) adds that track to the armed set or removes it (`seq_arm_key`; `m1_arms_now` tells the editor, over the link and in the FM-1 tab; [D-515](ledger/steps.md#D-515), [D-802](ledger/steps.md#D-802)). While a track is armed the panel keys play it at the default velocity (FX held on its keyboard: 127) and the device pages' info line says `ARM <name>` in seafoam (`REC <name>` in red while recording; `seq_arm_label`); the SEQ page puts ` ARM` after the bar's text and `ARMED` on the `TRACK n/m` line, #179. Black key n is track n with REC or SEQ held also on the SEQ page (where the keys alone pick rows); one past the tracks says `NO TRACK n` (`seq_no_track`). A mute or an arm redraws in place (`ui_dirty` 1, `seq_track_redraw`; a mute only on the SEQ page); the held flags (`rec_held_used`, `seq_held_used`) clear at the button's release. **REC is global** (#395, [D-548](ledger/steps.md#D-548); `seq_rec` on / off, `seq_rec_press`): on any page but the SEQ page, stopped, REC's release first starts the transport as PLAY does (`play_toggle`: MIDI Start out, so the editor follows); then recording is on, the page stays. On the SEQ page, stopped, REC's release starts or ends step record instead (`sr_rec_tap`, see Step record on the FM-1), and PLAY in step record starts the transport and the recording. Running, REC records at once; again, recording ends and the transport goes on. PLAY stops both (`play_toggle` calls `seq_rec_off`), and so does any other stop (the editor's TRANSPORT, a MIDI Stop: `seq_take` ends recording when the transport is not running, [D-801](ledger/steps.md#D-801)). What records: every armed Steps track (`seq_rec_sync`, every pass in `seq_take` and at once after REC or an arm: `eng_steps_rec(m, seq_rec && armed)`), so a track armed while REC is on records from then on ([D-800](ledger/steps.md#D-800)); another patch leaves REC on. The bar says `REC <name>`, `REC n TRACKS` or `REC: NO TRACK ARMED` ([D-549](ledger/steps.md#D-549)). On the device page the keys play, K1..K4 turn the sound; on the SEQ page a drum track's black keys sound, pick and record their row, a melodic track's 27 keys are its keyboard, every C dim. REC's LED stays lit off the page (`seq_take`). REC's old SEQ-only modes (black keys recording hits with nothing armed, REC on the armed track while stopped, REC arming the roll's track) are gone. **The count-in** (#414, [D-803](ledger/steps.md#D-803); MENU > PANEL > COUNT-IN: OFF, 1 BAR, 2 BARS, off as made, byte 12 of the settings record): with it on, REC turns REC on but the tracks record only when it ends (`seq_cin`, `seq_cin_poll` every pass; `seq_rec_sync` flags no track meanwhile). Its bars are counted from the transport's start, where the Steps tracks' bars are (`eng_run_ticks`: ticks since the engine took the start, 96 a bar, -1 before), so REC while stopped counts the transport's first bars and REC while running begins at the next bar line, and recording always begins on a bar. Each beat the bar says `COUNT-IN 1`..`COUNT-IN 4` (`COUNT-IN 2.3` with two bars: bar.beat, `ui_msg`, replaced by the next beat's), the footer says `COUNT-IN <name>` (`seq_arm_label`, red) and REC's LED is on for the beat's first half (`seq_rec_lit`); the keys sound as ever. REC again meanwhile is REC off with nothing recorded, and any stop ends it as it ends a recording. It clicks on each beat with play once's click ([D-803](ledger/steps.md#D-803)): `seq_rec_press` asks `eng_count_in(seq_cin_at, bars)`, which the engine takes at the first render with the transport running and counts the same bars from `E.run_ticks` (the bar line after `at`, or the start); REC off asks it with 0 bars, and `eng_transport(0)` ends it. Edits go into the running binary (`m1_patch_notes`) and to the editor (TRACK_NOW).
  - Play once (#632, [D-820](ledger/steps.md#D-820)): **SEQ held + PLAY** (`panel.c` `play_once_press`, on PLAY's press) calls `eng_play_once`. The engine owns it, sample-timed: the main loop's request (`once_cmd`, counted by `once_gen`) is taken at the next render; the transport stops, `ONCE_BAR` (96) ticks are counted on the clock (MIDI clock's too) with a click on beat 1's first sample and on every 24th tick after it (`click_sample`: a square wave falling to nothing in `CLICK_MS`, at `CLICK_LEVEL_Q16`, `CLICK_HZ` or `CLICK_ACCENT_HZ` on beat 1). The click is not in `eng_render`'s output: the render keeps where it stands (`click_k0`) and the clicks it started (`click_ev`), and `eng_click_mix` adds them to a buffer of the same length, each side then held to full scale. `audio.c` calls it after `uac_tap` took the half for USB audio and writes the analog half again at its level, so the click is heard on the speaker and the jacks only ([D-820](ledger/steps.md#D-820)), then the engine runs its own transport (`once_run`, which `eng_transport_running` adds to the main loop's) and each track the clock steps gets `trk_once` = its passes + 2 (the song's entries, else 1), counted down at each end of a pass (`steps_turn`); at 1 it loops on. Before the modules run, `steps_once_at` foresees where in the block the last track still above 1 ends its pass (`steps_turn_at` counts its steps as `m_steps` will: the start, the half-tick step, the block's ticks); from that sample `steps_fire` plays no note on a track at 1, so a shorter track whose loop starts again on the very tick the longest ends is silent there, whatever the module order. When no track is above 1 the transport stops. `eng_transport` ends it where it is. Its own starts and stops come out of `eng_clock_out` as edges 2 / -2, which `clock_out.h` sends as MIDI Start / Stop unasked; `panel_status` follows them (the PLAY LED, `COUNT-IN n` by `eng_once_beat`, `PLAY ONCE`). `engine.ts` `playOnce` / `countIn` are the same (`modules.ts` `stepsTurnAt`; the browser has one output, so its click is added there after the clamp; golden kits `play-once`, `play-once-song`, `play-once-loop`, `rec-count-in`; `host_test.c` renders a block at a time and calls `eng_click_mix`). In the FM-1 tab the wasm calls the import `once` (REC's count-in: `cin` with its bars, from `seq_cin_asked`) and the browser engine plays it with its click (the worklet posts `run` when its transport changes, `AudioHost.onRun`); the wasm's own starts and stops of it are not told (`play_auto`).
  - FM-1 tab: wasm import `arm` → `Editor.armTrack`; export `fm1w_arm` from the editor's ARM (and after every load). Tests: `panel_test` (arm; REC from the device page starting the transport and MIDI Start, a live take there, SEQ during it, the roll's keyboard, REC off with the transport going on, PLAY stopping both, REC with nothing armed, an arm during REC, ALGO during REC, a stop from elsewhere, REC on the environment; the REC LED, disarm; `PANEL_SNAP_ROLL` / `PANEL_SNAP_ROLL_REC` / `PANEL_SNAP_TAKE` / `PANEL_SNAP_SCROUNGE` write the screens), emulator smoke `seq()` (the 808 Kit's grid, then <7PH's roll). Not on a device.
- Env L/R `shot` (format 28): one-shot, the gate only starts it, the three segments run through, then the release; every envelope of a Drums-shelf factory assembly uses it, so their decay rows work when a Steps trig is short. The drum kit cables each track's `pitch` into its drum's oscillators (kick, snare, cowbell), so a step's note tunes the hit relative to the drum (C4 = as tuned).
- Factory assembly `drum kit` (#93): the 808 Kit's six tracks and drums (the drums nested as collapsed cards, `include` in gen-assemblies.ts), two mixers; its card shows every track's grid and the drums' main settings, `clk` / `reset` reach every track. `insertAssembly` / `validateAssemblyDef` carry a Steps module's name and notes. Each drum goes through a VCA whose cv is its track's `vel` (gain 127 / 100: the default velocity plays the drum as tuned), so an accented step (full velocity) plays louder.
- Factory: the 808 Kit and 909 Kit are one DRUMS Steps track each (#138) on the transport, into a Drum map that plays their drums, and a row's velocity sets its drum's level: 127 plays 127 / 100 of a plain step (velocity 100). The 909 Kit's kick, snare and hat go through a VCA inside their assembly whose cv (its `vel` input) is the row's velocity (gain 127 / 100). The 808 Kit has no such VCA: each drum's velocity sets the gain of a VCA inside it through a macro range (p1), and the tom and rim strike through a VCA whose cv is the velocity, in place of their `hit` Scale (a macro range on so small a gain would not survive the binary's 256 range steps); its one card `808 kit` holds the drums and both mixers, with the Roland TR-808's knobs only (gen-presets.ts). The 909 Kit's rows 3 and 5..8 (clap, open hat, tom, rim, cowbell) are leaner: one card `perc`, each voice's VCA gain set by its row's velocity through a macro range (p1, 0..G), so `<7PH` fits the FM-1 ([D-337](ledger/patches-and-presets.md#D-337)). Golden kits `steps-kit` (three tracks, accent, chance, swing, len, stop / run), `steps-clk` (clk edges, reset, MIDI clock ticks then back to the tempo), `steps-notes` / `steps-tie` (notes, velocities, long notes; written as pre-#133 step bytes and migrated), `steps-keys` (chords of mixed lengths into three voices, a note cut at len, chances, transpose) and `steps-legato` (one legato voice: back-to-back notes retrigger, overlapping ones glide); the harness has `ext` / `int` / `tick` events. Emulator smoke `seq()`: the page on the 808 Kit, a step, a track, PLAY, a mute, REC. Not verified on a device.

## MIDI in and out (#64, 2026-10-18)

- In (USB and TRS): one channel (MENU > MIDI > MIDI CHANNEL, omni by default): notes (but see the key range below), pitch bend, CC 1 (mod), aftertouch, program change n = bank slot n, counted from 0 (program change 0 = slot 01; an empty slot, or while the editor's Live is on: ignored), CC 16..19 = K1..K4 (the rows of the page in focus), CC 22 = morph, CC 80..86 = presses (64 and up) and releases of the patch buttons FX..ARP, CC 123 = all notes off; clock, Start / Continue / Stop (MENU > CLOCK IN).
- Out (USB only; MENU > MIDI > MIDI OUT, on by default; TRS has no out): every panel control, the "MIDI out map" below (#115), and MIDI clock (MENU > MIDI > CLOCK OUT, "MIDI clock out" below). What came in is not sent out again. Not verified: USB is not emulated.

### MIDI out map (#115, 2026-10-19)

Every control goes out as it is touched, raw, whatever it does on the device at that moment (a host can mirror or replay
the panel); on the MIDI channel (omni: channel 1). Sent from the panel core (`panel.c` `BTN_CC`, `ENC_CC`, `panel_master`),
checked by `tools/panel_test.sh`.

| Control | Message |
|---|---|
| keys | note on (velocity 100) / note off, at the octave; while the keys reach a Drum map a black key sends its row's note, a white key nothing ([D-525](ledger/steps.md#D-525)) |
| K1..K4 | CC 16..19, absolute: the bound row's position (only on a device page, when the knob moves a row) |
| SELECT, ALGO, PRESET | CC 20, 21, 23, relative two's complement (1..63 = up by n, 127..65 = down by 128 - n) |
| MASTER | CC 7, absolute (the pot's 10 bits >> 3; changes under 6 / 1024 dropped) |
| FX SCL ENV LFO EDIT GLO ARP | CC 80..86 |
| HOME SAVE SEQ REC PLAY | CC 87, 88, 89, 90, 91 |
| OCT- OCT+ | CC 92, 93 |
| PLAY starting / stopping the transport | also Start 0xFA / Stop 0xFC (not when PLAY held + PRESET set the tempo), sent with the clock's tick 0 (below) |
| the clock (MENU > MIDI > CLOCK OUT, on by default) | Clock 0xF8, 24 a quarter note at the tempo that plays, all the time ([D-477](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-477)) |

While the quick start or the controls sheet shows, only OCT, PLAY and the sheet's HOME go out, and a release only
when its press went out ([D-245](ledger/device-ui.md#D-245)).

Buttons send 127 on the press and 0 on the release, a switching patch button too (the editor and the FM-1 itself take
CC 80..86 coming in the same way, so a switch is one press). CC 22 (morph) is in only.

### MIDI clock out (#473, 2026-11-01)

The FM-1 sends MIDI clock over USB all the time, at the tempo that plays: the global tempo while TEMPO is GLOBAL, else
Main's ([D-907](ledger/device-ui.md#D-907)), as the web app's clock out does ([D-466](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-466)).
MENU > MIDI > CLOCK OUT (K4 on the MIDI tab, on by default; settings record byte 6 bit 1 set = off, so an older record
reads as on) turns the clock off; Start / Stop still go out with PLAY. With MIDI OUT off nothing goes out. While MIDI
clock in drives the clock ([D-452](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-452)) the FM-1 sends no clock; half a second after the last
tick in, it sends its own again at its tempo ([D-477](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-477)).

- Timing (`firmware/src/clock_out.h`): the ticks are the engine's (`eng_clock_out`: the ticks the last render made and
  their samples), so they follow the audio clock, not the main loop. The audio ISR puts each in a ring of 8 with the
  time it is due: the ISR's start, plus a half buffer (128 samples, when that render begins to sound), plus the tick's
  sample. The 2 kHz timer, just before `usb_poll`, hands the due ones to the USB MIDI ring (`midi_out_event`). So a tick
  leaves within 0.5 ms of its sample sounding, whatever the render's length; the host's USB frame (1 ms) adds its own.
- Start: the engine resets the tempo's phase when the transport starts, and the tracks' first steps sound on the
  start's first sample. The clock calls that sample tick 0: PLAY's Start (0xFA) goes out in the render where the engine
  starts, followed by a tick at once, so a device that follows counts its steps from the same tick as the FM-1's tracks.
  Stop (0xFC) goes out in the render where it stops. A Start or Stop with no engine running goes out after three halves.
- Measured on the test rig against the Mac (2026-11-01, `tools/m1_link.py clock 15 BPM [run]`, CoreMIDI's timestamps,
  15 s each): at 120 bpm the period's deviation per tick averaged 0.24 ms (99 % within 0.38 ms, at most 0.44 ms) with
  the transport running; at 300 bpm 0.23 ms (99 % within 0.38 ms, at most 0.42 ms) running and 0.25 ms (99 % within
  0.40 ms) stopped. No tick was dropped and no link reply was refused for want of room (DIAG pages 116, 117).
- A flash write (SAVE, a stored preset, the settings record 5 s after a tempo change, once stopped and silent for 2 s, [D-921](ledger/device-ui.md#D-921)) stops the audio for
  its erase, and the clock with it: the measured gap was 38 ms, after which the clock goes on from where it was. The
  ticks of that gap are not made up.
- A host that stops reading: at most 8 ticks wait in the USB ring; later ones are dropped, so no burst of old ticks
  arrives when it reads again. SysEx goes first on the endpoint (`ep1_tx`): during a long editor message the ticks wait
  behind it.
- DIAG page 116 (`tools/m1_link.py clock`): ticks sent, ticks dropped, bytes waiting, the USB MIDI ring's fill, CLOCK
  OUT, MIDI OUT, whether clock in drives, whether the transport runs. `tools/m1_link.py clock SECONDS` also times the
  ticks the computer receives (CoreMIDI timestamps) and prints the period's mean and deviation.
- The editor ignores 0xF8 from an input that is an FM-1 (the linked input, or one named like an FM-1: `input.ts`
  `isFm1In`), so the browser's tempo does not follow the FM-1 unasked; Start / Stop still come in as before.
- Tests: `tools/clock_out_test.sh` (ticks counted and timed at 20..300 bpm, a tempo change, global against patch tempo,
  Start / Stop and tick 0, clock in, CLOCK OUT off, MIDI OUT off, a host that stops reading, the settings bit),
  `panel_test` (the MIDI tab's K4).

### Following the FM-1's panel, any firmware (#120, 2026-10-19)

- The page reads a connected FM-1 through plain MIDI only (`web/src/mirror.ts` `Fm1Mirror`: its own listener on the
  port; the firmware from M-VAVE's identity handshake, `fm1-profiles.ts` `firmwareOf`: FM-1_7MMmmpp ours (FM-1_7XY before 1.8), FM-1_9XY Felucca
  or Sloop, FM-1_0XX / 8XX M-VAVE; Baud Girl by hand). A profile per firmware (`PROFILES`) turns its messages into panel
  inputs: Matryoshka the map above, and its Start / Stop; M-VAVE the keys; Felucca / Sloop the keys, CC 1 (Felucca's MODW mode) as the
  modulation wheel, Start / Stop; Baud Girl the keys and K1..K4 as CC 74 / 71 / 73 / 72.
- main.ts `mirrorInput`: buttons and encoders go through Matryoshka's panel logic (the FM-1 tab's WebAssembly copy,
  `Fm1View.drive`, loaded even while the tab is hidden; its focus / page events move the editor as the emulator's do)
  and the patch buttons also through `Editor.pressButton`; K1..K4 positions through `Editor.turnKnob`; keys, the
  modulation wheel, MASTER and the transport to the page. Nothing is sent back to the FM-1 (`fromDevice`), the transport
  included ([D-457](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-457), #549): the copy gets no MIDI for the SEQ page's keys, so it can read
  SEL or REC held + a key as a tap. While following, the copy's PLAY / REC (`js_transport`) never drives the editor's
  transport; the editor's transport follows the FM-1's own Start / Stop (`setPlaying` under `fromDevice` sends no
  TRANSPORT), and `emuRealign` sets the copy back to the editor's transport 400 ms after a replayed press the FM-1 did not
  confirm. PRESET is not followed while Live is on (the FM-1 then plays the editor's patch; its slots are its own).
- The panel's reports (#549, INFO feature bit 18, [D-481](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-481), [D-482](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-482)):
  after HELLO the FM-1 sends KEYS_NOW 0x57 for every key edge `panel_key` takes (main.c `m1_keys_now` queues it with
  `keys_now` and `panel_held`, `keys_report` sends it in the loop; only with MENU > MIDI OUT on) and GUIDE_NOW 0x58 with
  `guide_state` (help_ui.c, 14 bytes: the sheet's state, page, flags, focus, track, menu tab, buttons held, the rows'
  conditions, sheet row, octave, step record's cursor) after HELLO and whenever it changes (`guide_poll`: the
  conditions are asked only when the rest or `help_state_sig` changed). The editor (`panelReported`: the followed
  FM-1 is the link's, with the bit) puts KEYS_NOW through the copy (`Fm1View.keysNow`: the reported held buttons first,
  as presses or releases, then `Fm1Ui.keys`); `drive` then plays a button's MIDI only when it is news (`fm1w_held`), and
  the FM-1's notes skip `EmuKeys`. The copy's own track / lock / pattern / song / mute results during the replay are
  dropped (the FM-1 reports its own). 250 ms after the last GUIDE_NOW, KEYS_NOW or button MIDI / turn the copy took
  (`alignSoon`, `web/src/panel-align.ts`: GUIDE_NOW comes after the redraw a page change causes, the button's MIDI at
  once) `panelOffBy` compares the last GUIDE_NOW with the copy's `fm1w_guide`; where they differ `fm1w_align` (panel.c `panel_align`, built with `PANEL_ALIGN`) sets the copy
  there without playing an edge. `main.ts` keeps the last GUIDE_NOW in `fm1Panel` for the controls strip (#554, `web/src/controls-strip.ts`).
- Under Live a structural change uploads the patch only when its encoded bytes differ from what the FM-1 holds
  (`web/src/upload-gate.ts`, main.ts `structureChanged`): a comment or a layout change sends nothing. Edits sent live
  bring the gate's copy up to date 250 ms after they stop (`liveEdited`); until then a structural change uploads. A change
  that came from the FM-1 (`fromDevice`: it changed its own patch, a preset loaded there) forgets the bytes, so the next
  structural change uploads. Opening a patch always uploads it (`onLoad`).
- The Emulator view shows a "Mirroring your FM-1" marker over the panel (its tooltip names the firmware) and greys the
  controls that firmware does not report (`Fm1View.following`).
- The FM-1's CC on the Live link are not used by the page. A knob's value arrives as its row's 7-bit position (CC 16..19),
  not the exact value, and turns K1..K4 of the editor's page in focus; except under Live with the FM-1 on the editor's
  patch (`exactKnobs` in main.ts, #185): then PARAM_NOW (module, parameter, value) moves the parameter and FOCUS_NOW the
  page (the editor and the mirror's panel copy), so a turn cannot land on another row when the two disagree on the page.
- **LIVE** (#185, protocol 0x2C, feature bit 6): every 2 s while Live is on (`LIVE_MS`) the editor tells the FM-1; paused
  (the patch over the budget) it is sandbox mode (below); until 5 s
  after the last (`LIVE_HOLD_MS`, main.c `editor_live`) PRESET and program change load no slot (`EDITOR LIVE: PATCH STAYS`),
  so the FM-1 cannot leave the editor's patch under it. An FM-1 without LIVE (1.0.0) still can: its SLOT_NOW then ends
  `exactKnobs` until the next upload.
- Connect FM-1 (it answers HELLO, so it runs Matryoshka) also starts following it; FM-1 & MIDI > Follow the FM-1 does
  it for any firmware, with a firmware list (detect, or by hand).

### Sandbox mode: the FM-1 as a sandbox patch's controller (2026-10-24)

Rows: [D-467](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-467) (the mode), [D-468](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-468) (the message, INFO),
[D-232](ledger/device-ui.md#D-232) (the FM-1's screen and controls). Not checked on a device.

- **When.** The editor's patch is over the FM-1's budget (`deviceBudget(p).fits === false`) while it is connected with
  Live on: `liveSync.sandbox` (device.ts). LIVE keeps its paused flag (byte 1). With Live off, the sync line's Sync
  (`syncLine`: "Sandbox patch, not synced") starts it, and so does the connect offer's Sync
  ([D-460](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-460)). It ends when the patch fits (Live then uploads as before), when Live is
  turned off, or, on the FM-1, 5 s after the last LIVE (`SB_HOLD_MS`, the same as `LIVE_HOLD_MS`).
- **Editor** (main.ts `sandboxCheck`, called by `syncChip` on every link, Live and budget change): entering it starts
  following the FM-1's panel (the mirror, `followFm1('matryoshka')`) unless it already follows, and leaving it stops what
  it started (a user's Stop following wins). One transport: entering it runs both the editor's and the FM-1's if
  either ran (`liveSync.sandboxRuns`; `DeviceLink.running` is the FM-1's last MIDI Start / Stop or TRANSPORT), and the
  editor's PLAY and Space send TRANSPORT in sandbox mode as under Live (`liveSync.transports`), so a sequence the FM-1 was
  playing goes on in the browser and the FM-1's PLAY stays in step. Its knobs come as CC 16..19 positions (`Editor.turnKnob`); SELECT / ALGO move
  the editor's own focus (`sandboxStep`, wrapping as the FM-1 does: the firmware UI's WebAssembly copy may have refused a
  patch this big); the buttons go through that copy as before, the keys to the browser engine (the FM-1 sends them as plain notes; `web/src/sandbox-keys.ts` plays them as the FM-1's keys play the editor's patch: a Drum map's rows on black keys 1..8, [D-958](ledger/editor.md#D-958)); PRESET is not followed,
  but PRESET turned while the FM-1's PLAY is held (`fm1PlayHeld`, from PLAY's CC) moves Main's tempo a bpm a click within
  20..300 (`sandboxTempoTurn`) and tells the WebAssembly copy that PLAY was turned (`fm1w_play_turned`), so PLAY's
  release starts and stops nothing there either. Every `SANDBOX_MS` (80 ms) `sandboxSend` builds the page in focus
  (`sandbox-mode.ts` `sandboxPage`: the device, its page as the FM-1's chips title it, the page's place, four rows with
  the value and unit as the dock shows them, the position 0..1 and the choice count; then the place for the FM-1's header
  (the devices, the page groups, the group's pages), Main's tempo, unsaved edits (`unsavedChanges`, read at most every
  500 ms) and the armed devices) and sends it when `sandboxKey` differs from the last one sent (a fresh sandbox mode or
  reconnect sends it again). The FM-1 chip: `FM-1 · SANDBOX CONTROLLER` in gold (`sandboxChip`); with MENU > MIDI OUT reported off,
  "turn on MIDI OUT (GLO, K3) to control the sandbox"; with firmware older than 1.3 (no feature bit 8) its hover asks for
  the update (that FM-1 keeps its sound and shows `EDITOR PATCH TOO BIG`, and the CC 16..19 it sends are its own rows'
  positions, so a knob jumps the editor's row as on any Live-off follow).
- **Protocol.** SANDBOX 0x31 (host to device, no reply, feature bit 8; `protocol.ts` `sandboxFrame` / `parseSandbox`,
  `firmware/src/sandbox_msg.h` `sb_parse`): rows u8 (bit k: row k), index u8, count u8, patch 20, device 12, page 16, then
  four rows of label 12, value 10, unit 6, position u8 (0..255), steps u8 (0 continuous, n choices); names zero padded
  ASCII (`fm1Ascii`: a minus sign becomes `-`). 171 bytes. Then, from 1.8 ([D-468](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-468)),
  13 more (`SB_EXT_BYTES`): devices u8, device u8, groups u8, group u8, pages u8, page u8 (counts, and places from 0),
  tempo u16 (bpm, 0 none), flags u8 (bit 0 unsaved edits), armed u32 (bit d: device d). 184 bytes, 217 on the wire.
  Firmware before 1.8 reads the first 171 and ignores the rest; from an editor before 1.8 `sb_guess` fills them in: no
  device or group dashes, the group's pages from the title's "1/2", no tempo ("--"), KEYS WIRED not shown. INFO grows a seventh byte (feature bits
  8..15; `M1_FEATURES2` = 1: bit 8) and flag bit 2 = MENU > MIDI OUT on (`Info.midiOut`, null without bit 8); the FM-1
  sends INFO unasked after a LIVE when MIDI OUT changed since it last told (live.c `live_mout_told`), and `DeviceLink`
  takes an unasked INFO as an `info` event. Tests: `protocol.test.ts`, proto_test (`tools/golden.sh` feeds it the frame
  golden.ts writes, `proto-sandbox.syx`).
- **Firmware** (`sandbox_ui.c`, live.c, panel.c, audio.c). `m1_live(1)` turns sandbox mode on (`sb_on`); audio.c's
  `snd_muted` zeroes the output (the FM-1's own patch keeps running, unheard). Entering it brings page 0 up
  (`sb_home_due`, panel.c `panel_status`) from any screen but the menu and GLO, so the SEQ page does not stay up. Page 0 draws `sandbox_draw` instead of the
  device page (ui.c `view_draw`, `header_draw`), as a device page ([D-232](ledger/device-ui.md#D-232),
  [D-234](ledger/device-ui.md#D-234)): the device page's header from the editor's device, the page's group (its title
  without "1/2") and the group's pages with their dashes (`sb_head`, which pages_ui.c `head_layout` calls), the rows through
  pages_ui.c's `row_bar` in the device page's geometry, and the device page's footer on gold (`foot_row`, `sb_foot`):
  SANDBOX where the patch's name goes (`SANDBOX *` with unsaved edits, `KEYS WIRED` after it when the editor says nothing
  is armed), the editor's tempo, the octave and the battery; before the first page an empty header and a card. A page
  redraws only the rows and header sections that changed, and the footer when what it shows changed. Before the first
  page K1..K4, SELECT and ALGO change nothing on the FM-1 (its muted patch is never edited). K1..K4
  (`sandbox_knob`): from the position the editor sent, a choice a click (steps > 1) or 1/127 (5/127 fast), sent as CC
  16..19, the bar moving at once; the editor's next page does not take a turned knob's position back for 400 ms
  (`SB_ECHO_MS`). PLAY held + PRESET (`sandbox_tempo`) shows `TEMPO n` and the footer's tempo from the editor's tempo and
  PRESET's clicks (1 bpm a click, 20..300; the editor adds the same clicks from PRESET's CC), held for `SB_ECHO_MS` the
  same way; with no tempo from the editor it says `SANDBOX: TEMPO IN EDITOR`, and the FM-1's own tempo never moves. Only
  OCT, HOME, PLAY, GLO and the menu act on the FM-1 (`SB_OWN_BTNS`); every control still goes out as
  MIDI first (with MENU > MIDI OUT on). The keys play no Drum map and arm or mute nothing. PRESET waits
  (`SANDBOX: PATCH STAYS`). Tests: panel_test.c (mute on / off, the card, knobs inert before the first page, an older
  editor's page, the new fields, the page drawn in place against the page drawn whole, the tempo, the knobs, the
  buttons, the lapse, the page coming up), `tools/ui_preview.sh` with `SANDBOX=1` / `SANDBOX=2` (`MIDIOUT=0`, `SAVED=1`,
  `ARMED=1`) / `SANDBOX=3` (an older editor's page).

## Voicing key range: channel, low, high (#57 decision 13, 2026-10-18)

- The Voice allocator has `channel` (all, 1..16), `low` and `high` (note names, C-1..G9; defaults all / C-1 / G9). A group takes a key when it lies in low..high and, for keys from the keyboard or MIDI, it comes on the allocator's channel, or with `all` on the device's channel (MENU > MIDI CHANNEL; every channel in the browser). The rule is `takesKey` (patch.ts) and `takes_key` (engine.c). The shared keyboard (Main pitch / gate / vel) and the Arpeggiators play the device's channel only. An arpeggiated allocator's range judges the Arpeggiator's notes (the arpeggiator itself takes every key of the device's channel); its channel is not used. Note offs follow the same rule, so an off on another channel does not end a note.
- Splits and layers: two allocators with low / high side by side split the keyboard; allocators on channels 2, 3 ... make the FM-1 multitimbral over MIDI. Every note-on message now reaches the engine whatever MENU > MIDI CHANNEL says (main.c `midi_drain`); the other channel messages still obey it. The panel's 27 keys play on the device's channel (1 when omni).
- An allocator with a channel or range always gets a poly group of its own (deviceView), even with one voice. Binary format 25: the group record (`GROUP_REC` / `ENG_GROUP_REC` 48 bytes) ends with u8 channel (0 = the device's), u8 low, u8 high; `ENG_PATCH_COMPAT_VERSION` 31 today (older stored patches fall back to the fallback patch). Browser: Web MIDI passes the channel (`input.ts`, worklet `on` / `off` `ch`); `Engine.channel` is the device channel (0).
- Tests: `keyrange.test.ts`; golden kit `alloc-split` (three groups: below C4 on any channel, C4 up on channel 2, channel 3; notes on channels 1..4; a mutated C channel test fails it). Not verified on a device.

## Settings menu and manual (#66, 2026-10-18)

- GLO opens the menu on its MIDI tab (#395; HOME held opens nothing). ALGO steps the tabs: MIDI (MIDI CHANNEL, CLOCK IN, MIDI OUT), PANEL (LEDS, KNOB ACCEL, COUNT-IN: REC's count-in, [D-803](ledger/steps.md#D-803), TEMPO: GLOBAL or PATCH, [D-907](ledger/device-ui.md#D-907)) and SYSTEM (UPDATE MODE = update-only mode, FACTORY RESET, QUICK START: the quick start after power-on, [D-240](ledger/device-ui.md#D-240), OUTPUT: STEREO, MONO or MONO L, #612, [D-918](ledger/device-ui.md#D-918): MONO puts (L + R) / 2 on both sides of the analog output, MONO L on the left side (the tip) only and silences the right, [D-919](ledger/device-ui.md#D-919), folded in `fm1_alnk0_irq` after USB audio took the half in stereo; `out_mono` 0..2, settings record byte 4 bits 2..3; then, one ALGO step further, SYSTEM 2/2 ([D-923](ledger/device-ui.md#D-923), `menu_sys2`): EDITOR, ALLOW or IGNORE, #702, [D-484](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-484), `cfg.editor_ignore`, settings record byte 4 bit 4, reported in INFO's flags bit 3 with feature bit 19 and in an unasked INFO when it changes after a HELLO, main.c `editor_poll`; the editor's Connect and Install panel skip an FM-1 at IGNORE, except the test tools' page, `rigTools` in protocol.ts; no USB level setting, [D-927](ledger/device-ui.md#D-927): USB audio is the half at MASTER's level, settings record byte 4 bits 5..7 unread; 3.5MM OUT, #631, [D-925](ledger/device-ui.md#D-925): ON or OFF, `out_off`, settings record byte 6 bit 2; OFF zeroes the analog half last in `fm1_alnk0_irq`, after USB audio took it, and draws a red speaker with an X left of the footer's battery, `OFF_MARK` in pages_ui.c; HOME + OCT- + OCT+ held 2 s on any page switches it too, panel.c `out_combo`, and with HOME held first the OCT pair does not open the controls sheet) are pages of settings on K1..K4, drawn as a device page with the tab chips under the bars; INFO lists VERSION, CPU now, CPU PEAK, BANK SLOTS n OF 64, BATTERY (the smoothed reading 0..1023 and its bars n/3, or USB; [D-229](ledger/device-ui.md#D-229)) and PROCESSOR (2 CORES or 1 CORE, [D-377](ledger/engine.md#D-377)); GUIDE is one text SELECT scrolls, the ideas behind the controls ([D-248](ledger/device-ui.md#D-248)). UPDATE MODE and FACTORY RESET: a turn right arms the row for 5 s, GLO held 2 s does it; SELECT, OCT- and OCT+ have no other job in the menu, PRESET steps patches, PLAY held + PRESET sets the tempo as everywhere ([D-914](ledger/device-ui.md#D-914)), ENV opens the environment (HOME comes back to the tab, [D-249](ledger/device-ui.md#D-249)); SAVE saves and closes it, a HOME tap or a GLO tap goes back out.
- PLAY acts on its release; PLAY held + PRESET sets the Main module's tempo, the global tempo while TEMPO is GLOBAL (1 bpm a click, x5 when turned fast), refused while MIDI clock drives the clock; such a release does not start / stop.

## Controls sheet and quick start (#450, 2026-10-31)

- `firmware/src/help_ui.c`, [D-245](ledger/device-ui.md#D-245) to [D-247](ledger/device-ui.md#D-247). Both are a card 16 px in from the screen's edges (x and y 16..223) with a 2 px sky edge. The device has no framebuffer, so the page under a card is not read back: while `lcd_ovl` is set, `lcd_fill` and `lcd_line_out` (`firmware/src/lcd_ovl.h`, over lcd.c's and host_ui.h's `_raw` versions) dim everything the pages draw to 40 % and keep it off the card's square, so the page goes on drawing itself, partial redraws too, as a dim frame, and never paints over the card. The card draws with `lcd_ovl_self`. The page's texts under a card are not reported to SCREEN (screen_text.h); the card's are. Opening or closing a card redraws the page whole (`ui_full`).
- **The controls table** ([D-913](ledger/device-ui.md#D-913), #553): every control of the FM-1 is a row of `web/src/controls.ts`: its gesture (the button held, then a button, an encoder, K1..K4 or a key; alternatives; tap, held, hold 2 s), the sheet's words (`does`; `doesIn` other words on one state's tab; `plain` the words off the sheet where `does` leans on the rows around it), the states whose tab lists it and its order there (`sheet`), the states whose EVERYWHERE tab lists it (`everywhere`), `listed: false` for a row the sheet leaves out because a more universal one does the same ([D-916](ledger/device-ui.md#D-916)), a condition (`when`, a function `hc_<name>` in help_ui.c) and its effect. `npm run export-defs` (`web/scripts/controls-h.ts`) writes `firmware/src/controls.h`: the `HS_*` states and their names, each tab's lines in order (`CTL_SHEET`, `CTL_LINE`), the conditions (`HC_FN`) and, under `CTL_GESTURES`, each row's gesture and effect and the lines of the rows the sheet leaves out (`CTL_UNLISTED`). It refuses a line wider than the card (FONT_S's advances from font_bc.h, `help_row`'s layout), a tab that could hold more than 24 lines, and a condition help_ui.c does not define; CI checks controls.h is committed. `firmware/test/controls_test.h` (in `tools/panel_test.sh`) puts the FM-1 in each state that lists a row or where an unlisted row works (fixtures per state, some with variants: a Page button's list, a 32-step track, a menu tab), checks that the sheet shows the row there (an unlisted row: that its condition holds), drives its gesture and checks its effect (`ct_effect`); `CT_DEBUG=<row id>` prints each run. The handlers (panel.c, seq_ui.c, menu.c) do not read the table: moving them onto it, a page at a time, is #552. From the same table: the pages' hint lines (`HINTS`: each line its rows' `hint` words, two spaces apart, written as `HINT_<ID>` into controls.h with the width it is checked against; the SEQ page's line under the grid and its track line, the SONG page's two lines, the menu's line on every tab), the editor's ? card section "On the FM-1" (`controls-help.ts`, at start: the notes `FM1_NOTES`, then Everywhere, then each state, each row's `help`, else its `plain` or sheet words), `FIXED_DOES` (modules.ts, `fixedDoes`: the dock and the button editor) and the README's list of controls (between its `controls` markers, written by export-defs; CI checks it with the headers).
- **Controls sheet:** OCT- + OCT+ (`panel_edges`: an OCT pressed while the other is held, HOME not held: HOME + OCT- + OCT+ is 3.5MM OUT's 2 s hold, `out_combo`). Its header has two tabs: the state's (`help_state`: DEVICE, ENVIRONMENT, DRUM STEPS, PIANO ROLL, LIVE TAKE, SONG, SETTINGS, SANDBOX, STEP RECORD) and EVERYWHERE; the shown one is sky at 75 %. Rows are specs (`help_rows`, a loop over controls.h's lines of the tab; parts `k:` keycap, `n:` grey knob and name, `c:` numbered K1..K4 knobs, `w:` / `b:` a white / black key (`b:N`: BLACK N), `t:` grey words, `m:` bright words, `~` then the description at x 124), 19 px apart, 8 fit; SELECT scrolls by whole rows, ALGO right / left picks EVERYWHERE / the state's tab, HOME closes it. Rows that do not apply are left out: PRESET without presets for the focused device, ENV + SAVE on a device that is no assembly, SEL on one that takes no keys, OCT 17+ on a track of 16 steps, REC / SEQ + black key without Steps tracks, the menu's SELECT / K1..K4 / GLO hold on the tabs where they act. A patch button (FX, LFO, EDIT, ARP) is listed by its job: a Toggle `name on/off`, a Page button `name pages`, a Gate with a cable from its jack `name held` (or `on/off` when it switches), the name the patch's label in lower case. A row's words may hold `{list}` (`LIST_MARK`): `help_fill` puts in the Page button whose list shows, as the top bar names it (its label, or its name when the line would not fit the card); export-defs checks the line with each of FX, LFO, EDIT and ARP, and the editor's help and the README read "the Page button". The sheet follows the state (`help_follow`: state, focused device, Page list, menu tab, track, track length, patch).
- While a card shows, `panel_encoders` and `panel_edges` hand everything to `help_knobs_turn` / `help_edges`: PLAY starts and stops, the note keys play (on the SEQ page they play notes instead of entering steps; REC / SEQ + black key does nothing), every other control does nothing; a button let go ends what it held (`panel_release_quiet`: a Gate's jack, a Page button's hold). MIDI out: only OCT, PLAY and (on the sheet) HOME go out, and a release only if its press did (`midi_btn_sent`), so a mirror of the panel opens and closes its own sheet and nothing reaches the editor in sandbox mode.
- **Quick start:** after power-on (main.c, not in safe mode or update mode) while `cfg.quick`. HOME closes it, OCT- + OCT+ opens the sheet in its place, HOME + SAVE closes it, clears `cfg.quick` and writes the settings record at once (`settings_store`), and the bar says `QUICK START OFF: GLO>SYSTEM` (27 characters: `bar_text` takes 28). A boot message (FACTORY BANK WRITTEN and the like) waits for the card to close (`help_msg`). MENU > SYSTEM > QUICK START turns it on or off; FACTORY RESET (`cfg_defaults`) turns it on.
- Previews: `HELP=quick` or `HELP=sheet` (`HELP_TAB=1`, `HELP_TOP=n`) with `tools/ui_preview.sh`; tests: `firmware/test/help_test.h` in `tools/panel_test.sh`, the smoke's first boot (tools/emu/src/smoke.rs).
- **Nudges** ([D-249](ledger/device-ui.md#D-249)): `ui_msg` in the header, at most 26 characters, `NUDGE_MS` (1.5 s), only from a button or a knob (a key edge never draws, [D-228](ledger/device-ui.md#D-228): a key in sandbox mode sets `sb_mout_due` and `panel_status` shows it). A patch button with no job (`panel_btn_job`, ui.c: not a Toggle or Page button and no cable from its jack; the controls sheet leaves the same ones out) names itself (`EDIT: NOT USED IN PATCH`; FX and LFO keep their SEQ and SONG page jobs there); PRESET stepping patches with `bank_mask` 0 says `BANK EMPTY` (`patch_turn`); OCT on a SEQ page whose track has 16 steps or fewer says `ALL 16 STEPS SHOWN`; SEL on a device whose keys input no cable reaches says `NO KEYS REACH THIS DEVICE` (`dev_keys_input`, pages_ui.c: a Drum map with no keys source, or an engine the editor marks: a Voicing no cable reaches is not in the binary, so the device extras' track byte says `ENG_KEYS_UNREACHED` 0xFE, or with a Steps track driving it through other cables the track's index with `ENG_KEYS_UNREACHED_TRACK` (bit 7, #633, [D-295](ledger/patch-format.md#D-295)), patchbin.ts `keysUnreached`, [D-905](ledger/device-ui.md#D-905); `env_listeners` arms nothing for such an engine, a poly one included, whose voice group stays without its Voicing, and the editor arms it neither, arm.ts `noKeysReach`, [D-818](ledger/steps.md#D-818)); in sandbox mode SAVE, REC, SEQ, ENV and SEL (`SB_NUDGE_BTNS`) say `SANDBOX: SEQ IN EDITOR` and the like (SEL: `SANDBOX: ARM IN EDITOR`), from the menu too, where SAVE keeps its job, and with MENU > MIDI OUT off any control that only reaches the editor says `TURN ON MIDI OUT: GLO, K3` (also when sandbox mode begins). ENV in the menu opens the environment; GLO's press sets the menu's tab (`menu_open`), so HOME back from the environment keeps the tab. Tests: `firmware/test/nudge_test.h` (`NUDGE_SNAP=dir` writes the screens).
- **GUIDE** ([D-248](ledger/device-ui.md#D-248)): the menu's last tab, `manual.c`, the ideas of the vocabulary, no controls but its first two lines; panel_test.c checks its lines (35 characters, capitals, no `;`, no control named, a heading per idea).

## Editor link both ways (#65, 2026-10-18)

- **Live sync is the default on connect (#171).** Connecting (FM-1 & MIDI's Connect or the Connect FM-1 button at the main area's top right) runs `offerSync` (main.ts): `syncOnConnect` (device.ts, pure) gives `too-big` (the patch does not fit: no question, Live stays off), `off` (Not now was chosen in this visit: `sessionStorage['matryoshka.sync-declined']`) or `ask` (the `#sync-ask` dialog: Sync, Enter; Not now, Esc). The FM-1's patch cannot be read (SLOT_INFO gives a slot's name and length, not its data): main.ts passes no device binary, so a patch that fits always asks, and "Open the FM-1's patch" is left out. `syncOnConnect` also has a `sync` result (the FM-1's patch equals the editor's binary: on without asking) that nothing reaches until the patch can be read (#176). A reconnect with Live on sends the patch again. The FM-1 chip (`chipState`) reads FM-1 · not connected / synced / not synced / patch too big to sync, or `FM-1 · SANDBOX CONTROLLER` in sandbox mode ("Sandbox mode" above); a click while not synced offers sync, otherwise opens FM-1 & MIDI. FM-1 & MIDI has no checkbox (#180): its sync line shows the chip's state (`syncLine`, device.ts: "Synced with the FM-1 · Stop syncing", "Not synced · Sync", a sandbox patch's "Sync" into sandbox mode and its "Stop syncing", or the text alone while not connected), and its button calls the same `setLive` (the one switch; the state is `liveOn` in main.ts).
- The FM-1 tells the editor which slot it plays: SysEx SLOT_NOW (0x43: slot, name) whenever it loads a slot (HOME held + PRESET, program change, boot) or SAVEs. Its K1..K4 and patch buttons reach the editor as the USB MIDI CC of the MIDI out map (MENU > MIDI OUT on). With Live on, its knob turns move the rows of the editor's page in focus (CC 16..19), or arrive as PARAM_NOW while the FM-1 plays the editor's patch ([D-457](ledger/link-and-midi.md#D-457)), without being sent back and presses its patch buttons (`Editor.pressButton`: a switch flips on the press); the status line names the slot.
- The editor's patch buttons go to the FM-1 as CC 80..86 presses / releases (a switch: one press and release); its knob turns as PARAM as before. STORE writes the running patch into the slot the FM-1 plays; "Store to an FM-1 slot…" (palette) writes the open patch into any slot (SLOT command) without switching to it: its dialog (store-slot.ts, #635) lists the 64 slots read with SLOT_INFO, each with its patch's name or EMPTY, and offers the first empty one; the Library's bank upload writes all 64.
- Not verified on a device: USB is not emulated.

## Device pages: the knobs follow the focus (2026-10-19, branch device-pages)

Decided with the user (design canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/R8qCCtaZ8RghG1QRYeahgV): every top-level module or assembly
of the canvas is a **device** the FM-1 plays; its card's rows are its **pages**, four rows each, and K1..K4 turn the rows
of the page in focus. Assemblies hide the inside: what the card shows is what the player gets.

- Model (`web/src/pages.ts`, tests `pages.test.ts`): `devicesOf(patch)` = Main, then each Steps track followed by the devices it drives, then the other top-level modules
  (not Part, editor-only) and assemblies with rows in Tidy's reading order (columns from the left, half a card of slack,
  then from the top), the Output last (the grouped order, [D-217](ledger/device-ui.md#D-217); "The environment's order" above). An assembly's rows are its promoted parameters; a heading (`ParamRef.head`) starts a **section** (rows
  before the first heading take the assembly's name), a section holds **groups** of at most `PAGE_ROWS` (4) rows and each
  group is one page ("Filter 1/2" = the section's first group). `groupStarts` decides them: groups fill in fours, a
  `ParamRef.group` flag (a page break set by hand in Arrange mode), a `ParamRef.pin` flag (a page start Arrange kept so a
  move did not reflow the pages around it; no ×) or a column break ends one early (the flags belong to the position like
  `col` / `head`), a display does not count and starts the next group when the last is full. Cards draw a dashed divider above a
  group that has no heading or column of its own (`.erow.gstart`). A plain module's rows are its parameters (not
  `onScreen`), four to a page (`moduleRows`, the FM-1 makes the same pages itself), labelled with the long name where it
  fits `NAME_MAX` (12).
  An envelope's display row (adsr / env `view`) puts its shape on the page holding its rows (`Page.display`).
- Main's knobs are **edit bindings**, not signals: the Main module lost the outputs and parameters k1..k6 (format 29).
  `migrateKnobs` (`knob-migrate.ts`, run by `validatePatch`) turns an old patch's knob cables into a "Macros" assembly of
  **Macro** modules (new type, `value` 0..1, one output; C: `m_const`), one per knob used, at the knob's old value and
  with its old panel label as the row label, placed top left (everything else moves a column right when that spot is
  taken), so it is the first device: the old performance page. The factory patches no longer route knobs (gen-presets.ts):
  their instruments' cards are their pages. The tutorial's knob steps teach the focus and the Macro module instead.
- Editor (editor.ts): `Editor.devices` / `focus` (`fixFocus`), the dock's K1..K4 (`boundKnob`, dials at the row's
  position in its range; choices and switches snap) and two pickers DEVICE / PAGE (`fillFocus`) in place of K5 / K6. A
  press on any row (`focusRow`, capture phase) puts its page on the knobs. The bound rows get a ring and a numbered badge
  in the knob's colour (`markFocus`, `.param.bar.bound`); while an assembly is arranged every row shows its knob and
  where its pages start (`.page-start`). Dotted lines from the dials to the rows (`positionBindings`, `svg.bind-cables`,
  over everything; a row out of view gets none). MIDI CC 16..19 turn the bound rows (`Editor.turnKnob`, from main.ts's
  sink). The knob ticks under sliders (knob-rest.ts) are gone with the knob outputs.
- **Main dock: button block (#113, design board D):** the knob column is K1..K4 with their captions, then the DEVICE / PAGE
  pickers under them; each dial's binding wire starts at a fixed `.knob-jack` on the dock's top edge (ring + core in the
  knob colour, a faint stem down to the dial; `pointer-events: none`, not a port). The button block is the FM-1's two rows
  in device order (`dock-buttons.ts` `FM1_BUTTON_ROWS`): FX SCL ENV LFO EDIT GLO / HOME SAVE ARP SEQ PLAY REC. HOME SAVE
  SEQ REC are greyed and disabled (`.panel-btn.device`), PLAY is the transport switch (`#arp-btn`, Space). The seven
  patch-button jacks (`.dock-button-jacks`) share the grid's columns plus a 12 px lane right of REC for ARP's jack
  (`jackColumn`); gold traces (`svg.button-traces`, `Editor.positionTraces` from `positionCables`, `tracePath`) run
  straight up from the top row, and from ARP up into the gap between the rows, right past REC and up the lane.
- Knob colours are the logo's (style.css `--k1..--k6`: coral, gold, teal / seafoam, sand, lilac, blue), every mark with
  its number. Cables are drawn by the **role of the input** they land on (`cable-role.ts`): into a signal input the
  accent, bold (`role-signal`); into a CV input, a pin or a thumb, and into `pm` / `side`, the secondary colour, lighter
  (`role-mod`); into a gate / trigger / clock input a hollow rail in the gate colour (`role-gate`); into a keys input a
  three-wire ribbon in the ink colour (`role-keys`). The rail's core and the ribbon's core and centre wire are extra
  paths over the cable (`.cable-core`, `.cable-wire`, `pointer-events: none`), made in `Editor.drawCables` for the
  cable and its dock and card-entry copies, positioned with it. A route the user made inside a card (a side input to a
  parameter, `.cable.alias`) is solid too; a module's built-in link (`.cable.alias.fixed`) stays dashed and muted.
  **The rule (#156): solid = you made it (saved in the patch, has undo); dashed = the app is showing you something** (a
  knob target, the armed track, a cable mid-drag, a built-in link). Dashed lines march toward the receiving end
  (`@keyframes cable-march`, one dash period per cycle, off with `prefers-reduced-motion`). Assembly members get a
  tinted border, not a dashed one.
- Binary format 29 (`patchbin.ts`): header flag bit 5 = a device pages section after the step values, before the macro
  ranges (which stay last: proto.c edits them in place). Per device a kind byte: 0 = a plain module (its index; the
  FM-1 makes its pages from defs.h), 1 = an assembly with its name, pages (name, part, parts, envelope module + 1, row
  count) and rows (module index, parameter index, label), texts length-prefixed. A Voice allocator's rows (voices,
  assign, legato, priority, unison, steal) name its group setting: in the device form the allocator is its group's Part, and
  the parameter byte is 0x80 + the field (`POLY_ROW_PARAMS`). `binaryPage` / `editorPage` map the editor's focus to the
  binary's indices. `eng_devices_end` (engine.h) walks the section; `eng_load` skips it.
- Page row extras (format 35, #357, #358): bit 6 of a row's parameter byte (`ROW_MORE`, engine.h `ENG_ROW_MORE`; on a
  poly row only from format 51, below) says the row's extras follow its label: u8 n and n (module index, parameter index) pairs, the further
  parameters the row sets, then u8 k and k texts, its positions' names in capitals. A row without them is written as
  before, so every binary written before them is unchanged. The FM-1 keeps where each row's extras start (`ppage_t.xo`,
  an offset into the running binary): K1..K4 and MIDI CC 16..19 write the row's value to each further parameter too
  (`pages_also`, after the first; PARAM_NOW names only the first, the editor sets the rest), a preset loaded on the FM-1
  does the same (`dp_load`, `dev_row_more`), and a named position shows its name for the value (`row_name`). A Selector
  module's `pos` row spans 1..count on the FM-1 (`row_top`: the knobs, CC, the bar and MIDI out), as on the card.
- A VOICES row's top (format 51, [D-870](ledger/patches-and-presets.md#D-870)): on a poly row the extras are one byte, the
  most voices the FM-1's VOICES row offers (`ParamRef.top` on a Voice allocator's voices card row, `PageRow.top`,
  `patchbin.ts` `polyTop`; written only with a top, which makes the patch format 51, `uses111`). `gen-presets` sets it
  (`factory-build.ts` `voiceTops`) to the most voices with which the factory patch still fits the FM-1's budget, when that
  is under eight: Cassia 1's is 6. The FM-1 keeps it at `ppage_t.xo` as other extras (`row_more` gives none for a poly
  row); `poly_top` is the top of the knobs, MIDI CC 16..19, the bar and MIDI out, and `dp_load` holds a preset's voices to
  it (`dev_poly_top`). The editor and the browser engine do not read it.
- Protocol: FOCUS 0x29 (host → device: show a page), PARAM_NOW 0x47 (a row turned on the FM-1: module, parameter, Q16)
  and FOCUS_NOW 0x48 (the page the FM-1 shows) from the device; INFO feature bit 4. With Live on the editor follows the
  FM-1's page and turns, and sends its own focus; an upload is followed by the focus.
- Firmware (`firmware/src/pages_ui.c`): the device page is page 0. The layout is michael glaviano's design of 2026-10-24
  ([D-234](ledger/device-ui.md#D-234)); every number of it is one of the `LK_*` constants at the top of the drawing code
  (point depth 18, header 36, footer 15, tab at 2, header strength 15 %, edge 2, stack gap and inset 3, value 26 px,
  the toast 60 / 6 / 32 / -5 / 6 / 1 / 5), and the type sizes are fonts of their own (`tools/gen_font.py`: `FONT_F` 14,
  `FONT_H` 16, `FONT_N` 20, `FONT_V` 26, `FONT_T` 32, capitals, digits and signs; a check in pages_ui.c fails the build
  when a constant and its font disagree). Text sits as the mock's canvas puts it: a text's middle at the mock's y, its
  baseline a third of its size below (`BASE6`).
  - The header (y 0..35, `head_row`, a scanline at a time): three sections, the device | its page group | the group's
    pages, widths 2 : 3 : 1 of the screen. A section whose name needs more (its width + 14, + the point for the second;
    the third 12 + the point) grows and the others give the rest up evenly, never below their own need; two names too
    long for the screen together are cut, the longer first (`head_layout`). The first two sections end in a point 18 px
    deep at mid-height, the next one notched to match, a 2.5 px band of the strip colour between (the edges blended over
    the pixel they cross). Fills: lilac over night at 26 / 17 / 10 % times the header strength / 45 %. The names in
    ivory; under them a dash per device (the current gold) and per page group (the current sky), the others the
    section's lilac at 14 % more. The third section stacks one bar per page of the group, its left end along the notch,
    the current mist, the others dark; a group of one page is one mist bar. A page before its device's first heading is
    in the group PAGE. The header redraws only the sections that changed since it was drawn (`head_draw`, `hd_drawn`):
    the stack for another page of the group (about 1,400 px), the second section for another group, all of it (8,640 px)
    when a boundary moves or for another device. the menu's header is a coral band (MENU and the tab), a Page button's
    list a seafoam one, their text centred in it; a message (`bar_text`) covers the whole band. The sandbox page has the
    device page's header (sandbox_ui.c `sb_head`).
  - The bars (y 40..220, `bar_row`): four equal bars 4 px apart in the area between the header and the footer (4 px from
    each; the menu's ends above its tab chips: `geo_set`). A bar: the panel colour, the
    fill to the value in the knob's hue mixed 40 % with it, its last 2 px in the hue, an ivory tick where modulation has
    the parameter (rows 4..h-4); an accent tab in the hue, 4 px wide, 45 % of the bar high, 2 px from its left; the name
    10 px after it, the value right, 6 px from the bar's end, its unit after it in mist (`FONT_F`). A row the page lacks
    is a bar of the strip colour with a faint "-". Ten times a second a bar whose fill or live tick moved is updated in
    place (`pages_ticks`).
  - The footer (y 225..239, sand, `foot_row`): the patch's name (ARM / REC and the track's name while one is armed, red
    for REC; " *" when edited; while nothing is armed `KEYS WIRED` after it in the tempo's ink, `foot_t.w`, the name cut
    first, [D-236](ledger/device-ui.md#D-236)), the tempo with a quarter note (a bitmap: the mock's oval head and stem; "MIDI" on MIDI
    clock), the octave (= OCT =, OCT +, OCT ++, - OCT, -- OCT, centred in a slot as wide as its widest state, `FOOT_OCT_W`
    40 px, so the tempo stays put) and the battery, spread margin to margin (6 px). It is
    drawn whole (3,600 px) when its text changes (`foot_draw`, a hash of what it shows). The transport and the part count
    are not shown. In sandbox mode it is gold, with SANDBOX in place of the name and the editor's tempo (`sb_foot`).
  - Everything goes through one scanline composer per region (`page_row`, `bar_row`, `head_row`, `foot_row`,
    `toast_row`) into lcd.c's `lcd_line` and out through `line_out`, so a part drawn alone is the same pixels as the page
    drawn whole. `lcd_line` is one of two scanline buffers (#377, [D-242](ledger/device-ui.md#D-242)): `lcd_line_out`
    hands the row to the DMA and makes the other buffer `lcd_line`, so the next row is built while this one goes out
    (before, the one buffer was waited for before every row). A renderer writes only `lcd_line`, reads the pointer afresh
    for every row, and keeps no row of its own; the host stand-in (`firmware/test/host_ui.h`) counts a write into the
    buffer the LCD still reads, and panel_test.c wants none. A whole page (`pages_draw(1)`) is every row of the screen once, nothing cleared first: 57,600 px.
  - A turned knob moves its own bar in place (#298, `pages_row_update`): only the columns its fill's end and live tick
    crossed (the whole height), and its value and unit when they changed (their band), are redrawn (about 1,200 px a
    click), from what the bar showed (`pg_drawn`), with the same values the bar is then noted with. The live tick comes
    from the engine's effective value (`eng_get_eff`), which lags a turn until the next audio block: `eff_now` moves it
    by the turn meanwhile (`lag_note`), so a bar drawn whole and one moved in place agree pixel for pixel.
  - SELECT and ALGO show no toast: the header shows where they went. The page they reached is drawn at once
    (`pages_staged`, over the page that was there: `pg_plain`): the header's sections that changed, the band of each
    bar that holds its type (the rows readable), then the rest of the bars; nothing is cleared first. Between bands it
    gives way when an encoder has a step waiting (`pages_waiting`, main.c `encs_waiting`): turning fast redraws only the
    header.
  - HOME held + PRESET (and a program change, #170) grows the footer into a toast for 1 s (`toast_row`, `toast_first`): 60 px of
    sand, the slot's number ("07.") at the left margin and the patch's name at the right one in 32 px type (26 px when
    the two do not fit together, so a name stays whole), and along its bottom a dash per filled slot of the bank (the
    current one night, the others night at 22 % over sand), whole pixels, all as wide, the row centred. Above 32 filled
    slots the dashes take two balanced rows 2 px apart and the toast is 4 px taller. The toast is drawn first (14,400 px),
    then the page around it (`line_out` leaves the toast's rows alone while it stays); a waiting click stops after the
    toast. When its time is up only its band is drawn again from the page (`toast_restore`, 14,400 px); a knob turned or
    SELECT / ALGO meanwhile ends it.
  - `tools/panel_test.sh` turns every knob of every factory page (both ways, slow and fast, with and without audio blocks
    between the clicks), reaches every page of every factory patch with SELECT and ALGO (some draws stopped by a waiting
    click) and every patch with PRESET, lets each toast end, and checks that the screen is then the screen drawn whole,
    pixel for pixel. Host pixel counts (main before the redesign → after; ms at 750 px/ms, the SPI's 12 MHz): one ALGO
    click on the 808 Kit 44,392 → 39,744 (59 → 53 ms; over every factory page 41,661 on average, at most 44,100); one
    SELECT 51,976 → 46,944; one K1 click 684 → 1,218 (0.9 → 1.6 ms; the bars are taller); one PRESET 52,210 → 46,992;
    the toast's end 4,400 → 14,400 (it is a band now); a whole page 107,810 → 57,600 (144 → 77 ms). Preview:
    `PATCHTOAST=1 [BANK=n SLOT=s] tools/ui_preview.sh`, `BATT=0..4`. There is
  no picture area: the envelope drawing and page map are gone (faint envelopes in the bars are #112). SELECT steps the devices, ALGO the pages, K1..K4 edit relatively (fast turns five times as far, never on a reversal; HOME held, a tenth of a click, not under the row's editor step: #340, [D-244](ledger/device-ui.md#D-244); a click on a row of whole steps is a whole number of them, and such a row shows a whole value without decimals: [D-922](ledger/device-ui.md#D-922); a click reaches the module over 23 ms: #405),
  HOME climbs (`home_tap`, #391, [D-243](ledger/device-ui.md#D-243)): on a later page of a device to its first page, on a
  device's first page to the patch's home page (`pages_home`), on the home page nothing; out of a dive it goes back along
  the trail (below); MIDI CC 16..19 set the rows (absolute). A poly row writes the
  group record of `m1_cur` (`poly_row_set` in main.c; the voices and unison reload the engine).
- Panel core (`firmware/src/panel.c`, #108): what the knobs, keys and buttons do, apart from the HAL. main.c's polls
  read the hardware and hand it in (`panel_held` = the buttons held, `panel_encoders(st[7])`, `panel_keys(bits)`,
  `panel_edges(pressed, released)`); its loop calls `panel_status()` before the inputs and `panel_draw()` after them.
  The core's hooks (LEDs, `preset_step`, `save_state`, `midi_send`, `poly_row_set`, proto.c's `m1_*_now`) are main.c's
  on the device and stubs on a host. `tools/panel_test.sh` (firmware/test/panel_test.c, on ui_preview.c built with
  `UI_PREVIEW_LIB`) drives SELECT / ALGO / K1 / HOME / the menu / the trail / PLAY / tempo / SAVE / a key / MIDI out and
  checks the framebuffer; the firmware CI runs it.
  - Keys during a redraw (#230): a full page is ~108k pixels (panel_test), ~0.15 s of SPI at 12 MHz. The scan (`fm1__key`, in the
    TIMER5 tick) queues every debounced note-key edge in order (`fm1_in.kq`, 32; `fm1_input_key_peek` / `_pop`), and
    `keys_poll` hands each to `panel_key(n, down, quick)` through `panel_key_queue`, then `panel_keys(fm1_in.notes)`
    (a no-op unless the queue overflowed): a press and its release within one pass both play, in the order played.
    `panel_draw` sets `lcd_yield` (lcd.c calls it in `lcd_sync` while a transfer runs, before waiting; the host stand-ins
    before each fill / blit) to `keys_yield`: the queued edges go through `panel_key` with quick = 1, so a note starts
    within one transfer of the redraw, not after all of it. In quick mode nothing may draw: a key edge only changes
    state and sets `ui_dirty` (`panel_draw` clears it before it draws, so a change mid-draw is drawn next pass), a
    `header_draw` waits for the redraw's end (`ui_yielding`, `ui_header_due`), and REC / SEQ held + a black key (an arm
    or a mute draws a message and a row) is left in the queue, with every edge behind it, for the next `keys_poll`.
    Buttons and knobs are still taken once a pass (their edges are latched: none is lost, only later).
  - The battery (#240, [D-229](ledger/device-ui.md#D-229)): `batt_poll` (main.c) reads SARADC ch 3 (PB1's divider; PB1
    stays an analog input) every 2 s from the loop: one conversion dropped after MASTER's, the mean of four, one MASTER
    conversion dropped so `master_poll` reads ch 4 settled. Six polled conversions; their time is `batt_us` (DIAG page
    127 words 5 and 6: the time in us and the smoothed reading; `tools/m1_link.py cpu` prints them). `ui_battery` (ui.c)
    smooths it (a one-pole, a quarter of the way per reading, x16) and keeps the level: 0..3 bars at the stock firmware's
    thresholds 531 / 561 / 591 (taken from Felucca's `ui_draw.c batt_level`; not yet confirmed on our device), 3 counts of
    hysteresis on the way up. USB configured by a host and not suspended (`usb.config && !usb.suspended`) shows a bolt
    instead: no pin tells a charger apart (Felucca's note), so a plain USB charger shows the battery. State only: the
    loop's drawing (`panel_draw` -> `batt_draw`, menu.c) draws the battery at the right end of the device page's footer
    (the menu's settings tabs' and the sandbox page's too: `foot_batt_sync`, pages_ui.c; 11 x 6 and a 2 px nub, night on sand (on gold in sandbox mode), 0..3 bars,
    a bolt on USB power, the outline red when empty) only when the shown level changes, says LOW BATTERY once when the
