    level falls to 0 (again only after a bar or USB came back), and redraws the BATTERY row's value on an idle SYSTEM
    tab. The other pages (menu, SEQ, ENV) show no battery ([D-229](ledger/device-ui.md#D-229)). Host builds
    (host_ui.h `BATT_START` 900: the preview, the tests, EMU) start full; fm1-emu's ch 3 reads 640.
- **Button kinds (#116, designs E / F):** each patch button has one kind per patch (`web/src/buttons.ts`): Nothing
  (unassigned, no role), Gate (on while held or switched; patch its jack) or Toggle (`bypass` in the model: bypasses a
  module or an assembly, the same bypass as the card's ⏻; on = its target bypassed). Every button keeps its jack, high
  while held or engaged. Model: `MainPanel.kinds` (absent = Gate, so patches from before keep their behaviour; a new
  patch's buttons start as Nothing, `NEW_PANEL`, GLO aside until #118) and `MainPanel.targets` ({module} or {asm});
  `toggle` stays the PRESS setting (switches). An assembly target moves the members its own switch moves
  (`toggleTargets`: not those bypassed on their own). Binary format 30: flag bit 6 section (patchbin.ts top), before the
  macro ranges; `ENG_PATCH_COMPAT_VERSION` stays 29 (the panel section is unchanged). Engines: `Engine.setButton` /
  `eng_buttons` bypass a Toggle's modules when its state changes; a switching Toggle's state follows its targets
  (`syncToggles` / `toggles_sync`, also after a bypass from elsewhere); the firmware also writes the bypass bits into the
  running binary (`m1_patch_bypass`) so SAVE keeps them. LEDs: dark = Nothing and uncabled, dim = assigned or cabled
  (`panel_used`), lit = engaged. Editor: dock faces with label, kind mark, FM-1 name, light; the button editor popover
  (`button-editor.ts`, right-click or ⋯ on a dock button: DOES, BYPASSES, PRESS, LABEL; GLO gets PRESS and LABEL only).
  A Toggle press from the dock or the FM-1 (`Editor.pressButton`): a switching one is a patch edit (undo step), a held
  one only the engine and the card's look. Golden kit `button-toggle`.
- **Page buttons (#117):** a fourth kind, Page (`page`, kind byte 3): a list of device pages from any devices
  (`MainPanel.pages`: editor device key + page index, at most `PAGE_LIST_MAX` 16; pages that are gone are left out,
  `pageList`; in the binary (device, page) pairs in the device pages section's indices, `binaryPage`). A press puts the
  next page of the list on K1..K4, round the list; the first press from elsewhere is a trail step (HOME goes back, the
  trail entries carry the list state); held `PAGE_HOLD_MS` 400 ms, the list's first page shows while the button is down.
  While its list is on the button is engaged (lit, jack high) and the device's top bar reads "SCL 2/3 - AMP ENV"
  (`pages_strip`, seafoam). Inside a list ALGO steps the list; HOME leaves it, back one step along the trail to the page before the button's first press (with no step left, it only closes the list), and SELECT leaves it too as it moves the device. The controls sheet lists HOME for it (`leave EDIT's pages`). Firmware: `pages_ui.c`
  `pg_list_btn` / `pg_list_step` / `pg_list_peek` (reset on another patch) and `pages_list`; `panel.c` `page_button`,
  `page_hold_poll`, `page_mask`, `list_focus`, `trail_push`. Editor: the same rules on the dock knobs
  (`Editor.pagePress`, the list is left when the focus moves any other way); the popover's PAGES list (a picker,
  Alt-click a card row while it is open, drag or Alt+Up / Down to reorder, ×). Page buttons have no PRESS.
- See [D-220](ledger/device-ui.md#D-220).
- **ARP with nothing on it (2026-10-19, replaces #119's dock column):** the Arpeggiator stays a module; the dock has no
  Arpeggiator column (its controls are the card's and GLO's K3 / K4). ARP is "free" (`arp-button.ts` `arpFree`) when its
  kind is Nothing and its jack has no cable, or it is a Toggle whose target is gone; the dock then draws it "+ ARP / add
  one" (`Editor.freeArpButton`) and a click puts an arpeggiator on it (ARP becomes its switching Toggle). `arpChoices`
  lists what can be done: put each arpeggiator already in the patch on ARP; a new one for what Main's keys play straight
  (all of it, or each target alone when they feed several), off with the reason once the patch has `DEVICE.arps` (2);
  with an arpeggiator there and keys still played straight, route those through it. One enabled choice: the click does
  it; several: a menu above the button (`Editor.openArpMenu`), each row glowing the keys cables it takes over. Every
  choice is one undo step, selects the arpeggiator and shows a toast with Undo. Targets are named by the assembly the
  allocator sits in (`keysTargetName`). Anything already on ARP: the button acts as its kind says, no menu.
- **Status line (2026-10-19):** a 24 px row under the whole workspace (`index.html` `#statusline`, `statusline.ts`). Left:
  what the pointer is over, from the element's `data-help` / `data-help-tag`, else its title or an SVG `<title>` (a
  cable: "OSC out → Filter in. Click to disconnect it"); "NAME: text" splits into tag and text. While nothing is
  hovered it shows `pinStatus`'s message (a sandbox patch, over the budget: "Plays here, with no FM-1 limits: 2.7× its CPU,
  2.1× its memory." and a "What sandbox means" link, `sandboxWords` in sandbox.ts) or the idle hint. A sandbox patch also
  shows the gold SANDBOX pill at the left (`#sandbox-pill`), which opens the "No limits here" card (`showSandboxCard`; it
  shows by itself once per browser, `matryoshka.sandbox-seen`, the first time a patch is a sandbox patch). The budget's
  over-budget colour is gold (`--sandbox`, the theme's `--gate`), not red. Right: the FM-1
  budget (MEM / CPU side by side; open, its list above the status line). With it out of the dock (and the dock's ARP
  column gone, morph / B side / tempo in one column with CLOCK / RUN on its edge, the button block's columns as wide as
  their buttons through a subgrid, VEL / AFT captions) the dock fits a 1280 px window (1270 px with the sidebar open).
- **Cable hover glow (2026-10-19, design A):** a hovered cable on the canvas gets a copy in `#cable-glow`, a layer above
  the cards that takes no pointer: a blurred halo in the cable's colour, a fine light core and rings on its jacks
  (`Editor.glow`), so its run shows where it passes behind a card. `Editor.glowCables` lights given cables (the ARP menu).
  `drawCables` clears it: a path removed under the pointer gets no `pointerleave` (#150).
- **FM-1 view, a patch that does not fit (2026-10-19):** the view loads a patch over the budget anyway
  (`encodePatch(p, true)`; the emulator has the firmware's tables, not its timing) under a card (`Fm1View.notice`,
  amber "Over the FM-1 budget") that dismisses to a chip above the device ("Over budget · CPU 104 % · won't upload"),
  which brings it back. A patch past the firmware's tables is refused by `ui.load`: a red card, the view keeps the last
  patch it loaded and the chip names it. A dismissed card comes back only when the level changes or the patch fits again.
- Navigation (#114, #391): a button that changes the screen (SEQ, GLO = the menu (#395); page buttons #117) leaves a step on the trail (`panel.c` `page_set`: page + the device page in focus, `TRAIL_MAX` 8, the
  oldest dropped); a HOME tap on such a screen goes back one (`screen_back`, restoring the device page if it moved; the same button again
  goes back too). On a device page a HOME tap (`home_tap`, [D-243](ledger/device-ui.md#D-243)) goes to the device's first page, from there to the patch's home page (`pages_home`), and on the home page does nothing; these moves leave no step, landing on the home page clears the trail, and HOME depends only on what is on screen (a device page reached from a dive follows the same rule). A Page button's list showing on a device page, and sandbox mode, go back along the trail. ALGO moves sideways (device pages, menu tabs, SEQ tracks), SELECT picks (the device, the
  manual's scroll); HOME held + PRESET steps patches (in the menu PRESET alone does too, and HOME's release then stays), PRESET alone steps the focused device's presets (`dev_preset_step`, #395; a module: `NONE. HOME+PRESET: PATCHES`), but on the SEQ page the patch's pattern ([D-541](ledger/steps.md#D-541)). SAVE in the menu saves, then goes back.
- The UI as WebAssembly (#109): `firmware/wasm/fm1_ui.c` = the panel core and screens on the C engine over the host
  stand-ins of `firmware/test/host_ui.h` (shared with ui_preview.c and panel_test.c; no libc, framebuffer `fb` with a
  dirty flag, LEDs as bits). `npm run fm1-wasm` (web/scripts/fm1-wasm.mjs: clang `--target=wasm32 -ffreestanding
  -nostdlib`, then wasm-ld; Homebrew llvm + lld or Ubuntu clang + lld) writes `web/public/fm1-ui.wasm` (git-ignored,
  ~250 KB; the web and Pages workflows build it). Exports `fm1w_*` (load a patch binary + name + slot, tick = silent
  engine blocks for the time gone + the loop's bookkeeping and drawing, encoder / button / keys, focus / param from the
  editor, framebuffer, LEDs, page, focus); imports `fm1.focus / param / preset / save / midi / poly`. TS wrapper
  `web/src/fm1-ui.ts` (`Fm1Ui`, `ENCODERS`, `BUTTONS`); `fm1-ui.test.ts` checks a device page pixel for pixel against
  ui_preview and drives the panel (it rebuilds the wasm when a source is newer).
- The editor's Emulator view (EMU, #110): one of the PATCH | EMU | LIBRARY switch's views over the main area
  (`#view-switch`, main.ts `setView`; PATCH on a page load, not remembered; #218). Firmware installs are the Install
  panel (`#flash-view`, `openInstall` / `closeInstall`), over the main area and not a view: Get started >
  Install (it reads "Install v1.2": the bundled package's version, `__INSTALL_VERSION__`), the palette's Install firmware, `#flash`, and Connect FM-1 when the FM-1 answers M-VAVE's identity handshake
  with other firmware (`identifyFm1`, mirror.ts). Addresses: `#library` / `#assemblies` / `#presets` / `#community` open LIBRARY on
  that section (the last one is remembered per browser, `matryoshka.library-section`), `#emu` and `#fm1` EMU.
- The Library (#218, part 2): it manages what is stored and opens or inserts nothing (opening is the Open picker's, the
  palette's and the assembly browser's). One section shows at a time (`#lib-patches` / `#lib-assemblies` /
  `#lib-presets` / `#lib-community`, main.ts `showLibrarySection`); entering one rereads it and, with an FM-1 connected, reads the FM-1, and
  a connect or a disconnect (`libraryDeviceChanged`) does the same for the one on screen. **Patches** (`bank.ts`
  `buildPatchesSection`): "In this browser" is Your patches (→ into the FM-1 column, Rename, Delete with an Undo toast;
  a sandbox patch, over the budget, carries the gold SANDBOX chip and its → is off; the community patches added to Your
  patches follow, with their authors and Remove), with **Add from
  factory ▾** under it (the factory patches are only a source for the bank). "FM-1" always shows: without a device the
  bank's offline copy (`matryoshka.bank`: ▲ ▼, Rename, ×, ←, All factory patches, Empty the list; Send bank to FM-1
  off); with one what `readBank` finds, slots numbered 01..64 (internal slots 0..63: `slotLabel` in protocol.ts is the one place that adds 1), headed "On the FM-1 · Bank" when `bankMatches` (its used
  slots are 0..n-1 with the offline copy's device names and binary lengths) and "On the FM-1 · Unsaved bank" with **Save
  as bank…** when not. Then → writes a slot (`slotFor`: the slot of that name, asking before it replaces it, else the
  first empty one), × erases one (asks), and Send bank to FM-1 writes the offline copy (asks when `uploadLoses` finds
  slots it would lose). SLOT_INFO gives a slot's name and length, not its data, so ← and Save as bank… use this
  browser's own copy of a slot's patch (`knownCopy`: the offline copy, Your patches, then the factory patches, by device
  name and binary length); a slot with none is left out (Save as bank… says how many, after its confirm). Nothing merges
  on its own. The old over-budget list (`matryoshka.bank-over`) and any offline entry that stopped fitting fold into
  Your patches on the next read. **Presets** (`library-presets.ts`): the user's presets (→, Rename, Delete with Undo)
  and the FM-1's (`listPresets`: ←, Delete on the FM-1 with a confirm); → and Send all to FM-1 ask before replacing one
  of the FM-1's. **Assemblies** (`assemblies.ts` `buildAssembliesSection`): the user's own only (Rename, Export, Delete
  with Undo, Import .json); saving or importing over a name replaces at once with Undo (`storeAssembly` returns the
  undo). The two columns stack under 700 px, the browser above. Several named banks: #220. `web/src/fm1-view.ts` `Fm1View` draws the FM-1's front panel laid out as the device (the geometry of
  fm1-emu's fm1-live, after Felucca's controls diagram, in 1280 x 820 panel units scaled with `cqw`): MASTER / SELECT /
  PRESETS / ALGORITHM and OCT- / OCT+ top left, the LCD (2x, pixelated) in the middle, KNOB1..4 and the two rows of six
  buttons on the right, the 27 pill keys across the bottom; device colours, not the theme's. While it shows, the Main
  dock, the wheels and the deck (keyboard, Audio on) are hidden (`.workspace.fm1-mode`); the first press on the panel
  starts the audio, MASTER sets the output level (`AudioHost.setMaster`, square law, only while the tab shows, kept in
  localStorage `matryoshka.fm1-master`). Encoders: drag 8 px a click, wheel, arrows; buttons: press / hold, Shift-click
  latches one down. main.ts: the emulator's focus / param / step / stepval / mute go through `fm1Event` (the same path
  as a Live FM-1, flag `fromEmu`: not echoed to the emulator, but passed on to a Live FM-1); editor parameter edits go
  to it at once (`emuParam`), any other edit reloads the patch into it (`queueEmu`, 120 ms; it keeps the focus), the
  editor's focus and transport go to it, its PLAY sets the editor's. HOME held + PRESET (Shift-click latches HOME) steps the Open list (Your patches,
  the factory patches), skipping sandbox patches as the FM-1 does, SAVE = Save patch. The pill keys and the computer keyboard play through the note sink at the emulated
  octave; the held notes are the emulator's keys (`emu-keys.ts`), on its SEQ page they enter steps and are not sounded.
  For the panel's own keys (the pill keys, the computer keyboard, the on-screen one) the browser engine plays what
  panel.c gave the wasm's engine (`fm1_ui.c` reports each `eng_note_on` / `eng_note_off` of panel.c to the page), so a
  Drum map's rows land on black keys 1..8 as on the device ([D-643](ledger/editor.md#D-643)); a MIDI note plays its own number.
  The browser engine plays; there is no emulated flash bank. Lights (after X0X's browser emulator): host_ui.h keeps
  the HAL's LED arrays over a host matrix (id = (row - 1) * 11 + column), so seq_ui.c's step cells and the wasm's
  `leds_play` / `leds_octave` / `leds_avail` (as main.c) land there; `fm1w_led(kind, first)` reads them as bits (lit,
  dim, mid, breathing), `Fm1Ui.lights()`, and the panel colours buttons and keys from them (PLAY red stopped, green
  breathing running; SEQ steps lit, the playhead mid, tracks dim on the black keys).
- LCD restyle: the dark brand palette in RGB565 (ui.c `C_*`, pages_ui.c `P_*`) and Barlow Condensed SemiBold as
  4-bit anti-aliased bitmaps at four sizes (`tools/gen_font.py`, stdlib TrueType reader + the splash's scanline fill →
  `firmware/src/font_bc.h`, ~36 KB of flash; `font_bc.c` `text_bc` blends per channel). The device page, legend, menu,
  manual, SEQ page and the top bar use it; the update / crash screens keep the 5x7 font. Host preview:
  `tools/ui_preview.sh OUT.png PRESET DEVICE:PAGE OCTAVE 0` (env `TOAST=k`, `DEVTOAST=1`).
- Emulator: `tools/emu/src/font.rs` also reads font_bc.h and matches its fully covered / empty pixels (the blended edge is
  left out); the smoke test's home marker is the info line's `OCT `. Checked against the host preview only
  (`cargo test -- --ignored`); the smoke run needs a JieLi build (CI).
- Not done: the plain module's labels on the FM-1 are defs.h's short capitals (L1, CVAMT); the device page draws in full
  on focus changes (no partial redraw of the chips); none of it has run on hardware.

### LFO shape

The LFO shape input uses the block average in 0..1 to set its triangle rise time and pulse width. Without a cable it uses 0.5. At the ends its triangle becomes a falling or rising saw. Its appended `pulse` output shares the phase, reset, sync and depth of the main output. Both outputs reverse with `dir`. Sallen 20 exposes this as the modulation generator's continuous WAVEFORM control ([D-436](ledger/modules.md#D-436), Proposed).
